\mbox{\rm diam}(\varphi^{p}(\widetilde{{\mathcal{C}}}))\leq r_{0}e^{-p\hat{\epsilon}}<r(z_{p}).

By (4.16) there exists
\xi\in E^{u}(z_{p})
with
\|\xi\|\leq r_{0}e^{-p\hat{\epsilon}}<r(z_{p})
so that

z=\Phi^{u}_{z_{p}}(\xi)\in\varphi^{p}(\widetilde{{\mathcal{C}}})
and for
y=\varphi^{-p}(z)\in\widetilde{{\mathcal{C}}}
we have
d(y,z_{0})\geq\frac{1}{2}\mbox{\rm diam}(\widetilde{{\mathcal{C}}})
.
Thus for
\eta=(\Phi^{u}_{z_{0}})^{-1}(y)\in E^{u}(z_{0})
we have

\|\eta\|^{\prime}_{z_{0}}\geq\|\eta\|\geq\frac{1}{\Gamma_{0}}d(y,z_{0})\geq\frac{1}{2\Gamma_{0}}\mbox{\rm diam}(\widetilde{{\mathcal{C}}}).

Using (3.12),
R(z_{p})\leq R_{0}e^{p\hat{\epsilon}}
,
\mu_{1}=\lambda_{1}e^{-\hat{\epsilon}}
,
q(1-\hat{\epsilon}_{6})\leq p\leq q
,
r_{0}<1
and the above we obtain

\displaystyle\mbox{\rm diam}(\widetilde{{\mathcal{C}}})

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 2\Gamma_{0}\|\eta\|^{\prime}_{z_{0}}\leq 2\Gamma_{0}\frac{\|\xi\|^{\prime}_{z_{p}}}{\mu_{1}^{p}}\leq 2\Gamma_{0}R_{0}e^{p\hat{\epsilon}}\frac{\|\xi\|}{\mu_{1}^{p}}\leq 2\Gamma_{0}R_{0}\frac{r_{0}e^{2p\hat{\epsilon}}}{\lambda_{1}^{p}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{2\Gamma_{0}R_{0}\lambda_{1}^{q\hat{\epsilon}_{6}}e^{2q\hat{\epsilon}}}{\lambda_{1}^{q}}\leq\frac{2\Gamma_{0}R_{0}e^{q(\hat{\epsilon}_{6}\log\lambda_{1}+2\hat{\epsilon})}}{\lambda_{1}^{q}}\leq\frac{C_{3}e^{q\hat{\epsilon}_{7}}}{\lambda_{1}^{q}},

assuming
\hat{\epsilon}_{7}\geq 2\hat{\epsilon}+\hat{\epsilon}_{6}\log\lambda_{1}
and
C_{3}\geq 2\Gamma_{0}R_{0}
.
This proves the left-hand-side inequality in (
4.5
).

The existence of
u_{0}
with (4.6) follows from (4.20).

4.4
Constructing two families of sub-cylinders

We will now get important consequences of some of the arguments in the proof of Lemma 4.3.

In the next lemma we will use the following notation. Given a cylinder
\widetilde{{\mathcal{C}}}
in
\widetilde{R}
containing a
point
z_{0}\in\widetilde{{\mathcal{C}}}\cap\widetilde{P}_{0}
and an integer
p\geq 0
, consider the locally defined near
z_{0}
map

(F^{(0)})^{-1}\circ\hat{\varphi}_{z_{0}}^{-p}\circ F^{(p)}:\widetilde{{\mathcal{C}}}\longrightarrow\widetilde{{\mathcal{C}}},

where

F^{(p)}:(\Phi^{u}_{z_{p}})^{-1}\circ\mathcal{T}_{z_{p}}\circ\varphi^{p}:\widetilde{{\mathcal{C}}}\longrightarrow E^{u}(z_{p})\hskip 10.00002pt,\hskip 10.00002ptF^{(0)}:(\Phi^{u}_{z_{0}})^{-1}\circ\mathcal{T}_{z_{0}}:\widetilde{{\mathcal{C}}}\longrightarrow E^{u}(z_{0}).

See Figure 1 below.

Lemma 4.4.

Let
C>1
and
d>1
be given constants and let
\frac{1}{\gamma^{\alpha_{1}\beta}}\leq\theta<1
.

There exist global constants
q_{0},q_{1},q_{2}\in{\mathbb{N}}
,
\tilde{m}_{0}\in{\mathbb{N}}
and
\hat{\epsilon}_{12}>0
,

0<\hat{\epsilon}_{12}\leq\mbox{\rm const }\hat{\epsilon}
, which can be made arbitrarily small with
\hat{\epsilon}
, such that
q_{0}\leq q_{1}\leq q_{2}
and
for every cylinder
\widetilde{{\mathcal{C}}}
in
\widetilde{R}
of length
m\geq\tilde{m}_{0}
containing a point
z_{0}\in\widetilde{{\mathcal{C}}}\cap\widetilde{P}_{0}
, setting

p=[\tilde{\tau}_{m-k}(z_{0})]\hskip 10.00002pt\mbox{with}\hskip 10.00002ptk=\frac{4d(m-k)\hat{\epsilon}}{|\log\theta|},

we have the following:

(a)
There exists a
sub-cylinder
\widetilde{\Gamma}=\widetilde{\Gamma}(\widetilde{{\mathcal{C}}})
of
\widetilde{{\mathcal{C}}}
of co-length
q_{0}
in
\widetilde{{\mathcal{C}}}
so that for every
x\in\widetilde{\Gamma}
, for
u=F^{(0)}(x)\in E^{u}(z_{0})
we have

\|u^{(1)}\|\geq\kappa\,\mbox{\rm diam}(\widetilde{{\mathcal{C}}})

(4.21)

with
\kappa=d_{2}e^{-m\hat{\epsilon}_{12}}
.
Moreover, there exists a sub-cylinder
\widetilde{\Lambda}
of
\widetilde{{\mathcal{C}}}
of co-length
q_{1}
in
\widetilde{{\mathcal{C}}}

such that
for every
x\in\widetilde{\Lambda}

we have

\displaystyle C\hat{\varphi}_{z_{0}}^{p}(F^{(0)}(x))\in F^{(p)}(\widetilde{\Gamma})
. That is,

C\,\hat{\varphi}_{z_{0}}^{p}(F^{(0)}(\widetilde{\Lambda}))\subset F^{(p)}(\widetilde{\Gamma}).

(4.22)

(b)
For the sub-cylinders
\widetilde{\Gamma}
and
\widetilde{\Lambda}
of
\widetilde{{\mathcal{C}}}
as in part (a), there exists a sub-cylinder
\widetilde{\Theta}

of
\widetilde{\Gamma}
with co-length
q_{2}
in
\widetilde{{\mathcal{C}}}
such that

F^{(p)}(\widetilde{\Theta})\subset C\,\hat{\varphi}_{z_{0}}^{p}(F^{(0)}(\widetilde{\Lambda})).

(4.23)

Notice that, since the sub-cylinders
\widetilde{\Gamma}
of
\widetilde{{\mathcal{C}}}
that appear in Lemma 4.4
have co-lengths bounded by global constants, there exists a global constant
d_{1}>0
such that

\nu(\widetilde{\Gamma}^{\prime})\geq d_{1}\,\nu(\widetilde{{\mathcal{C}}}^{\prime}),

(4.24)

where
\widetilde{\Gamma}^{\prime}=\pi^{(U)}(\widetilde{\Gamma})
,
\widetilde{{\mathcal{C}}}^{\prime}=\pi^{(U)}(\widetilde{{\mathcal{C}}})
. A similar estimate applies
to
\nu(\widetilde{\Theta})
and
\nu(\widetilde{\Lambda})
.

Proof.

We will use the notation and the assumptions from the beginning of the proof of Lemma 4.3,
with some small changes. Set
Z_{m}=\widetilde{{\mathcal{P}}}^{m}(z_{0})
, and more generally,
Z_{j}=\widetilde{{\mathcal{P}}}^{j}(z_{0})
,
1\leq j\leq m
.
As before, let
r_{0}>0
be a small constant and let
y_{0}
be a point
with
B^{u}(y_{0},4r_{0})\subset W^{u}_{\widetilde{R}_{j_{0}}}(Z_{m})
and now
we assume
d(y_{0},Z_{m})>4r_{0}
. Let
y\in\widetilde{{\mathcal{C}}}

be the point with
\widetilde{{\mathcal{P}}}^{m}(y)=y_{0}
.

Again we will assume that the integer
k
satisfies (4.8), however now we will impose a stronger condition on
k
.
Namely we want something similar to (4.14):

|\log\theta|\,k=4d(m-k)\hat{\epsilon}.

(4.25)

That is
(4d\hat{\epsilon}+|\log\theta|)k=4dm\hat{\epsilon}
, which implies

m\hat{\epsilon}_{8}=k\leq m\hat{\epsilon}_{9},

(4.26)

where
\displaystyle\hat{\epsilon}_{9}=\frac{4d}{|\log\theta|}\,\hat{\epsilon}\leq\mbox{\rm const }\hat{\epsilon}
and

\displaystyle\hat{\epsilon}_{8}=\frac{4d}{4d\hat{\epsilon}+|\log\theta|}\,\hat{\epsilon}\leq\mbox{\rm const }\hat{\epsilon}
.

Notice that, since
1/\gamma^{\alpha_{1}\beta}\leq\theta<1
,
we have
\alpha_{1}\beta\log\gamma\geq|\log\theta|
, so (
4.25
) implies

(\alpha_{1}\beta\log\gamma)k\geq 4d(m-k)\hat{\epsilon}.

As in the proof of Lemma 4.3, set
p=[\tilde{\tau}_{m-k}(z_{0})]
. Notice that with our new definition of
k

and the related
p
, (4.10), (4.11) and (4.12) in the proof of Lemma 4.3 still hold, and therefore
(4.13) hold as well. Moreover,

\mbox{\rm diam}(\varphi^{p}(\widetilde{{\mathcal{C}}}))\leq\frac{3C}{\gamma^{\alpha_{1}k}}\leq\frac{3C}{\gamma^{\alpha_{1}\beta k}}.

Also, as in the proof of Lemma 4.3 we show that

q(1-\hat{\epsilon}^{\prime}_{6})\leq p\leq q(1-\hat{\epsilon}^{\prime}_{5})\leq q

(4.27)

for some constants
0<\hat{\epsilon}^{\prime}_{5}\leq\mbox{\rm const }\hat{\epsilon}
and
0<\hat{\epsilon}^{\prime}_{6}\leq\mbox{\rm const }\hat{\epsilon}
, which is the analogue
of (4.19) for our new definition of
p
.

Next, set
t=\tilde{\tau}_{m-k}(z_{0})-p\in[0,1)
,
Z_{m-k}=\widetilde{{\mathcal{P}}}^{-k}(Z_{m})=\widetilde{{\mathcal{P}}}^{m-k}(z_{0})
,

z_{p}=\varphi^{p}(z_{0})=\phi_{-t}(Z_{m-k})
,
y^{\prime}=\widetilde{{\mathcal{P}}}^{-k}(y_{0})
,
y^{\prime\prime}=\phi_{-t}(y^{\prime})
.
Consider the map

F=\widetilde{{\mathcal{P}}}^{k}\circ\phi_{t}:\varphi^{p}(\widetilde{{\mathcal{C}}})\longrightarrow W^{u}_{\widetilde{R}_{j_{0}}}(Z_{m}).

As before, by (2.1), we have

\displaystyle B^{u}(y^{\prime},\frac{4r_{0}d_{0}}{\gamma_{1}^{k}})\subset\widetilde{{\mathcal{P}}}^{-k}(B^{u}(y_{0},4r_{0}))
and

\displaystyle B^{u}(y^{\prime\prime},\frac{4r_{0}d_{0}}{\gamma_{1}^{k+1}})\subset F^{-1}(B^{u}(y_{0},4r_{0}))
.
Set

\hat{y}=\mathcal{T}_{z_{p}}(y^{\prime\prime})\in W^{u}_{\epsilon}(z_{p})
and
\eta=(\Phi^{u}_{z_{p}})^{-1}(\hat{y})\in E^{u}(z_{p})
.
Since
d(y_{0},Z_{m})>4r_{0}
, we have
d(y^{\prime\prime},z_{p})\geq\frac{4r_{0}d_{0}}{\gamma_{1}^{k+1}}
, and it follows from (4.1) that

d(\hat{y},z_{p})\geq\frac{4r_{0}d_{0}}{\tilde{c}_{2}\gamma_{1}^{k+1}}
and therefore

\displaystyle\|\eta\|\geq\frac{d(\hat{y},z_{p})}{R(z_{p})}\geq\frac{4r_{0}d_{0}}{\tilde{c}_{2}\gamma_{1}^{k+1}R(z_{p})}.

In what follows
it is more convenient to deal with
\widetilde{{\mathcal{P}}}^{m-k}(\widetilde{{\mathcal{C}}})
instead of
\varphi^{p}(\widetilde{{\mathcal{C}}})
, and the space
E^{u}(Z_{m-k})
, rather then
E^{u}(z_{p})
.
As before, for
z_{p}=\varphi^{p}(z_{0})
we have
r(z_{p})\geq r_{0}e^{-p\hat{\epsilon}}
.
It follows from (4.12), which holds again with the present choice of
k
as remarked earlier, that we have

\mbox{\rm diam}(\varphi^{p}(\widetilde{{\mathcal{C}}}))\leq\frac{3C}{\gamma^{\alpha_{1}k}}\leq\frac{3C}{\gamma^{\alpha_{1}\beta k}}=3Ce^{-k\,\alpha_{1}\beta\log\gamma},

therefore

\mbox{\rm diam}(\widetilde{{\mathcal{P}}}^{m-k}(\widetilde{{\mathcal{C}}}))\leq\frac{3C\gamma_{1}}{d_{0}}\,e^{-k\,\alpha_{1}\beta\log\gamma},

which, combined with
(\alpha_{1}\beta\log\gamma)k\geq 4d(m-k)\hat{\epsilon}
, implies

\displaystyle\mbox{\rm diam}((\Phi^{u}_{Z_{m-k}})^{-1}(\mathcal{T}_{Z_{m-k}}(\widetilde{{\mathcal{P}}}^{m-k}(\widetilde{{\mathcal{C}}})))

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{3C\gamma_{1}\tilde{c}_{2}R(Z_{m-k})}{d_{0}}\,e^{-k\,\alpha_{1}\beta\log\gamma}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{3C\gamma_{1}\tilde{c}_{2}R_{0}}{d_{0}}\,e^{(m-k)\hat{\epsilon}}\,e^{-4d(m-k)\hat{\epsilon}}\leq e^{-3d(m-k)\hat{\epsilon}},

since
d>1
, so

\displaystyle\frac{3C\gamma_{1}\tilde{c}_{2}R_{0}}{d_{0}}\,e^{(m-k)\hat{\epsilon}}\leq e^{d(m-k)\hat{\epsilon}}
, assuming
k
is sufficiently large.
Also, we have
e^{-d(m-k)\hat{\epsilon}}\leq r_{0}e^{-(m-k)\hat{\epsilon}}\leq r(Z_{m-k})
, again for large
k
.
The above gives

(\Phi^{u}_{Z_{m-k}})^{-1}(\mathcal{T}_{Z_{m-k}}(\widetilde{{\mathcal{P}}}^{m-k}(\widetilde{{\mathcal{C}}}))\subset E=\left\{u\in E^{u}(Z_{m-k}):\|u\|\leq e^{-3d(m-k)\hat{\epsilon}}\right\}.

(4.28)

Let
e_{1},e_{2},\ldots,e_{n_{u}}
be an orthonormal basis in
E^{u}(Z_{m-k})
, so that
\{e_{1},\ldots,e_{n_{1}}\}
is a
basis in
E^{u}_{1}(Z_{m-k})
.
Consider the subsets

H=\{u=(u_{1},\ldots,u_{n_{u}})\in E^{u}(Z_{m-k}):\|u\|<e^{-3d(m-k)\hat{\epsilon}}\>,\>u_{1}=0\}

and

H_{1}=\left\{u=(u_{1},\ldots,u_{n_{u}})\in E^{u}(Z_{m-k}):\|u\|<e^{-3d(m-k)\hat{\epsilon}}\>,\>\|u_{1}\|<\frac{1}{4}e^{-4d(m-k)\hat{\epsilon}}\right\}

of
E
. Clearly
H\subset H_{1}
. We will also need
\widehat{H}_{1}=(\hat{\phi}_{t})^{-1}(H_{1})\subset E^{u}(z_{p}).

[formula]
[formula]

Figure 1

Set

\epsilon=\frac{e^{-4d(m-k)\hat{\epsilon}}}{4}.

(4.29)

Consider a rectangular box
\Delta
contained entirely in
E\setminus H_{1}
with sides of length
\epsilon
parallel to the coordinate axes.
To construct
\Delta
,
fix a point
w\in H
with

\|w_{1}\|=\frac{1}{2}e^{-4d(m-k)\hat{\epsilon}},

and then
consider the cube
\Delta
in
E
with centre
w
and sides of length
\epsilon
.

Then, applying the map
(\hat{\phi}_{t})^{-1}
to the cube
\Delta
, we get that its
image is contained in
E(z_{p})\setminus H_{2}
, where

H_{2}=\left\{v=(v_{1},\ldots,v_{n_{u}})\in E^{u}(z_{p}):\|v\|<e^{-3d(m-k)\hat{\epsilon}}\>,\>\|v_{1}\|<\frac{d_{0}\tilde{c}_{1}}{4\gamma_{1}}e^{-4d(m-k)\hat{\epsilon}}\right\}.

Consider the largest cylinder
\widetilde{X}
in
\widetilde{{\mathcal{C}}}_{1}=\widetilde{{\mathcal{P}}}^{m-k}(\widetilde{{\mathcal{C}}})

such that
\Delta\supset(\Phi^{u}_{Z_{m-k}})^{-1}\circ\mathcal{T}_{Z_{m-k}}(\widetilde{X})
.
Denote by
n
the length of the cylinder
\widetilde{X}
.
Then
\widetilde{X}\subset\mathcal{T}^{-1}_{Z_{m-k}}(\Phi^{u}_{Z_{m-k}}(\Delta))
, however
the cylinder
\widetilde{Y}
of length
n-1
containing
\widetilde{X}
is not contained in

\mathcal{T}^{-1}_{Z_{m-k}}(\Phi^{u}_{Z_{m-k}}(\Delta)))
, and using (3.9) we get

\mbox{\rm diam}(\widetilde{Y})\geq\mbox{\rm diam}(\mathcal{T}^{-1}_{Z_{m-k}}(\Phi^{u}_{Z_{m-k}}(\Delta)))\geq\frac{\tilde{c}_{1}\,\epsilon}{R_{0}}.

By Lemma 4.1(c),

\mbox{\rm diam}(\widetilde{Y})\leq C_{1}\mbox{\rm diam}_{\theta}(\widetilde{Y})=C_{1}\theta^{n-1}.

Hence
C_{1}\theta^{n-1}\geq\tilde{c}_{1}\,\epsilon/R_{0}
. So, we have

\theta^{n}\geq\mbox{\rm const }\epsilon
for some global constant
\mbox{\rm const }>0
. This gives

n\log\theta\geq\mbox{\rm const }+\log\epsilon
, hence
n|\log\theta|\leq c^{\prime}-\log\epsilon
, and therefore

n\leq\frac{c^{\prime}-\log\epsilon}{|\log\theta|}=c^{\prime\prime}-\frac{\log\epsilon}{|\log\theta|}

for some global constants
c^{\prime},c^{\prime\prime}\in{\mathbb{R}}
.

Consider an arbitrary point
x^{\prime}\in\widetilde{X}^{\prime}=\pi^{(U)}(\widetilde{X})
. Since the length of
\widetilde{{\mathcal{C}}}_{1}=\widetilde{{\mathcal{P}}}^{m-k}(\widetilde{{\mathcal{C}}})
is
k
,
it follows from (4.2) and (4.3) that

\frac{\nu(\widetilde{X}^{\prime})}{\nu(\widetilde{{\mathcal{C}}}_{1}^{\prime})}\geq\frac{c_{1}e^{g_{n}(x^{\prime})}}{c_{2}e^{g_{k}(x^{\prime})}}=\frac{c_{1}}{c_{2}}e^{g_{n-k}(\sigma^{k}(x^{\prime}))}\geq\frac{c_{1}^{2}}{c_{2}}\rho_{1}^{n-k}=\frac{c_{1}^{2}}{c_{2}}e^{(n-k)\log\rho_{1}}=\frac{c_{1}^{2}}{c_{2}}\left(e^{-n}e^{k}\right)^{|\log\rho_{1}|}.

From the estimate above,

\displaystyle e^{-n}\geq e^{-c^{\prime\prime}}\,e^{\frac{\log\epsilon}{|\log\theta|}}=c^{\prime\prime\prime}\,\epsilon^{1/|\log\theta|}

for some global constant
c^{\prime\prime\prime}>0
.
On the other hand, it follows from (
4.25
) that

\displaystyle e^{k}=e^{4d(m-k)\hat{\epsilon}/|\log\theta|}
.
Therefore, using (
4.29
),

\frac{\nu(\widetilde{X}^{\prime})}{\nu(\widetilde{{\mathcal{C}}}_{1}^{\prime})}\geq\frac{c_{1}^{2}}{c_{2}}\left(c^{\prime\prime\prime}\,\epsilon\,e^{4d(m-k)\hat{\epsilon}}\right)^{\frac{|\log\rho_{1}|}{|\log\theta|}}=\frac{c_{1}^{2}}{c_{2}}\left(c^{\prime\prime\prime}/4\right)^{\frac{|\log\rho_{1}|}{|\log\theta|}}=d^{\prime}_{1}.

Thus, there exists a global constant
d^{\prime}_{1}>0
so that
\nu(\widetilde{X}^{\prime})\geq d^{\prime}_{1}\,\nu(\widetilde{{\mathcal{C}}}_{1}^{\prime})
.

In this way we have constructed a cylinder
\widetilde{X}
in
\widetilde{{\mathcal{C}}}_{1}
so that

(\Phi^{u}_{Z_{m-k}})^{-1}\circ\mathcal{T}_{Z_{m-k}}(\widetilde{X})\subset E\setminus H_{1}
,
which implies

(\hat{\phi}_{t})^{-1}\circ(\Phi^{u}_{Z_{m-k}})^{-1}\circ\mathcal{T}_{Z_{m-k}}(\widetilde{X})\subset E^{u}(z_{p})\setminus H_{2},

(4.30)

and
\nu(\widetilde{X}^{\prime})\geq d^{\prime}_{1}\,\nu(\widetilde{{\mathcal{C}}}_{1}^{\prime})
.

Let
\widetilde{\Gamma}
be the sub-cylinder of
\widetilde{{\mathcal{C}}}
such that
\widetilde{{\mathcal{P}}}^{m-k}(\widetilde{\Gamma})=\widetilde{X}
.
Set
\widetilde{\Gamma}^{\prime}=\pi^{(U)}(\widetilde{\Gamma})\subset U
.

To estimate
\nu(\widetilde{\Gamma}^{\prime})/\nu(\widetilde{{\mathcal{C}}}^{\prime})
we will use (4.2). Consider an arbitrary
z^{\prime}\in\widetilde{\Gamma}^{\prime}
.
Since
n
is the length of the cylinder
X
, the length of
\Gamma
is
n+m-k
. It follows from (4.2) that

\frac{\nu(\widetilde{\Gamma}^{\prime})}{\nu(\widetilde{{\mathcal{C}}}^{\prime})}\geq\frac{c_{1}e^{g_{n+m-k}(z^{\prime})}}{c_{2}e^{g_{m}(z^{\prime})}}=\frac{c_{1}}{c_{2}}e^{g_{n-k}(\sigma^{m}(z^{\prime}))}.

Then exactly as in the estimate for
\displaystyle\frac{\nu(\widetilde{X})}{\nu(\widetilde{{\mathcal{C}}}_{1})}
we obtain

\nu(\widetilde{\Gamma}^{\prime})\geq d^{\prime}_{1}\,\nu(\widetilde{{\mathcal{C}}}^{\prime}).

(4.31)

It follows from (4.30) and
\widetilde{{\mathcal{P}}}^{m-k}(\widetilde{\Gamma})=\widetilde{X}
, that

(\Phi^{u}_{z_{p}})^{-1}(\mathcal{T}_{z_{p}}(\varphi^{p}(\widetilde{\Gamma})))=(\hat{\phi}_{t})^{-1}\circ(\Phi^{u}_{Z_{m-k}})^{-1}\circ\mathcal{T}_{Z_{m-k}}(\widetilde{{\mathcal{P}}}^{m-k}(\widetilde{\Gamma})),

hence

(\Phi^{u}_{z_{p}})^{-1}(\mathcal{T}_{z_{p}}(\varphi^{p}(\widetilde{\Gamma})))\subset\widehat{H}_{1}\setminus H_{2}.

(4.32)

Proof of
(4.21):
Consider the map

\hat{\phi}_{t}=(\Phi^{u}_{Z_{m-k}})^{-1}\circ\phi_{t}\circ\Phi^{u}_{z_{p}}:E^{u}(z_{p},r(z_{p}))\longrightarrow E^{u}(Z_{m-k},r(Z_{m-k})).

Given
v\in\hat{\varphi}_{z_{0}}^{m-k}(\widetilde{\Gamma})
, it follows from (
4.32
) and the definition of
H_{2}
that

\|v^{(1)}\|\geq\|v_{1}\|\geq\frac{d_{0}\tilde{c}_{1}}{4\gamma_{1}}e^{-4d(m-k)\hat{\epsilon}}.

Set
\xi=\hat{\phi}_{t}^{-1}\cdot v\in E^{u}(z_{p})
. Then

\|\xi^{(1)}\|\geq\frac{d_{0}\tilde{c}_{1}}{4\gamma_{1}}e^{-4d(m-k)\hat{\epsilon}}
.
Consequently for
u=\hat{\varphi}_{z_{p}}^{-p}(\xi)
, using

\nu_{1}^{p}=\lambda_{1}^{p}e^{p\hat{\epsilon}}
and
(m-k)\tilde{\tau}_{0}\leq p\leq q

as in the proof of Lemma 4.3, it follows from (3.12) that

\|u^{(1)}\|^{\prime}_{z_{0}}\geq\frac{\|\xi^{(1)}\|^{\prime}_{z_{p}}}{\nu_{1}^{p}}\geq\frac{\|\xi^{(1)}\|}{\lambda_{1}^{p}e^{2p\hat{\epsilon}}R_{0}}\geq\frac{d_{0}\tilde{c}_{1}e^{-\frac{4dq\hat{\epsilon}}{\tilde{\tau}_{0}}}}{4\gamma_{1}R_{0}e^{2q\hat{\epsilon}}\lambda_{1}^{q}}\geq\frac{d_{0}\tilde{c}_{1}e^{-q\hat{\epsilon}_{11}}}{4\gamma_{1}R_{0}\lambda_{1}^{q}},

where
\hat{\epsilon}_{11}=\frac{4d\hat{\epsilon}}{\tilde{\tau}_{0}}+2\hat{\epsilon}\leq\mbox{\rm const }\,\hat{\epsilon}
.

On the other hand, (
4.5
) gives

\mbox{\rm diam}(\widetilde{{\mathcal{C}}})\leq\frac{C_{3}\,e^{q\hat{\epsilon}_{7}}}{\lambda_{1}^{q}}\leq\frac{e^{-q\hat{\epsilon}_{11}}}{e^{-q\hat{\epsilon}_{11}}}\,\frac{C_{3}e^{q\hat{\epsilon}_{7}}}{\lambda_{1}^{q}}\leq\|u^{(1)}_{1}\|^{\prime}_{z_{0}}\,\frac{4\gamma_{1}R_{0}C_{3}}{d_{0}\tilde{c}_{1}}\,e^{q(\hat{\epsilon}_{7}+\hat{\epsilon}_{11})}.

Thus, for every
x\in\widetilde{\Gamma}^{\prime}
, for
u=(\Phi^{u}_{z_{0}})^{-1}(\mathcal{T}_{z_{0}}(x))\in E^{u}(z_{0})

we have
\|u^{(1)}\|\geq\frac{\|u^{(1)}\|^{\prime}_{z_{0}}}{R_{0}}\geq\kappa\,\mbox{\rm diam}(\widetilde{{\mathcal{C}}})
,
where
\kappa=d_{2}e^{-q\hat{\epsilon}_{12}}
for some
0<\hat{\epsilon}_{12}\leq\mbox{\rm const }\,\hat{\epsilon}
,
where
d_{2}=\frac{d_{0}\tilde{c}_{1}}{4\gamma_{1}R_{0}^{2}C_{3}}
.
This proves (4.21).

We will now repeat the above for a different subset of
\widetilde{{\mathcal{C}}}
.
Choose a sufficiently large integer
\tilde{q}\geq 1
(a global constant determined by
C
)
so that the cylinder
\widetilde{{\mathcal{D}}}_{2}
in
W^{u}_{\widetilde{R}_{j_{0}}}(Z_{m})
of length

\tilde{q}
containing the point
Z_{m}
is so that
\widetilde{{\mathcal{D}}}_{2}\subset B^{u}(Z_{m},r_{0}/C)

for the given constant
C>1
. Let
\widetilde{{\mathcal{D}}}
be the sub-cylinder
of
\widetilde{{\mathcal{C}}}
of co-length
\tilde{q}
so that
\widetilde{{\mathcal{P}}}^{m}(\widetilde{{\mathcal{D}}})=\widetilde{{\mathcal{D}}}_{2}
. Then for the map
F
defined by

F=\widetilde{{\mathcal{P}}}^{k}\circ\phi_{t}:W^{u}_{\epsilon}(z_{p})\longrightarrow W^{u}_{\widetilde{R}_{j_{0}}}(Z_{m})
we have

\varphi^{p}(\widetilde{{\mathcal{D}}})=F^{-1}(\widetilde{{\mathcal{D}}}_{2})\subset F^{-1}(B^{u}(Z_{m},r_{0}/C)).

Set

\widetilde{H}=\frac{1}{C}H=\left\{u=(u_{1},\ldots,u_{n_{u}})\in E^{u}(Z_{m-k}):\|u\|<\frac{1}{C}e^{-3d(m-k)\hat{\epsilon}}\>,\>u_{1}=0\right\},

\widetilde{H}_{1}=\frac{1}{C}H_{1}=\left\{u=(u_{1},\ldots,u_{n_{u}})\in E^{u}(Z_{m-k}):\|u\|<\frac{1}{C}e^{-3d(m-k)\hat{\epsilon}}\>,\>\|u_{1}\|<\frac{1}{4C}e^{-4d(m-k)\hat{\epsilon}}\right\},

\widetilde{H}_{2}=\frac{1}{C}H_{2}=\left\{v=(v_{1},\ldots,v_{n_{u}})\in E^{u}(z_{p}):\|v\|<\frac{1}{C}e^{-3d(m-k)\hat{\epsilon}}\>,\>\|v_{1}\|<\frac{d_{0}\tilde{c}_{1}}{4\gamma_{1}C}e^{-4d(m-k)\hat{\epsilon}}\right\},

H_{1}^{\prime}=(\hat{\phi}_{t})^{-1}(\widetilde{H}_{1})=(\hat{\phi}_{t})^{-1}(\frac{1}{C}H_{1})=\frac{1}{C}\widehat{H}_{1}\subset E^{u}(z_{p}),

\epsilon^{\prime}=\frac{e^{-4d(m-k)\hat{\epsilon}}}{4C}=\frac{\epsilon}{C}.

Recall the fixed point
w\in H
.

Consider now the point
\displaystyle\tilde{w}=\frac{w}{C}\in\widetilde{H}
.
Then

\|\tilde{w}_{1}\|=\frac{1}{2C}e^{-4d(m-k)\hat{\epsilon}}.

Let
\Delta^{\prime}
be the cube in
E
with centre
\tilde{w}
and sides of length
\epsilon^{\prime}
so that

(\hat{\phi}_{t})^{-1}(\Delta^{\prime})\subset E(z_{p})\setminus\widetilde{H}_{2}
.
Consider the largest cylinder
\widetilde{Y}
in
\widetilde{{\mathcal{D}}}_{1}=\widetilde{{\mathcal{P}}}^{m-k}(\widetilde{{\mathcal{D}}})

such that
\Delta^{\prime}\supset(\Phi^{u}_{Z_{m-k}})^{-1}\circ\mathcal{T}_{Z_{m-k}}(\widetilde{Y})
.
If
\tilde{n}
is the
length of the cylinder

\widetilde{Y}
, as in the previous case we get

\tilde{n}\leq\frac{c^{\prime}-\log\epsilon^{\prime}}{|\log\theta|}=c^{\prime\prime}-\frac{\log\epsilon^{\prime}}{|\log\theta|}

for some global constants
c^{\prime},c^{\prime\prime}\in{\mathbb{R}}
.

Since
\widetilde{{\mathcal{D}}}_{1}=\widetilde{{\mathcal{P}}}^{m-k}(\widetilde{{\mathcal{D}}})
is a sub-cylinder of
\widetilde{{\mathcal{C}}}_{1}
of length
k+\tilde{q}
and
\widetilde{Y}
is
a sub-cylinder of
\widetilde{{\mathcal{C}}}_{1}
of length
\tilde{n}
, for
\widetilde{Y}^{\prime}=\pi^{(U)}(\widetilde{Y})
and
\widetilde{{\mathcal{D}}}_{1}^{\prime}=\pi^{(U)}(\widetilde{{\mathcal{D}}}_{1})
,
given an arbitrary point
z^{\prime}\in\widetilde{Y}^{\prime}
, it follows from (4.2) that

\frac{\nu(\widetilde{Y}^{\prime})}{\nu(\widetilde{{\mathcal{D}}}^{\prime}_{1})}\geq\frac{c_{1}e^{g_{\tilde{n}}(z^{\prime})}}{c_{2}e^{g_{k+\tilde{q}}(z^{\prime})}}=\frac{c_{1}}{c_{2}}e^{g_{\tilde{n}-k-\tilde{q}}(\widetilde{{\mathcal{P}}}^{k+\tilde{q}}(z^{\prime}))}\geq\frac{c_{1}^{2}}{c_{2}}\rho_{1}^{\tilde{n}-(k+\tilde{q})}=\frac{c_{1}^{2}}{c_{2}}\left(e^{-\tilde{n}}e^{k+\tilde{q}}\right)^{|\log\rho_{1}|}.

As before
\displaystyle e^{k}=e^{4d(m-k)\hat{\epsilon}/|\log\theta|}
, and

\displaystyle e^{-\tilde{n}}\geq e^{-c^{\prime\prime}}\,e^{\frac{\log\epsilon^{\prime}}{|\log\theta|}}=c^{\prime\prime\prime}\,(\epsilon^{\prime})^{1/|\log\theta|}

for some global constant
c^{\prime\prime\prime}>0
.
Since
\tilde{q}
is a global constant, it follows that

\frac{\nu(\widetilde{Y}^{\prime})}{\nu(\widetilde{{\mathcal{D}}}^{\prime}_{1})}\geq\frac{c_{1}^{2}}{c_{2}}\left(\tilde{c}^{\prime}\,\epsilon^{\prime}\,e^{4d(m-k)\hat{\epsilon}}\right)^{\frac{|\log\rho_{1}|}{|\log\theta|}}=\frac{c_{1}^{2}}{c_{2}}\left(\frac{\tilde{c}^{\prime}}{4C}\right)^{\frac{|\log\rho_{1}|}{|\log\theta|}}=d^{\prime\prime}_{1}

for some constants
\tilde{c}^{\prime}>0
and
d^{\prime\prime}_{1}=d^{\prime\prime}_{1}(C)>0
.

For the cylinder
\widetilde{Y}
in
\widetilde{{\mathcal{D}}}_{1}
just constructed we have

(\Phi^{u}_{Z_{m-k}})^{-1}\circ\mathcal{T}_{Z_{m-k}}(\widetilde{Y})\subset E\setminus\widetilde{H}_{1}
,
so

(\hat{\phi}_{t})^{-1}\circ(\Phi^{u}_{Z_{m-k}})^{-1}\circ\mathcal{T}_{Z_{m-k}}(\widetilde{Y})\subset E(z_{p})\setminus\widetilde{H}_{2},

(4.33)

and
\nu(\widetilde{Y}^{\prime})\geq d^{\prime\prime}_{1}\,\nu(\widetilde{{\mathcal{D}}}^{\prime}_{1})
.

Let
\widetilde{\Lambda}
be the sub-cylinder of
\widetilde{{\mathcal{D}}}
such that
\widetilde{{\mathcal{P}}}^{m-k}(\widetilde{\Lambda})=\widetilde{Y}
; then the length of
\widetilde{\Lambda}
is
\tilde{n}+m-k
.
Using (4.2) again, we get

\frac{\nu(\widetilde{\Lambda}^{\prime})}{\nu(\widetilde{{\mathcal{D}}}^{\prime})}\geq\frac{c_{1}e^{g_{\tilde{n}+m-k}(z)}}{c_{2}e^{g_{m+\tilde{q}}(z)}}=\frac{c_{1}}{c_{2}}e^{g_{\tilde{n}-(k+q_{1})}(\widetilde{{\mathcal{P}}}^{m+q_{1}}(z))}\geq\frac{c_{1}}{c_{2}}\rho_{1}^{\tilde{n}-(k+q_{1})}\geq d^{\prime\prime}_{1},

repeating the argument from the estimate of
\displaystyle\frac{\nu(\widetilde{Y}^{\prime})}{\nu(\widetilde{{\mathcal{D}}}^{\prime}_{1})}
. Thus,

\nu(\widetilde{\Lambda}^{\prime})\geq d^{\prime\prime}_{1}\,\nu(\widetilde{{\mathcal{D}}}^{\prime}).

(4.34)

It follows from (4.33) and
\widetilde{{\mathcal{P}}}^{m-k}(\widetilde{\Lambda})=\widetilde{Y}
that

(\Phi^{u}_{z_{p}})^{-1}(\mathcal{T}_{z_{p}}(\varphi^{p}(\widetilde{\Lambda})))=(\hat{\phi}_{t})^{-1}\circ(\Phi^{u}_{Z_{m-k}})^{-1}\circ\mathcal{T}_{Z_{m-k}}(\widetilde{{\mathcal{P}}}^{m-k}(\widetilde{{\mathcal{D}}}))\subset\widehat{G}\setminus\widetilde{H}_{2},

where

\widehat{G}=(\Phi^{u}_{z_{p}})^{-1}(\mathcal{T}_{z_{p}}(\varphi^{p}(\widetilde{{\mathcal{D}}})))\subset H^{\prime}_{1}=(\hat{\phi}_{t})^{-1}(\widetilde{H}_{1})=\frac{1}{C}\,\widehat{H}_{1}.

Therefore

(\Phi^{u}_{z_{p}})^{-1}(\mathcal{T}_{z_{p}}(\varphi^{p}(\widetilde{{\mathcal{D}}})))\subset H^{\prime}_{1}\setminus\widetilde{H}_{2},

(4.35)

Now (4.34) implies

\frac{\nu(\widetilde{\Lambda}^{\prime})}{\nu({\mathcal{D}}^{\prime})}\geq d^{\prime\prime}_{1}

with the same constant
d^{\prime\prime}_{1}=d^{\prime\prime}_{1}(C)>0
. Since
\widetilde{{\mathcal{D}}}
has co-length
\tilde{q}
in
\widetilde{{\mathcal{C}}}
,
it follows that
\frac{\nu(\widetilde{\Lambda}^{\prime})}{\nu({\mathcal{C}}^{\prime})}\geq d^{\prime\prime\prime}_{1}
for some global constant
d^{\prime\prime\prime}_{1}>0
.
Thus, there exists a global constant
q_{1}\geq 1
so that the co-length of
\widetilde{\Lambda}
in
\widetilde{{\mathcal{C}}}

is
\leq q_{1}
.

It follows from (4.34) that if
z\in\widetilde{\Lambda}
, then for
w=(\Phi^{u}_{z_{0}})^{-1}(\mathcal{T}_{z_{0}}(z))\in E^{u}(z_{0})
we have

\xi=\hat{\varphi}_{z_{0}}^{p}(w)\in H^{\prime}_{1}\setminus\widetilde{H}_{2}
, so
\|\xi_{1}\|\geq\frac{d_{0}\tilde{c}_{1}}{4\gamma_{1}C}e^{-4d(m-k)\hat{\epsilon}}
.
This shows that
C\xi\in H^{\prime}_{1}\setminus H_{2}
.

So, for the maps

F^{(p)}:(\Phi^{u}_{z_{p}})^{-1}\circ\mathcal{T}_{z_{p}}\circ\varphi^{p}:\widetilde{{\mathcal{C}}}\longrightarrow E^{u}(z_{p})\hskip 10.00002pt,\hskip 10.00002ptF^{(0)}:(\Phi^{u}_{z_{0}})^{-1}\circ\mathcal{T}_{z_{0}}:\widetilde{{\mathcal{C}}}\longrightarrow E^{u}(z_{0})

we have

F^{(p)}(x)=C\,\hat{\varphi}_{z_{0}}^{p}(F^{(0)}(x))

for all
x\in\widetilde{\Lambda}
. While it appears that in general the locally defined near
z_{0}
map

(F^{(0)})^{-1}\circ\hat{\varphi}_{z_{0}}^{-p}\circ F^{(p)}:\widetilde{{\mathcal{C}}}\longrightarrow\widetilde{{\mathcal{C}}},

does not have to send a sub-cylinder to a sub-cylinder, it follows from our construction that, assuming that
global constant
q_{1}\geq 1
is sufficiently large, for every sub-cylinder
\widetilde{\Gamma}
of
\widetilde{{\mathcal{C}}}

of co-length
q_{0}
there exists a sub-cylinder
\widetilde{\Lambda}_{1}
of
\widetilde{{\mathcal{C}}}
of co-length
q_{1}
such that

F^{(p)}(\widetilde{\Lambda}^{\prime}_{1})\subset C\,\hat{\varphi}_{z_{0}}^{p}(F^{(0)}(\widetilde{\Gamma}^{\prime}))
.
Also, for every sub-cylinder

\widetilde{\Lambda}
of
\widetilde{{\mathcal{C}}}
of co-length
q_{0}
there exists a sub-cylinder
\widetilde{\Gamma}_{1}
of
\widetilde{{\mathcal{C}}}
of co-length
q_{2}
such that

F^{(p)}(\widetilde{\Lambda}^{\prime})\supset C\,\hat{\varphi}_{z_{0}}^{p}(F^{(0)}(\widetilde{\Gamma}_{1}^{\prime}))
.

This proves the lemma.

5
Contact Anosov flows

5.1
Temporal distance function vs contact form

We continue here with the assumptions and notation from Sect. 4. However now we assume that

\phi_{t}
is a
C^{2}
contact Anosov flow
on the compact Riemannian manifold
M

with a
C^{2}
invariant contact form
\omega
.

For any
x\in{\mathcal{L}}
consider the
C^{2}
map

\tilde{\varphi}_{x}=(\exp^{u}_{\varphi(x)})^{-1}\circ\varphi\circ\exp^{u}_{x}:E^{u}(x;r(x))\longrightarrow E^{u}(\varphi(x),\tilde{r}(\varphi(x)))\;.

It is well-defined assuming that the
\hat{\epsilon}
-slowly varying radius function
r(x)
and the
\hat{\epsilon}/2
-slowly varying radius function
\tilde{r}(x)

are chosen appropriately as in Sect. 3.
As with the maps
\hat{\varphi}_{x}
, for
y\in{\mathcal{L}}
and an integer
j\geq 1
we will use the notation

\tilde{\varphi}_{y}^{j}=\tilde{\varphi}_{\varphi^{j-1}(y)}\circ\ldots\circ\tilde{\varphi}_{\varphi(y)}\circ\tilde{\varphi}_{y}\hskip 10.00002pt,\hskip 10.00002pt\tilde{\varphi}_{y}^{-j}=(\tilde{\varphi}_{\varphi^{-j}(y)})^{-1}\circ\ldots\circ(\tilde{\varphi}_{\varphi^{-2}(y)})^{-1}\circ(\tilde{\varphi}_{\varphi^{-1}(y)})^{-1}\;,

at any point where these sequences of maps are well-defined.
In a similar way one defines the maps
\tilde{\varphi}_{x}
and their iterations on
E^{s}(x;r(x))
.
It follows from the definitions that
\hat{\varphi}_{x}=(\Psi^{u}_{\varphi(x)})^{-1}\circ\tilde{\varphi}_{x}\circ\Psi^{u}_{x}
.

The main ingredient in this section is the following lemma of Liverani (Lemma B.7 in
[
L
]
)
which significantly strengthens a lemma of Katok and Burns (
[
KB
]
).

Lemma 5.1.
(
[
L
]
)
Let
\phi_{t}
be a
C^{2}
contact Anosov flow on
M
with a

C^{2}
contact form
\omega
.
Then there exist constants
C_{0}>0
,
\beta>0
and
\epsilon_{0}>0
such that
for any
z\in M
, any
x\in W^{u}_{\epsilon_{0}}(z)
and any
y\in W^{s}_{\epsilon_{0}}(z)
we have

|\Delta(x,y)-d\omega_{z}(u,v)|\leq C_{0}\,\left[\|u\|^{2}\,\|v\|^{\beta}+\|u\|^{\beta}\|v\|^{2}\right]\;,

(5.1)

where
u\in E^{u}(z)
and
v\in E^{s}(z)
are such that
\exp^{u}_{z}(u)=x
and
\exp^{s}_{z}(v)=y
.

Replacing the constant
\beta>0
from Sect. 3 with a smaller one if necessary, we will assume
that
the constant
\beta>0
above is the same as the one in Sect. 3
.
Fix a constant
\epsilon_{0}

with the above property. We will also assume that
0<\epsilon_{1}<\epsilon_{0}
satisfy the assumptions
in Sect. 2 and
\epsilon_{0}<r_{0}
.

The two-form
d\omega
is
C^{1}
, so there exists a constant
C_{0}>0
such that

|d\omega_{x}(u,v)|\leq C_{0}\|u\|\,\|v\|\hskip 10.00002pt,\hskip 10.00002ptu,v\in T_{x}M\>,\>x\in M.

(5.2)

Moreover, there exists a constant
c_{0}>0
such that for any
x\in M
and any
u\in E^{u}(x)
with
\|u\|=1

there exists
v\in E^{s}(x)
with
\|v\|=1
such that
|d\omega_{x}(u,v)|\geq 2c_{0}
.
Fix constants
C_{0}
and
c_{0}

with these properties and (5.1) as well.

Corollary 5.2.

Under the assumptions in Lemma
5.1
, we can choose the constant

C_{0}>0

so that for any
\hat{z}\in M
, any
x,z\in W^{u}_{\epsilon_{0}}(\hat{z})
and any
y\in W^{s}_{\epsilon_{0}}(z)
we have

|\Delta(x,y)|\leq C_{0}\,\|\hat{u}-\hat{w}\|^{\beta}\,\|v\|^{\beta},

(5.3)

where
\hat{u},\hat{w}\in E^{u}(\hat{z})
and
v\in E^{s}(z)
are such that
\exp^{u}_{\hat{z}}(\hat{u})=x
,
\exp^{u}_{\hat{z}}(\hat{w})=z
and
\exp^{s}_{z}(v)=y
.
Thus, we can choose the constant
C_{0}>0
and
\beta>0
so that

\displaystyle|\Delta(x,y)|\leq C_{0}\,(d(x,z))^{\beta}\,(d(z,y))^{\beta}

under the above assumptions about
x
and
y
.

Proof of Corollary
5.2.
From the assumptions about
M
,
W^{u}_{\epsilon_{0}}(\hat{z})
is a
C^{2}
local submanifold of
M
.

Given
\hat{z}\in M
,
x\in W^{u}_{\epsilon_{0}}(\hat{z})
and
y\in W^{s}_{\epsilon_{0}}(z)
, let

\hat{u},\hat{w}\in E^{u}(\hat{z})
and
v\in E^{s}(z)
be such that
\exp^{u}_{\hat{z}}(\hat{u})=x
,
\exp^{u}_{\hat{z}}(\hat{w})=z
and
\exp^{s}_{z}(v)=y
.
It follows from Lemma 5.1 used for the points
x\in W^{u}_{\epsilon_{0}}(z)
and
y\in W^{s}_{\epsilon_{0}}(z)
that if

u^{\prime}\in E^{u}(z)
is such that
\exp^{u}_{z}(u^{\prime})=x
, then

|\Delta(x,y)-d\omega_{z}(u^{\prime},v)|\leq C_{0}\,\left[\|u^{\prime}\|^{2}\,\|v\|^{\beta}+\|u^{\prime}\|^{\beta}\|v\|^{2}\right].

(5.4)

Consider the map

\psi=(\exp^{u}_{\hat{z}})^{-1}\circ\exp^{u}_{z}:E^{u}(z,\epsilon)\longrightarrow E^{u}(\hat{z},\epsilon_{0})
, defined for
appropriately chosen small
0<\epsilon\leq\epsilon_{0}
. It follows from general properties of normal neighbourhoods
on Riemannian manifolds that
\frac{1}{C}\|\xi-\eta\|\leq\|\psi(\xi)-\psi(\eta)\|\leq C\,\|\xi-\eta\|
for all
\xi,\eta\in E^{u}(z,\epsilon)

for some global constant
C>0
. Since
x=\exp^{u}_{z}(u^{\prime})=\exp^{u}_{\hat{z}}(\hat{u})
, so
\hat{u}=(\exp^{u}_{\hat{z}})^{-1}(\exp^{u}_{z}(u^{\prime}))
,
and similarly
\hat{w}=(\exp^{u}_{\hat{z}})^{-1}(z)=(\exp^{u}_{\hat{z}})^{-1}(\exp^{u}_{z}(0))
, we get
\psi(u^{\prime})-\psi(0)=\hat{u}-\hat{w}
, so

\|u^{\prime}\|\leq C\,\|\hat{u}-\hat{w}\|.

Now using (5.4) we get

\displaystyle|\Delta(x,y)|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle|d\omega_{z}(u^{\prime},v)|+C_{0}\,\left[\|u^{\prime}\|^{2}\,\|v\|^{\beta}+\|u^{\prime}\|^{\beta}\|v\|^{2}\right]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C_{0}\,\|u^{\prime}\|\,\|v\|+2C_{0}\,\|u^{\prime}\|^{\beta}\,\|v\|^{\beta}\leq 3CC_{0}\|\hat{u}-\hat{w}\|^{\beta}\,\|v\|^{\beta}.

Thus (5.3) holds, taking an appropriate larger constant
C_{0}>0
.

As is well-known the contact form
\omega
vanishes on every stable/unstable manifold of a point on
M
,
while
d\omega
vanishes on every weak stable/unstable manifold (see e.g.

[
KH
]
or Appendix B in
[
L
]
). Moreover,
for Lyapunov regular points
x\in{\mathcal{L}}
and every

u=(u^{(1)},\ldots,u^{(\tilde{k})})\in E^{u}(x;r(x))
and
v=(v^{(1)},\ldots,v^{(\tilde{k})})\in E^{s}(x;r(x))

we have
10
10

10

See e.g. Lemma 9.1 in
[
St5
]
, where this is formally proved.

\displaystyle d\omega_{x}(u,v)=\sum_{i=1}^{\tilde{k}}d\omega_{x}(u^{(i)},v^{(i)}).

In the proof of the following lemma we will use some arguments from Sect. 9 in
[
St5
]
.

Lemma 5.3.

There exist constants
C_{4}>0
and
\tilde{\beta}
with
0<\tilde{\beta}\leq\beta
such that:

(a)
For any unstable cylinder
\widetilde{{\mathcal{C}}}

in
\widetilde{R}
with
\widetilde{{\mathcal{C}}}\cap\widetilde{P}_{0}\neq\emptyset
,
any
x_{0},z_{0}\in\widetilde{{\mathcal{C}}}
, and any
y_{0},b_{0}\in W^{s}_{\epsilon_{0}}(z_{0})
we have

|\Delta(x_{0},y_{0})-\Delta(x_{0},b_{0})|\leq C_{4}\,\mbox{\rm diam}(\widetilde{{\mathcal{C}}})\,(d(y_{0},b_{0}))^{\beta}.

(5.5)

In particular,

|\Delta(x_{0},y_{0})|\leq C_{4}\,\mbox{\rm diam}(\widetilde{{\mathcal{C}}})\,(d(y_{0},z_{0}))^{\beta}\leq C_{4}\,\mbox{\rm diam}(\widetilde{{\mathcal{C}}}).

More precisely we have

\displaystyle|\Delta(x_{0},y_{0})-\Delta(x_{0},b_{0})|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle|d\omega_{z_{0}}(u^{(1)}_{0},v^{(1)}_{0}-\eta^{(1)}_{0})|+C_{0}L_{0}\|u^{(1)}_{0}\|\,\|v_{0}-\eta_{0}\|\,(\|v_{0}-\eta_{0}\|^{\beta}+\|\eta_{0}\|^{\beta})

(5.6)

\displaystyle+C_{4}\,(\mbox{\rm diam}(\widetilde{{\mathcal{C}}}))^{1+\tilde{\beta}},

and

\displaystyle|\Delta(x_{0},y_{0})|\leq|d\omega_{z_{0}}(u^{(1)}_{0},v^{(1)}_{0})|+C_{0}L_{0}\|u^{(1)}_{0}\|\,\|v_{0}\|^{1+\beta}+C_{4}\,(\mbox{\rm diam}(\widetilde{{\mathcal{C}}}))^{1+\tilde{\beta}},

(5.7)

where
u_{0}\in E^{u}(z_{0})
and
v_{0},\eta_{0}\in E^{s}(z_{0})
are such that
\exp^{u}_{z_{0}}(u_{0})=\hat{x}_{0}=\mathcal{T}_{z_{0}}(x_{0})
,

\exp^{s}_{z_{0}}(v_{0})=y_{0}
and
\exp^{s}_{z_{0}}(\eta_{0})=b_{0}
.

(b)
For any unstable cylinder
\widetilde{{\mathcal{C}}}
in
\widetilde{R}
, any
z_{0}\in\widetilde{{\mathcal{C}}}\cap\widetilde{P}_{0}
,
any
x_{0}\in W^{u}_{R}(z_{0})
with
\hat{x}_{0}=\mathcal{T}_{z_{0}}(x_{0})\in\mathcal{T}_{z_{0}}({\mathcal{C}})
, and any
y_{0},b_{0}\in W^{s}_{\epsilon_{0}}(z_{0})
we have

\displaystyle|\Delta(x_{0},y_{0})-\Delta(x_{0},b_{0})|

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle|d\omega_{z_{0}}(u^{(1)}_{0},v^{(1)}_{0}-\eta^{(1)}_{0})|-C_{0}L_{0}\|u^{(1)}_{0}\|\,\|v_{0}-\eta_{0}\|\,(\|v_{0}-\eta_{0}\|^{\beta}+\|\eta_{0}\|^{\beta})

(5.8)

\displaystyle-C_{4}\,(\mbox{\rm diam}(\widetilde{{\mathcal{C}}}))^{1+\tilde{\beta}},

and

\displaystyle|\Delta(x_{0},y_{0})|\geq|d\omega_{z_{0}}(u^{(1)}_{0},v^{(1)}_{0})|-C_{0}L_{0}\|u^{(1)}_{0}\|\,\|v_{0}\|^{1+\beta}-C_{4}\,(\mbox{\rm diam}(\widetilde{{\mathcal{C}}}))^{1+\tilde{\beta}},

(5.9)

where
u_{0}
,
v_{0}
and
\eta_{0}
are as in part
(a).

Proof.

Let
\widetilde{{\mathcal{C}}}
be a cylinder of length
m
in
\widetilde{R}
and let
z_{0}\in\widetilde{{\mathcal{C}}}\cap\widetilde{P}_{0}
.
Let
x_{0},z_{0}\in\widetilde{{\mathcal{C}}}
,
y_{0},b_{0}\in W^{s}_{\epsilon_{0}}(z_{0})
.
Then
R(z_{0})\leq R_{0}
,
r(z_{0})\geq r_{0}
.
We have

x_{0}=\Phi^{u}_{z_{0}}(u_{0})=\exp^{u}_{z_{0}}(\tilde{u}_{0})

for some
u_{0},\tilde{u}_{0}\in E^{u}(z_{0})
with
\tilde{u}_{0}=\Psi^{u}_{z_{0}}(u_{0})
.
Then

\|u_{0}\|,\|\tilde{u}_{0}\|\leq R_{0}\;\mbox{\rm diam}(\widetilde{{\mathcal{C}}}).

Similarly, write

y_{0}=\exp^{s}_{z_{0}}(\tilde{v}_{0})=\Phi^{s}_{z_{0}}(v_{0})\hskip 10.00002pt\mbox{\rm and}\hskip 10.00002ptb_{0}=\exp^{s}_{z_{0}}(\tilde{\eta}_{0})=\Phi^{s}_{z_{0}}(\eta_{0})

for some

v_{0},\tilde{v}_{0},\eta_{0},\tilde{\eta}_{0}\in E^{s}(z_{0})
with
\tilde{v}_{0}=\Psi^{s}_{z_{0}}(v_{0})
and

\tilde{\eta}_{0}=\Psi^{s}_{z_{0}}(\eta_{0})
. It follows from (3.7) that

\|\tilde{v}_{0}-v_{0}\|\leq R_{0}\|v_{0}\|^{1+\beta}\>\>,\>\>\|\tilde{u}_{0}-u_{0}\|\leq R_{0}\|u_{0}\|^{1+\beta}\>\>,\>\>\|\tilde{\eta}_{0}-\eta_{0}\|\leq R_{0}\|\eta_{0}\|^{1+\beta}.

(5.10)

In particular
\|\tilde{v}_{0}\|\leq 2\|v_{0}\|
,
\|\tilde{\eta}_{0}\|\leq 2\|\eta_{0}\|
and
\|\tilde{u}_{0}\|\leq 2\|u_{0}\|\leq 2R_{0}\mbox{\rm diam}(\widetilde{{\mathcal{C}}})
.
For
j\geq 0
define

z_{j}=\varphi^{j}(z_{0})\hskip 10.00002pt,\hskip 10.00002ptx_{j}=\varphi^{j}(x_{0})\hskip 10.00002pt,\hskip 10.00002pty_{j}=\varphi^{j}(y_{0})\hskip 10.00002pt,\hskip 10.00002ptu_{j}=\hat{\varphi}^{j}_{z_{0}}(u_{0})\hskip 10.00002pt,\hskip 10.00002pt\tilde{u}_{j}=\tilde{\varphi}^{j}_{z_{0}}(\tilde{u}_{0}),

\hat{u}_{j}=d\hat{\varphi}^{j}_{z_{0}}(0)\cdot u_{0}\hskip 10.00002pt,\hskip 10.00002pt\hat{v}_{j}=d\hat{\varphi}^{j}_{z_{0}}(0)\cdot v_{0}\hskip 10.00002pt,\hskip 10.00002ptv_{j}=\hat{\varphi}^{j}_{z_{0}}(v_{0})\hskip 10.00002pt,\hskip 10.00002pt\tilde{v}_{j}=\tilde{\varphi}^{j}_{z_{0}}(\tilde{v}_{0}),

b_{j}=\varphi^{j}(b_{0})\hskip 10.00002pt,\hskip 10.00002pt\hat{\eta}_{j}=\hat{\varphi}^{j}_{z_{0}}(0)\cdot\eta_{0}\hskip 10.00002pt,\hskip 10.00002pt\eta_{j}=\hat{\varphi}^{j}_{z_{0}}(\eta_{0})\hskip 10.00002pt,\hskip 10.00002pt\tilde{\eta}_{j}=\tilde{\varphi}^{j}_{z_{0}}(\tilde{\eta}_{0}).

Notice that
\tilde{u}_{j}=\Psi^{u}_{z_{j}}(u_{j})
,
\tilde{v}_{j}=\Psi^{s}_{z_{j}}(v_{j})
,
\tilde{\eta}_{j}=\Psi^{s}_{z_{j}}(\eta_{j}),

so it follows from (3.7) that

\|u_{j}-\tilde{u}_{j}\|\leq R(z_{j})\|u_{j}\|^{1+\beta}\>,\>\|v_{j}-\tilde{v}_{j}\|\leq R(z_{j})\|v_{j}\|^{1+\beta}\>,\>\|\eta_{j}-\tilde{\eta}_{j}\|\leq R(z_{j})\|\eta_{j}\|^{1+\beta}.

(5.11)

Moreover,

\exp^{u}_{z_{j}}(\tilde{u}_{j})=\varphi^{j}(\exp^{u}_{z_{0}}(\tilde{u}_{0}))=\varphi^{j}(x_{0})=x_{j}
,
\exp^{s}_{z_{j}}(\tilde{v}_{j})=y_{j}
and

\exp^{s}_{z_{j}}(\tilde{\eta}_{j})=b_{j}
,
so Lemma 5.1 implies

|\Delta(x_{j},y_{j})-d\omega_{z_{j}}(\tilde{u}_{j},\tilde{v}_{j})|\leq C_{0}\,\left[\|\tilde{u}_{j}\|^{2}\,\|\tilde{v}_{j}\|^{\beta}+\|\tilde{u}_{j}\|^{\beta}\|\tilde{v}_{j}\|^{2}\right]

(5.12)

and

|\Delta(x_{j},b_{j})-d\omega_{z_{j}}(\tilde{u}_{j},\tilde{\eta}_{j})|\leq C_{0}\,\left[\|\tilde{u}_{j}\|^{2}\,\|\tilde{\eta}_{j}\|^{\beta}+\|\tilde{u}_{j}\|^{\beta}\|\tilde{\eta}_{j}\|^{2}\right].

(5.13)

We will use these a bit later.

Let the constants
0<\hat{\epsilon}_{2}\leq\hat{\epsilon}_{1}
be as in Lemma 4.3. By the latter there exists an integer
k<m
so that (4.8) holds,
and moreover for
p=[\tilde{\tau}_{m-k}(z_{0})]
, (4.12) and (4.18) hold, in particular we have

\displaystyle\varphi^{p}(\widetilde{{\mathcal{C}}})\subset B^{u}(z_{p},r(z_{p}))
,
so
x_{p}=\varphi^{p}(x_{0})\in B^{u}(z_{p},r(z_{p}))
and
\|u_{p}\|=\|(\Phi^{u}_{z_{p}})^{-1}(x_{p})\|\leq R(z_{p})\chi\leq R_{0}e^{p\hat{\epsilon}}
.
As before, set
q=[\tilde{\tau}_{m}(z_{0})]
.
Then, as in the proof of Lemma 4.3 we have (4.19).

We will now use an argument from
[
St5
]
with
\ell=p/2
,
assuming for simplicity that
p
is an even number (the other case is similar).
We have
r(z_{j})\geq r_{0}e^{-j\hat{\epsilon}}
, so for all
0\leq j\leq p

it follows from (3.12) that

\|u_{j}\|\leq\|u_{j}\|^{\prime}_{z_{j}}\leq\frac{\|u_{p}\|^{\prime}_{z_{p}}}{\mu_{1}^{p-j}}\leq\Gamma(z_{p})\frac{\|u_{p}\|}{\mu_{1}^{p-j}}\leq\Gamma_{0}e^{p\hat{\epsilon}}\frac{R_{0}e^{p\hat{\epsilon}}}{\mu_{1}^{p-j}}=\Gamma_{0}R_{0}\frac{e^{2p\hat{\epsilon}}}{\mu_{1}^{p-j}}\leq r(z_{j})

(5.14)

for
j\leq\ell=p/2
. Indeed, for such
j
we have

\displaystyle\Gamma_{0}R_{0}\frac{e^{2p\hat{\epsilon}}}{\mu_{1}^{p-j}}e^{j\hat{\epsilon}}\leq\Gamma_{0}R_{0}\frac{e^{3p\hat{\epsilon}}}{\mu_{1}^{p/2}}=\Gamma_{0}R_{0}\left(\frac{e^{3\hat{\epsilon}}}{\mu_{1}}\right)^{p/2}\leq r_{0}
,
assuming that
p
is sufficiently large. Thus (5.14) holds for all
0\leq j\leq\ell
.

The above also shows that
\|u_{j}\|\leq r_{0}
for all
0\leq j\leq\ell
.
Essentially repeating the above estimate, we get

\|u_{\ell}\|\leq\|u_{\ell}\|^{\prime}_{z_{\ell}}\leq\frac{\|u_{p}\|^{\prime}_{z_{p}}}{\mu_{1}^{p-\ell}}\leq\frac{\Gamma(z_{p})e^{\ell\hat{\epsilon}}\|u_{p}\|}{\lambda_{1}^{\ell}}\leq\Gamma_{0}R_{0}\chi\frac{e^{\ell\hat{\epsilon}}e^{2p\hat{\epsilon}}}{\lambda_{1}^{\ell}}\leq\frac{R_{0}\Gamma_{0}e^{5\ell\hat{\epsilon}}}{\lambda_{1}^{\ell}}\leq r_{0}<1,

(5.15)

assuming
\ell
is sufficiently large.
Using (3.12) again (on stable manifolds) and assuming
\|v_{0}\|<1
, we get

\|v_{\ell}\|=\|v_{\ell}\|^{\prime}_{z_{\ell}}\leq\frac{\|v_{0}\|^{\prime}_{z_{0}}}{\mu_{1}^{\ell}}\leq\frac{\Gamma_{0}e^{\ell\hat{\epsilon}}\|v_{0}\|}{\lambda_{1}^{\ell}}\leq\frac{\Gamma_{0}e^{\ell\hat{\epsilon}}}{\lambda_{1}^{\ell}}.

(5.16)

Similarly,
\|\eta_{\ell}\|\leq\frac{\Gamma_{0}e^{\ell\hat{\epsilon}}}{\lambda_{1}^{\ell}}
, assuming
\|\eta_{0}\|<1
.

It follows from (5.10) and (5.11), repeating yet again some of the above estimates, that for
0\leq j\leq\ell
we have

\|\tilde{u}_{j}\|\leq\|u_{j}\|(1+R(z_{j})\|u_{j}\|^{\beta})\leq\|u_{j}\|(1+R_{0}r_{0})\leq 2\|u_{j}\|
.
Also,

\|\tilde{v}_{j}\|\leq 2\|v_{j}\|
for all
j
since the flow is contracting on stable manifolds.
Using these, it follows from (5.12) that

\displaystyle|\Delta(x_{j},y_{j})-d\omega_{z_{j}}(u_{j},v_{j})|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 4C_{0}R(z_{j})\,\|u_{j}\|\,\|v_{j}\|(\|u_{j}\|^{\beta}+\|v_{j}\|^{\beta})

(5.17)

\displaystyle+8C_{0}\,\left[\|u_{j}\|^{2}\,\|v_{j}\|^{\beta}+\|u_{j}\|^{\beta}\|v_{j}\|^{2}\right]

for
0\leq j\leq\ell
. Similarly (5.13) implies

\displaystyle|\Delta(x_{j},b_{j})-d\omega_{z_{j}}(u_{j},\eta_{j})|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 4C_{0}R(z_{j})\,\|u_{j}\|\,\|\eta_{j}\|(\|u_{j}\|^{\beta}+\|\eta_{j}\|^{\beta})

(5.18)

\displaystyle+8C_{0}\,\left[\|u_{j}\|^{2}\,\|\eta_{j}\|^{\beta}+\|u_{j}\|^{\beta}\|\eta_{j}\|^{2}\right]

for
0\leq j\leq\ell
.

Next, we will be estimating

|\Delta(x_{0},y_{0})-d\omega_{z_{0}}(u_{0},v_{0})|.

Since
\Delta
is
\varphi
-invariant and
d\omega
is
d\varphi
-invariant we have

\Delta(x_{0},y_{0})=\Delta(x_{j},y_{j})
and
d\omega_{z_{0}}(u_{0},v_{0})=d\omega_{z_{j}}(\hat{u}_{j},\hat{v}_{j})

for all
j
. (Notice that
d\hat{\varphi}_{x}(0)=d\varphi(x)
for all
x\in M
.)
With
j=\ell
, it follows from Lemma 3.1 and
z_{0}\in\widetilde{P}_{0}
that

\|\hat{u}^{(1)}_{\ell}-u^{(1)}_{\ell}\|\leq L(z_{\ell})\,\|u_{\ell}\|^{1+\beta}\leq L_{0}e^{\ell\hat{\epsilon}}\,\|u_{\ell}\|^{1+\beta}.

(5.19)

Using Lemma 10.7(b) in
[
St5
]
(see Lemma 3.3 above), backwards on stable manifolds, with

a=d\hat{\varphi}^{-\ell}_{z_{\ell}}(0)\cdot(v^{(1)}_{\ell})\in E^{s}(z_{0})
and
b=d\hat{\varphi}^{-\ell}_{z_{\ell}}(0)\cdot(\eta^{(1)}_{\ell})\in E^{s}(z_{0})
,
since

v_{0}=\hat{\varphi}^{-\ell}_{z_{\ell}}(v_{\ell})
and
\eta_{0}=\hat{\varphi}^{-\ell}_{z_{\ell}}(\eta_{\ell})
, it follows that

\|(a^{(1)}-b^{(1)})-(v^{(1)}_{0}-\eta^{(1)}_{0})\|\leq L_{0}\left[\|v_{0}-\eta_{0}\|^{1+\beta}+\|\eta_{0}\|^{\beta}\|v_{0}-\eta_{0}\|\right]\leq 2L_{0}\|v_{0}-\eta_{0}\|.

Thus,

\|d\hat{\varphi}^{-\ell}_{z_{\ell}}(0)\cdot(v^{(1)}_{\ell}-\eta^{(1)}_{\ell})-(v^{(1)}_{0}-\eta^{(1)}_{0})\|\leq L_{0}\|v_{0}-\eta_{0}\|\,(\|v_{0}-\eta_{0}\|^{\beta}+\|\eta_{0}\|^{\beta}).

(5.20)

Proceeding as in Sect. 9 in
[
St5
]
and using (5.19), we obtain

\displaystyle|d\omega_{z_{\ell}}(u_{\ell},v_{\ell}-\eta_{\ell})|\leq|d\omega_{z_{\ell}}(u^{(1)}_{\ell},v^{(1)}_{\ell}-\eta^{(1)}_{\ell})|+C_{0}\sum_{i=2}^{\tilde{k}}\|u_{\ell}^{(i)}\|\,(\|v_{\ell}^{(i)}\|+\|\eta_{\ell}^{(i)}\|)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle|d\omega_{z_{\ell}}(\hat{u}^{(1)}_{\ell},v^{(1)}_{\ell}-\eta^{(1)}_{\ell})|+C_{0}L_{0}e^{\ell\hat{\epsilon}}\|u_{\ell}\|^{1+\beta}\|v^{(1)}_{\ell}-\eta^{(1)}_{\ell}\|+C_{0}\sum_{i=2}^{\tilde{k}}\|u_{\ell}^{(i)}\|\,(\|v_{\ell}^{(i)}\|+\|\eta_{\ell}^{(i)}\|)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle|d\omega_{z_{\ell}}(d\hat{\varphi}^{\ell}_{z_{0}}(0)\cdot u^{(1)}_{0},v^{(1)}_{\ell}-\eta^{(1)}_{\ell})|+C_{0}L_{0}e^{\ell\hat{\epsilon}}\|u_{\ell}\|^{1+\beta}\|v^{(1)}_{\ell}-\eta^{(1)}_{\ell}\|+C_{0}\sum_{i=2}^{\tilde{k}}\|u_{\ell}^{(i)}\|\,(\|v_{\ell}^{(i)}\|+\|\eta_{\ell}^{(i)}\|)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle|d\omega_{z_{0}}(u^{(1)}_{0},d\hat{\varphi}^{-\ell}_{z_{\ell}}(0)\cdot(v^{(1)}_{\ell}-\eta^{(1)}_{\ell}))|+C_{0}L_{0}e^{\ell\hat{\epsilon}}\|u_{\ell}\|^{1+\beta}\|v^{(1)}_{\ell}-\eta^{(1)}_{\ell}\|+C_{0}\sum_{i=2}^{\tilde{k}}\|u_{\ell}^{(i)}\|\,(\|v_{\ell}^{(i)}\|+\|\eta_{\ell}^{(i)}\|).

Now (5.20) implies

\displaystyle|d\omega_{z_{0}}(u^{(1)}_{0},d\hat{\varphi}^{-\ell}_{z_{\ell}}(0)\cdot(v^{(1)}_{\ell}-\eta^{(1)}_{\ell})|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle|d\omega_{z_{0}}(u^{(1)}_{0},v^{(1)}_{0}-\eta^{(1)}_{0})|+|d\omega_{z_{\ell}}(u^{(1)}_{0},d\hat{\varphi}^{-\ell}_{z_{\ell}}(0)\cdot(v^{(1)}_{\ell}-\eta^{(1)}_{\ell})-(v^{(1)}_{0}-\eta^{(1)}_{0})|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle|d\omega_{z_{0}}(u^{(1)}_{0},v^{(1)}_{0}-\eta^{(1)}_{0})|+C_{0}L_{0}\|u^{(1)}_{0}\|\,\|v_{0}-\eta_{0}\|\,(\|v_{0}-\eta_{0}\|^{\beta}+\|\eta_{0}\|^{\beta}).

The latter and the previous estimate yield

\displaystyle|d\omega_{z_{\ell}}(u_{\ell},v_{\ell}-\eta_{\ell})|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle|d\omega_{z_{0}}(u^{(1)}_{0},v^{(1)}_{0}-\eta^{(1)}_{0})|+C_{0}L_{0}\|u^{(1)}_{0}\|\,\|v_{0}-\eta_{0}\|\,(\|v_{0}-\eta_{0}\|^{\beta}+\|\eta_{0}\|^{\beta})

(5.21)

\displaystyle+C_{0}L_{0}e^{\ell\hat{\epsilon}}\|u_{\ell}\|^{1+\beta}\|v^{(1)}_{\ell}-\eta^{(1)}_{\ell}\|+C_{0}\sum_{i=2}^{\tilde{k}}\|u_{\ell}^{(i)}\|\,(\|v_{\ell}^{(i)}\|+\|\eta_{\ell}^{(i)}\|).

Consequently,

\displaystyle|d\omega_{z_{\ell}}(u_{\ell},v_{\ell}-\eta_{\ell})|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\mbox{\rm Const}\;\,\mbox{\rm diam}(\widetilde{{\mathcal{C}}})\,\|v_{0}-\eta_{0}\|+\mbox{\rm Const}\;e^{\ell\hat{\epsilon}}\|u_{\ell}\|^{1+\beta}\|v^{(1)}_{\ell}-\eta^{(1)}_{\ell}\|

(5.22)

\displaystyle+C_{0}\sum_{i=2}^{\tilde{k}}\|u_{\ell}^{(i)}\|\,(\|v_{\ell}^{(i)}\|+\|\eta_{\ell}^{(i)}\|).

Using similar estimates from below, we obtain

\displaystyle|d\omega_{z_{\ell}}(u_{\ell},v_{\ell}-\eta_{\ell})|

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle|d\omega_{z_{0}}(u^{(1)}_{0},v^{(1)}_{0}-\eta^{(1)}_{0})|-C_{0}L_{0}\|u^{(1)}_{0}\|\,\|v_{0}-\eta_{0}\|\,(\|v_{0}-\eta_{0}\|^{\beta}+\|\eta_{0}\|^{\beta})

(5.23)

\displaystyle-C_{0}L_{0}e^{\ell\hat{\epsilon}}\|u_{\ell}\|^{1+\beta}\|v^{(1)}_{\ell}-\eta^{(1)}_{\ell}\|-C_{0}\sum_{i=2}^{\tilde{k}}\|u_{\ell}^{(i)}\|\,(\|v_{\ell}^{(i)}\|+\|\eta_{\ell}^{(i)}\|).

Next, it follows from (
4.5
) that

\displaystyle\mbox{\rm diam}(\widetilde{{\mathcal{C}}})\geq\frac{e^{-q\hat{\epsilon}_{7}}}{C_{3}\lambda_{1}^{q}}

for some global constant
C_{3}>0
, so

\displaystyle\lambda_{1}^{q}\,e^{q\hat{\epsilon}_{7}}\geq\frac{1}{C_{3}\,\mbox{\rm diam}(\widetilde{{\mathcal{C}}})}
,
that is

\displaystyle q\,\log\lambda_{1}+q\hat{\epsilon}_{7}\geq\log\frac{1}{C_{3}\,\mbox{\rm diam}(\widetilde{{\mathcal{C}}})}>0
.
Assuming
\hat{\epsilon}
and so
\hat{\epsilon}_{7}
is sufficiently small and using (4.19), this gives

\frac{p}{1-\hat{\epsilon}_{6}}\,(\log\lambda_{1}+\hat{\epsilon}_{7})\geq\log\frac{1}{C_{3}\,\mbox{\rm diam}(\widetilde{{\mathcal{C}}})}
,
therefore for
\ell=p/2
we get

\ell>\frac{1-\hat{\epsilon}_{6}}{2(\log\lambda_{1}+\hat{\epsilon}_{7})}\,\log\frac{1}{C_{3}\,\mbox{\rm diam}(\widetilde{{\mathcal{C}}})}.

(5.24)

Now (5.15) implies

\displaystyle\|u_{\ell}\|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle R_{0}\Gamma_{0}(\lambda_{1}e^{-5\hat{\epsilon}})^{-\ell}=R_{0}\Gamma_{0}e^{-\ell\log(\lambda_{1}e^{-5\hat{\epsilon}})}\leq R_{0}\Gamma_{0}\,e^{-\frac{(1-\hat{\epsilon}_{6})\log(\lambda_{1}e^{-5\hat{\epsilon}})}{2(\log\lambda_{1}+\hat{\epsilon}_{7})}\log\left(\frac{1}{C_{3}\,{\rm\footnotesize diam}(\widetilde{{\mathcal{C}}})}\right)}

(5.25)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle R_{0}\Gamma_{0}\,\left(\frac{1}{C_{3}\,\mbox{\rm diam}(\widetilde{{\mathcal{C}}})}\right)^{-\frac{(1-\hat{\epsilon}_{6})(\log\lambda_{1}-5\hat{\epsilon})}{2(\log\lambda_{1}+\hat{\epsilon}_{7})}}\leq R_{0}\Gamma_{0}C_{3}\,\left(\mbox{\rm diam}(\widetilde{{\mathcal{C}}})\right)^{\frac{(1-\hat{\epsilon}_{6})(\log\lambda_{1}-5\hat{\epsilon})}{2(\log\lambda_{1}+\hat{\epsilon}_{7})}}.

(Using here
C_{3}>1
and
\frac{(1-\hat{\epsilon}_{6})(\log\lambda_{1}-5\hat{\epsilon})}{2(\log\lambda_{1}+\hat{\epsilon}_{7})}<1
.)
Similarly, (5.16) yields

\displaystyle\|v_{\ell}\|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\Gamma_{0}(\lambda_{1}e^{-\hat{\epsilon}})^{-\ell}\leq\Gamma_{0}\,e^{-\frac{(1-\hat{\epsilon}_{6})\log(\lambda_{1}e^{-\hat{\epsilon}})}{2(\log\lambda_{1}+\hat{\epsilon}_{7})}\log\left(\frac{1}{C_{3}\,{\rm\footnotesize diam}(\widetilde{{\mathcal{C}}})}\right)}

(5.26)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\Gamma_{0}C_{3}\,\left(\mbox{\rm diam}(\widetilde{{\mathcal{C}}})\right)^{\frac{(1-\hat{\epsilon}_{6})(\log\lambda_{1}-\hat{\epsilon})}{2(\log\lambda_{1}+\hat{\epsilon}_{7})}}.

The same estimate holds for
\|\eta_{\ell}\|
.

Using these we get the following estimates for terms in (5.18) with
j=\ell
:

\displaystyle\|u_{\ell}\|\,\|v_{\ell}\|(\|u_{\ell}\|^{\beta}+\|v_{\ell}\|^{\beta})\leq C^{\prime}_{3}\left(\mbox{\rm diam}(\widetilde{{\mathcal{C}}})\right)^{(2+\beta)\frac{(1-\hat{\epsilon}_{6})(\log\lambda_{1}-5\hat{\epsilon})}{2(\log\lambda_{1}+\hat{\epsilon}_{7})}}\leq C^{\prime}_{3}(\mbox{\rm diam}(\widetilde{{\mathcal{C}}}))^{1+\hat{\beta}},

where
C^{\prime}_{3}=2(\Gamma_{0}R_{0}C_{3})^{3}
and we choose

0<\hat{\beta}=\frac{1}{2}\min\left\{\frac{\beta}{4}\;,\>\frac{\log\lambda_{2}-\log\lambda_{1}}{2\log\lambda_{1}}\right\}.

(5.27)

Then

(2+\beta)\frac{(1-\hat{\epsilon}_{6})(\log\lambda_{1}-5\hat{\epsilon})}{2(\log\lambda_{1}+\hat{\epsilon}_{7})}=(1+\beta/2)(1-\hat{\epsilon}_{6})\left(1-\frac{5\hat{\epsilon}+\hat{\epsilon}_{7}}{\log\lambda_{1}+\hat{\epsilon}_{7}}\right)\geq 1+\hat{\beta},

assuming
\hat{\epsilon}_{6}>0
,
\hat{\epsilon}_{7}>0
and
\hat{\epsilon}>0
are sufficiently small.
Similarly, we obtain

e^{\ell\hat{\epsilon}}\|u_{\ell}\|^{1+\beta}\|v_{\ell}\|\leq C^{\prime\prime}_{3}(\mbox{\rm diam}(\widetilde{{\mathcal{C}}}))^{1+\hat{\beta}}

(5.28)

for some global constant
C^{\prime\prime}_{3}>0
.
To prove the latter we need an estimate from above similar to (5.24).
First, using (
4.5
) we get

\displaystyle\mbox{\rm diam}(\widetilde{{\mathcal{C}}})\leq\frac{C_{3}e^{q\hat{\epsilon}_{7}}}{\lambda_{1}^{q}},

so

\displaystyle\lambda_{1}^{q}\,e^{-q\hat{\epsilon}_{7}}\leq\frac{C_{3}}{\mbox{\rm diam}(\widetilde{{\mathcal{C}}})},

that is

\displaystyle q\,\log\lambda_{1}-q\hat{\epsilon}_{7}\leq\log\frac{C_{3}}{\mbox{\rm diam}(\widetilde{{\mathcal{C}}})}.

Now (4.19) yields

\displaystyle p\,(\log\lambda_{1}-\hat{\epsilon}_{7})\leq\log\frac{C_{3}}{\mbox{\rm diam}(\widetilde{{\mathcal{C}}})},

therefore for
\ell=p/2
we get

\ell\leq\frac{1}{2(\log\lambda_{1}-\hat{\epsilon}_{7})}\,\log\frac{C_{3}}{\mbox{\rm diam}(\widetilde{{\mathcal{C}}})}.

This, (5.15) and (5.16), using (5.24) again as well, imply

\displaystyle e^{\ell\hat{\epsilon}}\|u_{\ell}\|^{1+\beta}\|v_{\ell}\|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\left(\frac{C_{3}}{\mbox{\rm diam}(\widetilde{{\mathcal{C}}})}\right)^{\frac{\hat{\epsilon}}{2(\log\lambda_{1}-\hat{\epsilon}_{7})}}\,C^{\prime}_{3}\left(\mbox{\rm diam}(\widetilde{{\mathcal{C}}})\right)^{(2+\beta)\frac{(1-\hat{\epsilon}_{6})(\log\lambda_{1}-5\hat{\epsilon})}{2(\log\lambda_{1}+\hat{\epsilon}_{7})}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C_{3}C^{\prime}_{3}\,\left(\mbox{\rm diam}(\widetilde{{\mathcal{C}}})\right)^{(2+\beta)\frac{(1-\hat{\epsilon}_{6})(\log\lambda_{1}-5\hat{\epsilon})}{2(\log\lambda_{1}+\hat{\epsilon}_{7})}-\frac{\hat{\epsilon}}{2(\log\lambda_{1}-\hat{\epsilon}_{7})}}\leq C^{\prime\prime}_{3}\,(\mbox{\rm diam}(\widetilde{{\mathcal{C}}}))^{1+\hat{\beta}},

where
C^{\prime\prime}_{3}=C_{3}\,C^{\prime}_{3}
. The above holds since
\frac{\hat{\epsilon}}{2(\log\lambda_{1}-\hat{\epsilon}_{7})}<\frac{\beta}{4}
,
assuming
\hat{\epsilon}>0
is sufficiently small, and

\displaystyle(2+\beta)\frac{(1-\hat{\epsilon}_{6})(\log\lambda_{1}-5\hat{\epsilon})}{2(\log\lambda_{1}+\hat{\epsilon}_{7})}-\frac{\beta}{4}\geq(1+\beta/2)(1-\hat{\epsilon}_{6})\left(1-\frac{5\hat{\epsilon}+\hat{\epsilon}_{7}}{\log\lambda_{1}+\hat{\epsilon}_{7}}\right)-\frac{\beta}{4}\geq 1+\hat{\beta},

assuming that
\hat{\epsilon}_{6}>0
and
\hat{\epsilon}>0
are sufficiently small.
This proves (5.28). We get a similar estimate replacing
v_{\ell}
by
\eta_{\ell}
.

Next, for any
u=u^{(1)}+u^{(2)}+\ldots+u^{(\tilde{k})}\in E^{u}(z)
or
E^{s}(z)
,
z\in M
, set

\check{u}^{(2)}=u^{(2)}+\ldots+u^{(\tilde{k})}
,
so that
u=u^{(1)}+\check{u}^{(2)}
.
Using Lemma 3.5 in
[
St3
]
(see Lemma 3.2 above),
p=2\ell
,
u_{p}\in E^{u}(z_{p},r(z_{p}))
and
\|u_{p}\|\leq R_{0}e^{p\hat{\epsilon}}
,
we get

\|\check{u}^{(2)}_{\ell}\|^{\prime}_{z_{\ell}}\leq\frac{\Gamma_{0}e^{p\hat{\epsilon}}\|\check{u}^{(2)}_{p}\|}{\mu_{2}^{\ell}}\leq\frac{\Gamma_{0}e^{p\hat{\epsilon}}\|u_{p}\|}{\mu_{2}^{\ell}}\leq\frac{\Gamma_{0}R_{0}e^{4\ell\hat{\epsilon}}}{\mu_{2}^{\ell}}.

Similarly, using Lemma 3.2 above (backwards for the map
\varphi^{-1}
on stable manifolds),

z_{0}\in\widetilde{P}_{0}
,
v_{0}\in E^{s}(z_{0},r_{0})
and the fact that
\|v_{0}\|\leq\epsilon<1
, we get

\|\check{v}^{(2)}_{\ell}\|^{\prime}_{z_{\ell}}\leq\frac{\Gamma_{0}\|v_{0}\|e^{2\ell\hat{\epsilon}}}{\mu_{2}^{\ell}}\leq\frac{\Gamma_{0}e^{2\ell\hat{\epsilon}}}{\mu_{2}^{\ell}}.

Hence for
i\geq 2
we have

\|u^{(i)}_{\ell}\|\leq|\check{u}^{(2)}_{\ell}|\leq\Gamma(z_{\ell})\|\check{u}^{(2)}_{\ell}\|\leq\frac{\Gamma^{2}_{0}R_{0}e^{5\ell\hat{\epsilon}}}{\lambda_{2}^{\ell}},

where we used
\mu_{2}=\lambda_{2}e^{-\hat{\epsilon}}
. Similarly

\displaystyle\|v^{(i)}_{\ell}\|\leq\frac{\Gamma^{2}_{0}e^{3\ell\hat{\epsilon}}}{\lambda_{2}^{\ell}}.

From these estimates, (5.24), and the assumptions about
\hat{\epsilon}
, we get

\displaystyle\|u^{(i)}_{\ell}\|\,\|v^{(i)}_{\ell}\|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\Gamma_{0}^{4}R_{0}\,(\lambda_{2}e^{-5\hat{\epsilon}})^{-2\ell}=\Gamma_{0}^{4}R_{0}\,e^{-2\ell\log(\lambda_{2}e^{-5\hat{\epsilon}})}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\Gamma_{0}^{4}R_{0}\,e^{\frac{-(1-\hat{\epsilon}_{6})\log(\lambda_{2}e^{-5\hat{\epsilon}})}{\log\lambda_{1}+\hat{\epsilon}_{7}}\,\log\left(\frac{1}{C_{3}\,{\rm\footnotesize diam}(\widetilde{{\mathcal{C}}})}\right)}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\Gamma_{0}^{4}R_{0}\,\left(C_{3}\,\mbox{\rm diam}(\widetilde{{\mathcal{C}}})\right)^{\frac{(1-\hat{\epsilon}_{6})(\log\lambda_{2}-5\hat{\epsilon})}{\log\lambda_{1}+\hat{\epsilon}_{7}}}\leq C^{\prime\prime}_{3}(\mbox{\rm diam}(\widetilde{{\mathcal{C}}}))^{1+\hat{\beta}},

where
C^{\prime\prime}_{3}=\Gamma_{0}^{4}R_{0}(C_{3})^{\log\lambda_{2}/\log\lambda_{1}}
. Here we are assuming (5.27) and that

\hat{\epsilon}_{6}>0
and
\hat{\epsilon}_{7}>0
are sufficiently small. Since

\frac{(1-\hat{\epsilon}_{6})\log\lambda_{2}}{\log\lambda_{1}+\hat{\epsilon}_{7}}>\frac{\log\lambda_{2}+\log\lambda_{1}}{2\log\lambda_{1}},

for small
\hat{\epsilon}_{6}>0
,
\hat{\epsilon}_{7}>0
, we derive

\frac{(1-\hat{\epsilon}_{6})(\log\lambda_{2}-5\hat{\epsilon})}{\log\lambda_{1}+\hat{\epsilon}_{7}}=\frac{(1-\hat{\epsilon}_{6})\log\lambda_{2}}{\log\lambda_{1}+\hat{\epsilon}_{7}}-\frac{5(1-\hat{\epsilon}_{6})\hat{\epsilon}}{\log\lambda_{1}+\hat{\epsilon}_{7}}>\frac{\log\lambda_{2}+\log\lambda_{1}}{2\log\lambda_{1}}-\frac{5\hat{\epsilon}}{\log\lambda_{1}}\geq 1+\hat{\beta},

assuming that
\hat{\epsilon}>0
is sufficiently small. This proves the above estimate for
\|u^{(i)}_{\ell}\|\,\|v^{(i)}_{\ell}\|
.

Using (5.22), (5.28) and the above estimates for
\|u^{(i)}_{\ell}\|
and
\|v^{(i)}_{\ell}\|
, we obtain

\displaystyle|d\omega_{z_{\ell}}(u_{\ell},v_{\ell}-\eta_{\ell})|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\mbox{\rm Const}\;\,\mbox{\rm diam}(\widetilde{{\mathcal{C}}})\,\|v_{0}-\eta_{0}\|+\mbox{\rm Const}\;\,(\mbox{\rm diam}(\widetilde{{\mathcal{C}}}))^{1+\hat{\beta}}.

It now follows from (5.17) and (5.18) with
j=\ell
and the previous estimates that

\displaystyle|\Delta(x_{0},y_{0})-\Delta(x_{0},b_{0})|=|\Delta(x_{\ell},y_{\ell})-\Delta(x_{\ell},b_{\ell})|

(5.29)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle|d\omega_{z_{\ell}}(u_{\ell},v_{\ell})-d\omega_{z_{\ell}}(u_{\ell},\eta_{\ell})|+4C_{0}R(z_{\ell})\,\|u_{\ell}\|\,\|v_{\ell}\|(\|u_{\ell}\|^{\beta}+\|v_{\ell}\|^{\beta})

\displaystyle+8C_{0}\,\left[\|u_{\ell}\|^{2}\,\|v_{\ell}\|^{\beta}+\|u_{\ell}\|^{\beta}\|v_{\ell}\|^{2}\right]

\displaystyle+4C_{0}R(z_{\ell})\,\|u_{\ell}\|\,\|\eta_{\ell}\|(\|u_{\ell}\|^{\beta}+\|\eta_{\ell}\|^{\beta})+8C_{0}\,\left[\|u_{\ell}\|^{2}\,\|\eta_{\ell}\|^{\beta}+\|u_{\ell}\|^{\beta}\|\eta_{\ell}\|^{2}\right]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle|d\omega_{z_{\ell}}(u_{\ell},v_{\ell}-\eta_{\ell})|+\mbox{\rm Const}\;\,(\mbox{\rm diam}(\widetilde{{\mathcal{C}}}))^{1+\hat{\beta}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\mbox{\rm Const}\;\,\mbox{\rm diam}(\widetilde{{\mathcal{C}}})\,\|v_{0}-\eta_{0}\|+\mbox{\rm Const}\;\,(\mbox{\rm diam}(\widetilde{{\mathcal{C}}}))^{1+\hat{\beta}}.

The more precise estimate (5.6), for some global constant
C_{4}>0
, follows immediately from (5.21).
In particular, with
\eta_{0}=0
, the latter gives (5.7).

In a similar way, this time using (5.23) we obtain (5.8), and with
\eta_{0}=0
it gives (5.9).

To prove (5.5) we just repeat an argument from Sect. 9.3.3 in
[
St5
]
.

Case 1.

\mbox{\rm diam}(\widetilde{{\mathcal{C}}})\leq\|v_{0}-\eta_{0}\|^{\beta/2}
. Then by (5.29),

|\Delta(x_{0},y_{0})-\Delta(x_{0},b_{0})|\leq\mbox{\rm Const}\;\,\mbox{\rm diam}(\widetilde{{\mathcal{C}}})\,\|v_{0}-\eta_{0}\|^{\hat{\beta}\beta/2}.

So, (5.5) holds with
\beta
replaced by
\hat{\beta}\beta/2
.

Case 2.

\mbox{\rm diam}(\widetilde{{\mathcal{C}}})\geq\|v_{0}-\eta_{0}\|^{\beta/2}
.
Consider the point
X=\phi_{\Delta(x_{0},y_{0})}([x_{0},y_{0}])\in W^{u}_{\epsilon}(y_{0})
.
It is easy to see that

\Delta(x_{0},y_{0})-\Delta(x_{0},b_{0})=\Delta(X,y_{0})-\Delta(X,b_{0})=-\Delta(X,b_{0}).

We have
X=\exp^{u}_{y_{0}}(\tilde{t})
and
b_{0}=\exp^{s}_{y_{0}}(\tilde{s})
for some
\tilde{t}\in E^{u}(y_{0})
and

\tilde{s}\in E^{s}(y_{0})
. Clearly
\|\tilde{t}\|\leq\mbox{\rm Const}\;
.
Using Lemma 5.1 we get

|\Delta(X,b_{0})|\leq C_{0}[|d\omega_{y_{0}}(\tilde{t},\tilde{s})|+\|\tilde{t}\|^{2}\|\tilde{s}\|^{\beta}+\|\tilde{t}\|^{\beta}\|\tilde{s}\|^{2}]\leq\mbox{\rm Const}\;\,\|\tilde{s}\|^{\beta}.

However,
\|\tilde{s}\|\leq\mbox{\rm Const}\;d(y_{0},b_{0})\leq\mbox{\rm Const}\;\|v_{0}-\eta_{0}\|
, so

|\Delta(X,b_{0})|\leq\mbox{\rm Const}\;\|v_{0}-\eta_{0}\|^{\beta}\leq\mbox{\rm Const}\;\mbox{\rm diam}(\widetilde{{\mathcal{C}}})\|v_{0}-\eta_{0}\|^{\beta/2}.

Therefore

\displaystyle|\Delta(x_{0},y_{0})-\Delta(x_{0},b_{0})|\leq\mbox{\rm Const}\;\mbox{\rm diam}(\widetilde{{\mathcal{C}}})\|v_{0}-\eta_{0}\|^{\beta/2}
,
so (5.5) holds with
\beta
replaced by
\beta/2
.
This proves Lemma 5.3.

5.2
Non-integrability of contact Anosov flows

Fix constants

C_{4}>0
,
\beta>0
and
\tilde{\beta}>0
with the properties in Lemma 5.3.
Let the small constant
\hat{\epsilon}>0
be as in Sect. 3 and
0<\epsilon_{1}<\epsilon_{0}
as in Sect. 2.

Recall the projections
\mathcal{T}_{z}:W^{u}_{\widetilde{R}}(z)\longrightarrow W^{u}_{\epsilon_{0}}(z)
for
z\in R
from Sect. 4.1
and the constants
C_{0}>0
and
c_{0}>0
introduced before Lemma 5.1. Set

\delta_{0}=\left(\frac{c_{0}}{32C_{0}L_{0}R_{0}}\right)^{1/\tilde{\beta}}.

(5.30)

The following lemma is derived from the non-integrability of the flow which stems from the fact that the
flow is contact.

Lemma 5.4.

There exist global constants
\tilde{m}_{0}\geq 1
,
\delta^{\prime}\in(0,1)
,
d_{2}\in(0,1)

and
\hat{\epsilon}_{13}\leq\mbox{\rm const }\hat{\epsilon}
which can be made
arbitrarily small with
\hat{\epsilon}
such that for every integer
m\geq\tilde{m}_{0}
we have the following:

(a)
For any
z_{0}\in\widetilde{R}\cap\widetilde{P}_{0}
and any cylinder
\widetilde{{\mathcal{C}}}
of length
m
in
W^{u}_{\widetilde{R}}(z_{0})
with
z_{0}\in\widetilde{{\mathcal{C}}}
,
if
x_{0}\in\widetilde{{\mathcal{C}}}
is such that

u_{0}=(\Phi^{u}_{z_{0}})^{-1}(\mathcal{T}_{z_{0}}(x_{0}))\in E^{u}(z_{0})

(5.31)

satisfies

\|u^{(1)}_{0}\|\geq\kappa\,\mbox{\rm diam}(\widetilde{{\mathcal{C}}}),

(5.32)

where
\kappa=d_{2}e^{-m\hat{\epsilon}_{13}}
,
then there exist a point
y_{0}=y_{0}(z_{0},x_{0})\in B^{s}(z_{0},\epsilon_{1})
such that we have

12c_{0}\delta_{0}\,\|u^{(1)}_{0}\|\leq|\Delta(x_{0},b_{1})-\Delta(x_{0},b_{2})|

(5.33)

for any
b_{1},b_{2}\in W^{s}_{R}(z_{0})
with
d(z_{0},b_{1})<2\delta^{\prime}
and
d(y_{0},b_{2})<2\delta^{\prime}
. Thus,

12c_{0}\delta_{0}\,\kappa\,\mbox{\rm diam}(\widetilde{{\mathcal{C}}})\leq|\Delta(x_{0},b_{1})-\Delta(x_{0},b_{2})|

(5.34)

under the above conditions.

(b)
There exists an integer
N_{0}\geq 1

such that for any integer
N\geq N_{0}
, any
z_{0}\in\widetilde{R}\cap\widetilde{P}_{0}
, any cylinder
\widetilde{{\mathcal{C}}}
in
\widetilde{R}

of length
m\geq\tilde{m}_{0}
in
W^{u}_{\widetilde{R}}(z_{0})
with
z_{0}\in\widetilde{{\mathcal{C}}}
, and any
x_{0}\in\widetilde{{\mathcal{C}}}
such that

u_{0}\in E^{u}(z_{0})
with
(
5.31
)
satisfies
(
5.32
)
, there exist families of points

y_{1}=y_{1}(z_{0},x_{0})\in\widetilde{{\mathcal{P}}}^{N}(B^{u}(z_{0},\epsilon^{\prime}))\cap B^{s}(z_{0},\delta^{\prime})\>\>,\>\>y_{2}=y_{2}(z_{0},x_{0})\in\widetilde{{\mathcal{P}}}^{N}(B^{u}(z_{0},\epsilon^{\prime}))\cap B^{s}(y_{0},\delta^{\prime}).

such that

(
5.33
)
holds for any
b_{1}\in B^{s}(y_{1},\delta^{\prime})
, any
b_{2}\in B^{s}(y_{2},\delta^{\prime})
.

Proof of Lemma
5.4.
(a)
Fix for a moment
z_{0}\in\widetilde{R}\cap\widetilde{P}_{0}
. Let
\widetilde{{\mathcal{C}}}
be a cylinder of length
m\geq\tilde{m}_{0}
in
\widetilde{R}
with
z_{0}\in\widetilde{{\mathcal{C}}}
,
and let
x_{0}
,
u_{0}
and
\kappa
satisfy (
5.31
) – (
5.32
).
By the choice of
c_{0}>0
in Sect. 5.1, there exists a vector
\tilde{v}\in E^{s}_{1}(z_{0})
with
\|\tilde{v}\|=1

such that

d\omega_{z_{0}}(u^{(1)}_{0},\tilde{v})\geq 2c_{0}\,\|u^{(1)}_{0}\|.

(5.35)

Fix a vector
\tilde{v}
with the above property and set

v_{0}=10\delta_{0}\,\tilde{v}\in E^{s}_{1}(z_{0})\hskip 10.00002pt,\hskip 10.00002pty_{0}=\Phi^{s}_{z_{0}}(v_{0})\in W^{s}_{\epsilon_{1}}(z_{0}),

(5.36)

assuming that
\delta_{0}>0
is sufficiently small so that
10\delta_{0}<\epsilon_{1}
.
Then

\frac{\|v_{0}\|}{R_{0}}\leq d(z_{0},y_{0})\leq\|v_{0}\|
,
and

|d\omega_{z_{0}}(u_{0},v_{0})|=|d\omega_{z_{0}}(u^{(1)}_{0},v_{0})|\geq 2c_{0}\|u^{(1)}_{0}\|\|v_{0}\|=20c_{0}\delta_{0}\|u^{(1)}_{0}\|,

while (
5.31
) and (
5.32
) imply

\kappa\,\mbox{\rm diam}(\widetilde{{\mathcal{C}}})\leq\|u^{(1)}_{0}\|\leq\|u_{0}\|\leq R_{0}\,\mbox{\rm diam}(\widetilde{{\mathcal{C}}})
.
Notice that

|d\omega_{z_{0}}(u^{(1)}_{0},v_{0})|\leq C_{0}\|u^{(1)}_{0}\|\,\|v_{0}\|=10C_{0}\delta_{0}\|u^{(1)}_{0}\|\leq 10C_{0}\,\delta_{0}\,R_{0}\,\mbox{\rm diam}(\widetilde{{\mathcal{C}}}).

We will now prove that

|\Delta(x_{0},y_{0})|\geq 14c_{0}\delta_{0}\,\|u^{(1)}_{0}\|.

(5.37)

In what follows we use the notation from the proof of Lemma 5.3 and also part (b) in Lemma 5.3 (see (5.9) there).
Using it with
b_{0}=z_{0}
(so
\eta_{0}=0
) and using
v_{0}\in E^{s}_{1}(z_{0})
and (5.36) we get

\displaystyle|\Delta(x_{0},y_{0})|

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle|d\omega_{z_{0}}(u^{(1)}_{0},v_{0})|-C_{0}L_{0}\,\|u^{(1)}_{0}\|\,\|v_{0}\|^{1+\beta}-C_{4}\,(\mbox{\rm diam}(\widetilde{{\mathcal{C}}}))^{1+\tilde{\beta}}

(5.38)

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle 2c_{0}\,\|u^{(1)}_{0}\|\,\|v_{0}\|-C_{0}L_{0}\,\|u^{(1)}_{0}\|\,\|v_{0}\|^{1+\beta}-C_{4}\,(\mbox{\rm diam}(\widetilde{{\mathcal{C}}}))^{1+\tilde{\beta}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\|u^{(1)}_{0}\|\|v_{0}\|(2c_{0}-C_{0}L_{0}\|v_{0}\|^{\beta})-C_{4}\,(\mbox{\rm diam}(\widetilde{{\mathcal{C}}}))^{1+\tilde{\beta}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 10\delta_{0}\|u^{(1)}_{0}\|(2c_{0}-C_{0}L_{0}(10\delta_{0})^{\beta})-C_{4}\,(\mbox{\rm diam}(\widetilde{{\mathcal{C}}}))^{1+\tilde{\beta}}

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle 15c_{0}\delta_{0}\|u^{(1)}_{0}\|-C_{4}\,(\mbox{\rm diam}(\widetilde{{\mathcal{C}}}))^{1+\tilde{\beta}},

assuming
\delta_{0}>0
is sufficiently small so that
\displaystyle C_{0}L_{0}(10\delta_{0})^{\beta}<c_{0}/2
.
Since
\delta_{0}
,
c_{0}
and
C_{4}>0
are global constants, using (
4.5
) and assuming that
m_{0}\geq 1
is sufficiently large
and
m\geq m_{0}
we have

C_{4}\,(\mbox{\rm diam}(\widetilde{{\mathcal{C}}}))^{\tilde{\beta}}\leq C_{4}\left(\frac{C_{3}e^{m\tau_{0}\hat{\epsilon}_{7}}}{\lambda_{1}^{m\tilde{\tau}_{0}}}\right)^{\tilde{\beta}}\leq\frac{c_{0}\delta_{0}\,d_{2}}{8}e^{-m\hat{\epsilon}_{13}}=\frac{c_{0}\delta_{0}\,\kappa}{8},

choosing
\hat{\epsilon}_{13}\leq\mbox{\rm const }\hat{\epsilon}
. Thus,

C_{4}(\mbox{\rm diam}(\widetilde{{\mathcal{C}}}))^{1+\tilde{\beta}}\leq\frac{c_{0}\delta_{0}\,\kappa}{8}\,\mbox{\rm diam}(\widetilde{{\mathcal{C}}})\leq\frac{c_{0}\delta_{0}}{8}\,\|u^{(1)}_{0}\|.

(5.39)

This and the above estimates imply (
5.37
).

In a similar way, this time using (5.7), we get

\displaystyle|\Delta(x_{0},y_{0})|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle|d\omega_{z_{0}}(u^{(1)}_{0},v^{(1)}_{0})|+C_{0}L_{0}\,\|u^{(1)}_{0}\|\,\|v_{0}\|^{1+\beta}+C_{4}\,(\mbox{\rm diam}(\widetilde{{\mathcal{C}}}))^{1+\tilde{\beta}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C_{0}\,\|u^{(1)}_{0}\|\,\|v_{0}\|+C_{0}L_{0}\,\|u^{(1)}_{0}\|\,\|v_{0}\|^{1+\beta}+C_{4}\,(\mbox{\rm diam}(\widetilde{{\mathcal{C}}}))^{1+\tilde{\beta}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\|u^{(1)}_{0}\|\|v_{0}\|(C_{0}+C_{0}L_{0}\|v_{0}\|^{\tilde{\beta}})+C_{4}\,(\mbox{\rm diam}(\widetilde{{\mathcal{C}}}))^{1+\tilde{\beta}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 20\delta_{0}C_{0}\|u^{(1)}_{0}\|+C_{4}\,(\mbox{\rm diam}(\widetilde{{\mathcal{C}}}))^{1+\tilde{\beta}}.

We will use this with
y_{0}
replaced by
b_{1}\in W^{s}_{R}(z_{0})
with
d(z_{0},b_{1})<2\delta^{\prime}
for some small
\delta^{\prime}>0

(to be determined later). Let
b_{1}=\Phi^{s}_{z_{0}}(v_{1})
for some
v_{1}\in E^{s}(z_{0})
. Then
\|v_{1}\|\leq R_{0}d(z_{0},b_{1})<2R_{0}\delta^{\prime}

and (
5.39
) give

|\Delta(x_{0},b_{1})|\leq 2C_{0}R_{0}\delta^{\prime}\|u^{(1)}_{0}\|+\frac{c_{0}\delta_{0}}{8}\|u^{(1)}_{0}\|\leq\frac{c_{0}\delta_{0}}{4}\,\|u^{(1)}_{0}\|,

(5.40)

assuming that

4C_{0}R_{0}\delta^{\prime}\leq c_{0}\delta_{0}/8
.
For this and later use define

\delta^{\prime}=\frac{c_{0}\delta_{0}}{64C_{0}L_{0}R_{0}}=\frac{c_{0}}{64C_{0}L_{0}R_{0}}\left(\frac{c_{0}}{32C_{0}L_{0}\Gamma_{0}}\right)^{1/\tilde{\beta}}.

(5.41)

Assume also that
b_{2}\in W^{s}_{R}(z_{0})
with
d(y_{0},b_{2})<2\delta^{\prime}
. Using (5.6) with
b_{0}=b_{2}
, so that

\|v_{0}-\eta_{0}\|\leq R_{0}\,d(y_{0},b_{2})<2R_{0}\delta^{\prime}
, we get

\displaystyle|\Delta(x_{0},y_{0})-\Delta(x_{0},b_{2})|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\|u^{(1)}_{0}\|2R_{0}\delta^{\prime}(C_{0}+2C_{0}L_{0})+C_{4}\,(\mbox{\rm diam}(\widetilde{{\mathcal{C}}}))^{1+\tilde{\beta}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\|u^{(1)}_{0}\|2\delta^{\prime}3R_{0}C_{0}L_{0}+\frac{c_{0}\delta_{0}}{8}\,\|u^{(1)}_{0}\|\leq\frac{c_{0}\delta_{0}}{4}\,\|u^{(1)}_{0}\|,

using the choice of
\delta^{\prime}
in (
5.41
).

Now (
5.37
) and the above two estimates yield

\displaystyle|\Delta(x_{0},b_{1})-\Delta(x_{0},b_{2})|

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle|\Delta(x_{0},y_{0})|-|\Delta(x_{0},y_{0})-\Delta(x_{0},b_{2})|-|\Delta(x_{0},b_{1})|

(5.42)

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle 14c_{0}\delta_{0}\,\|u^{(1)}_{0}\|-c_{0}\delta_{0}\,\|u^{(1)}_{0}\|=13c_{0}\delta_{0}\,\|u^{(1)}_{0}\|,

that is (
5.33
) holds.
This proves part (a).

(b) As in Sect. 6 in
[
D1
]
, we will use the fact that
\widetilde{{\mathcal{P}}}^{N}(U)
fills in
\widetilde{R}
densely as
N\to\infty
.

Let
\delta_{0}>0
and
\delta^{\prime}>0
be as in part (a).
Take the integer
\tilde{m}_{0}\geq 1
so large that for every unstable
cylinder
\widetilde{{\mathcal{C}}}
in
\widetilde{R}
of length
m\geq\tilde{m}_{0}
we have
\mbox{\rm diam}(\widetilde{{\mathcal{C}}})<\epsilon^{\prime}=\frac{\epsilon_{1}}{2\tilde{c}_{2}}
; see (4.1).

Using the symbolic coding provided by the Markov family
\{\widetilde{R}_{i}\}
it is easy to see that
there exists an integer
N_{0}\geq 1
such that for any integer
N\geq N_{0}
we have

\widetilde{{\mathcal{P}}}^{N}(B^{u}_{\epsilon^{\prime}}(z))\cap B^{s}(z^{\prime},\delta^{\prime})\neq\emptyset
for any
z,z^{\prime}\in\widetilde{R}
. We choose
N_{0}
such that

1/\gamma^{N_{0}}<\delta^{\prime}.

Let
\widetilde{{\mathcal{C}}}
be a cylinder of length
m\geq\tilde{m}_{0}
in
\widetilde{R}
and let
z_{0}\in\widetilde{{\mathcal{C}}}\cap\widetilde{P}_{0}
.
Let
x_{0}\in\widetilde{{\mathcal{C}}}
and
u_{0}\in E^{u}(z_{0})
satisfy (
5.31
), where
\kappa\in(0,1)
satisfies (
5.32
).
Define
N_{0}
as above, and let
N\geq N_{0}
.
Choose
y_{0}=y_{0}(z_{0},x_{0})\in B^{s}(z_{0},\epsilon_{1})

as in part (a).

It follows from the choice of
N
that for each
i=1,2
there exist

y_{1}(z_{0},x_{0})\in\widetilde{{\mathcal{P}}}^{N}(B^{u}(z_{0},\epsilon^{\prime}))\cap B^{s}(z_{0},\delta^{\prime})\>\>,\>\>y_{2}(z_{0},x_{0})\in\widetilde{{\mathcal{P}}}^{N}(B^{u}(z_{0},\epsilon^{\prime}))\cap B^{s}(y_{0},\delta^{\prime}).

Fix points
y_{1}=y_{1}(z_{0},x_{0})
,
y_{2}=y_{2}(z_{0},x_{0})
with these properties; these are then points in
W^{s}_{\epsilon_{1}}(z_{0})
.
Let
b_{1},b_{2}\in W^{s}_{\epsilon}(z_{0})
be so that
b_{1}\in B^{s}(y_{1},\delta^{\prime})
and
b_{2}\in B^{s}(y_{2},\delta^{\prime})
.
Then
b_{1}\in B^{s}(z_{0},2\delta^{\prime})
and
b_{2}\in B^{s}(y_{0},2\delta^{\prime})
, so (
5.33
) holds.

Assume the
integer
\tilde{m}_{0}\geq 1
is chosen so large that
for any
z\in R
and any
unstable cylinder
{\mathcal{C}}
of length
\geq\tilde{m}_{0}
in
R
we have
\mbox{\rm diam}(\widetilde{\Psi}({\mathcal{C}}))\leq r_{0}

and
\mbox{\rm diam}(\mathcal{T}_{z}({\mathcal{C}}))\leq r_{0}
for any
z\in{\mathcal{C}}
. We will use the constant
\delta_{0}
defined
by (
5.30
).

Fix a constant
d>1
such that

T=\frac{1}{2}\left(\frac{2d\beta}{\tau_{0}}-1\right)>\frac{1}{\tilde{\beta}}

(5.43)

is a very large constant, to be specified later,
where
\beta>0
is the constant from Sect. 5.1 and
\tilde{\beta}\in(0,\beta)
is the constant from Lemma 5.3.
As before we assume that
\beta>0
is sufficiently small so
that it satisfies the requirements of Lemma 5.1 and also those in Sect. 3. In what follows
we will use Lemma 4.3 with the choice of the constant
d
in (
5.43
).

Let the constants
\tilde{m}_{0}\geq 1
and
q_{1}\geq q_{0}
be as in Lemma 4.4.

As before, we choose
N_{0}
so that
1/\gamma^{N_{0}}<\delta^{\prime}.
Recall the constant
\alpha_{2}\in(0,1)

from Sect. 4.2 and
fix a constant
\alpha_{3}\in(0,\alpha_{2})
.

Lemma 5.5.

There exist global constants

d_{3}>1
,
d_{4}\in(0,1)
,
T>0
and
0<\hat{\epsilon}_{13}\leq\mbox{\rm const }\hat{\epsilon}
which can be made arbitrarily small with
\hat{\epsilon}
such that for
any point
z_{0}\in\widetilde{P}_{0}
and any cylinder
\widetilde{{\mathcal{C}}}
in
\widetilde{R}
of length
m\geq\tilde{m}_{0}
containing
z_{0}
there exist
sub-cylinders
\widetilde{\Gamma}
and
\widetilde{\Lambda}

of
\widetilde{{\mathcal{C}}}
having co-lengths
q_{0}
and
q_{1}
in
\widetilde{{\mathcal{C}}}
, respectively, so that
the following hold:

(a)
For every
x\in\widetilde{\Gamma}
, for
u=(\Phi^{u}_{z_{0}})^{-1}(\mathcal{T}_{z_{0}}(x))\in E^{u}(z_{0})
we have

\|u^{(1)}\|\geq\kappa\,\mbox{\rm diam}(\widetilde{{\mathcal{C}}}),

(5.44)

where

\kappa=d_{2}e^{-m\hat{\epsilon}_{13}}
.

(b)
For every constant
S=S(m)\geq 1
, possibly depending on the cylinder
\widetilde{{\mathcal{C}}}
,
there exist an integer
j_{0}=j_{0}(m)\geq 1
and finite families
\{\widetilde{\Gamma}_{j}\}_{j=1}^{j_{0}}
and

\{\widetilde{\Lambda}_{j}\}_{j=1}^{j_{0}}
of sub-cylinders of
\widetilde{{\mathcal{C}}}
such that

\cup_{j=1}^{j_{0}}\widetilde{\Gamma}_{j}\subset\widetilde{\Gamma}\hskip 10.00002pt,\hskip 10.00002pt\cup_{j=1}^{j_{0}}\widetilde{\Lambda}_{j}=\widetilde{\Lambda},

(5.45)

\displaystyle\mbox{\rm diam}(\widetilde{\Gamma}_{j})\leq\frac{1}{S}\mbox{\rm diam}(\widetilde{{\mathcal{C}}})\hskip 10.00002pt,\hskip 10.00002pt\mbox{\rm diam}(\widetilde{\Lambda}_{j})\leq\frac{1}{S}\mbox{\rm diam}(\widetilde{{\mathcal{C}}}),

and

\mbox{\rm diam}_{\theta}(\widetilde{\Gamma}_{j})\leq\frac{1}{\tilde{S}}((\mbox{\rm diam}_{\theta}(\widetilde{{\mathcal{C}}}))^{1/\alpha_{3}}\hskip 10.00002pt,\hskip 10.00002pt\mbox{\rm diam}_{\theta}(\widetilde{\Lambda}_{j})\leq\frac{1}{\tilde{S}}(\mbox{\rm diam}_{\theta}(\widetilde{{\mathcal{C}}}))^{1/\alpha_{3}},

(5.46)

for all
j=1,\ldots,j_{0}
, where

\tilde{S}=\frac{C_{1}^{1+1/\alpha_{3}}}{S^{\alpha_{2}}}.

We can choose all sub-cylinders
\widetilde{\Gamma}_{j}
and
\widetilde{\Lambda}_{j}
so that their lengths do not exceed
T\,m
and

\frac{1}{d_{3}}\leq\frac{\nu(\widetilde{\Gamma}^{\prime}_{j})}{\nu(\widetilde{\Lambda}^{\prime}_{j})}\leq d_{3}

(5.47)

for all
j=1,\ldots,j_{0}
, where
\widetilde{\Gamma}^{\prime}_{j}=\pi^{(U)}(\widetilde{\Gamma}_{j})
and
\widetilde{\Lambda}^{\prime}_{j}=\pi^{(U)}(\widetilde{\Lambda}_{j})
.

(c)
Moreover, for the sub-cylinders of
\widetilde{{\mathcal{C}}}
from parts (a) and (b),
any integer
N\geq N_{0}
, any
j=1,\ldots,j_{0}
and any
i=1,2
there exist a (Hölder) continuous map

B^{u}(z_{0},\epsilon^{\prime\prime})\ni x\mapsto v_{i,j}(z_{0},x)\in U,

such that
\sigma^{N}(v_{i,j}(z_{0},x))=x
for all
x\in B^{u}(z_{0},\epsilon^{\prime\prime})

and the following property holds:

I_{N}(x^{\prime},z^{\prime})=\left|\psi(z_{0},x^{\prime})-\psi(z_{0},z^{\prime})\right|\geq 6c_{0}\delta_{0}\,\kappa\,\mbox{\rm diam}(\widetilde{{\mathcal{C}}})

(5.48)

for all
z\in\widetilde{\Lambda}_{j}
and
x\in\widetilde{\Gamma}_{j}
, where
z^{\prime}=\pi^{(U)}(z)
,
x^{\prime}=\pi^{(U)}(x)\in U
, and

\psi(z_{0},x)=\tau_{N}(v_{1,j}(z_{0},x))-\tau_{N}(v_{2,j}(z_{0},x)).

Proof.

We will now use the results of Lemma 4.4 with the constant
d>1
as in (
5.43
) and some of the set-up in its proof.

Define the global constant
\delta^{\prime}\in(0,1)
by (
5.41
) and choose the global constant
N_{0}\geq 1
as in the proof of Lemma 5.4(b).

Take an arbitrary constant
C^{\prime}>8
,
and set

\displaystyle C=\frac{3C^{\prime}}{c_{0}\delta_{0}d_{2}}>2C^{\prime},

where
\delta_{0}>0
is as in (
5.30
) and
c_{0}>0
is as in Sect. 5.1.
We will use Lemma 4.4 with this particular
C
.

Fix for a moment
z_{0}\in\widetilde{P}_{0}
. Assume
z_{0}\in\widetilde{R}_{i_{0}}
.
Let
\widetilde{{\mathcal{C}}}
be a cylinder of length
m\geq m_{0}
with
z_{0}\in\widetilde{{\mathcal{C}}}
,
Z_{m}=\widetilde{{\mathcal{P}}}^{m}(z_{0})\in\widetilde{R}_{j_{0}}
for some
j_{0}
.

.

\|w^{(1)}_{p}\|\geq\frac{d_{0}\tilde{c}_{1}}{4C\gamma_{1}}e^{-4d(m-k)\hat{\epsilon}}.

(5.49)

It follows from (

) that
there exists

In most of what follows we will deal with the

As in the proof of Lemma 5.3 we will use the notation

x_{j}=\varphi^{j}(x_{0})\hskip 10.00002pt,\hskip 10.00002ptu_{j}=\hat{\varphi}^{j}_{z_{0}}(u_{0})\in E^{u}(z_{j})\hskip 10.00002pt,\hskip 10.00002pt\hat{u}_{j}=\hat{\varphi}^{j}_{z_{0}}(0)\cdot u_{0}\in E^{u}(z_{j})\hskip 10.00002pt,\hskip 10.00002ptv_{j}=\hat{\varphi}^{j}_{z_{0}}(v_{0})\in E^{s}(z_{j}).

Note.

It follows from

u_{p}=Cw_{p}\in H\setminus H_{2}
, the definition of
H_{2}
(see the proof of Lemma 4.4)
and
p=[\tilde{\tau}_{m-k}(z_{0})]\in[(m-k)\tilde{\tau}_{0},(m-k)\tau_{0}+1)
that

\|u_{p}\|\leq e^{-3d(m-k)\hat{\epsilon}}\leq e^{-3d(p-1)\hat{\epsilon}/\tau_{0}}\leq e^{-2dp\hat{\epsilon}/\tau_{0}}\hskip 10.00002pt,\hskip 10.00002pt\|u^{(1)}_{p}\|\geq\frac{d_{0}\tilde{c}_{1}}{4\gamma_{1}}e^{-4d(m-k)\hat{\epsilon}}\geq\frac{d_{0}\tilde{c}_{1}}{4\gamma_{1}}e^{-4dp\hat{\epsilon}/\tilde{\tau}_{0}},

(5.50)

assuming
p
is sufficiently large.
Similarly for
w
and
w_{p}
we have

\|w_{p}\|\leq\frac{1}{C}e^{-2dp\hat{\epsilon}/\tau_{0}}\hskip 10.00002pt,\hskip 10.00002pt\|w^{(1)}_{p}\|\geq\frac{d_{0}\tilde{c}_{1}}{4C\gamma_{1}}e^{-4dp\hat{\epsilon}/\tilde{\tau}_{0}}.

(5.51)

Next, it follows from Lemma 3.1 that

\|u^{(1)}_{p}-\hat{u}^{(1)}_{p}\|\leq L(z_{p})\|u_{p}\|^{1+\beta}\leq L_{0}e^{p\hat{\epsilon}}\|u_{p}\|^{1+\beta},

so
\|\hat{u}^{(1)}_{p}\|\leq\|u_{p}\|(1+L_{0}e^{p\hat{\epsilon}}\|u_{p}\|^{\beta})
. Now (5.50) and (
5.43
) yield

L_{0}e^{p\hat{\epsilon}}\|u_{p}\|^{\beta}\leq L_{0}e^{p\hat{\epsilon}}e^{-2d\beta p\hat{\epsilon}/\tau_{0}}\leq L_{0}e^{-p\hat{\epsilon}(2d\beta/\tau_{0}-1)}<L_{0}e^{-p\hat{\epsilon}}<1,

since
2d\beta/\tau_{0}-1>1
.
Thus,

\|\hat{u}^{(1)}_{p}\|\leq 2\|u_{p}\|\leq 2e^{-2dp\hat{\epsilon}/\tau_{0}}
, and

\|u^{(1)}_{p}-\hat{u}^{(1)}_{p}\|\leq L_{0}e^{-p\hat{\epsilon}(2d\beta/\tau_{0}-1)}=L_{0}e^{-2Tp\hat{\epsilon}}.

(5.52)

Similarly, again by Lemma 3.1,

\|w^{(1)}_{p}-\hat{w}^{(1)}_{p}\|\leq L(z_{p})\|w_{p}\|^{1+\beta}\leq L_{0}e^{p\hat{\epsilon}}\|w_{p}\|^{1+\beta}\leq\frac{L_{0}}{C}e^{-2Tp\hat{\epsilon}},

and as above we derive

\|\hat{w}^{(1)}_{p}\|\leq 2\|w^{(1)}_{p}\|\leq\frac{2}{C}e^{-2dp\hat{\epsilon}/\tau_{0}}.

Since
u_{p}=Cw_{p}
, it follows that
u^{(1)}_{p}=Cw^{(1)}_{p}
, therefore

\|\hat{u}^{(1)}_{p}-C\hat{w}^{(1)}_{p}\|\leq\|\hat{u}^{(1)}_{p}-u^{(1)}_{p}\|+\|u^{(1)}_{p}-Cw^{(1)}_{p}\|+\|Cw^{(1)}_{p}-\hat{w}^{(1)}_{p}\|=\|\hat{u}^{(1)}_{p}-u^{(1)}_{p}\|+\|Cw^{(1)}_{p}-C\hat{w}^{(1)}_{p}\|,

and therefore

\|\hat{u}^{(1)}_{p}-C\hat{w}^{(1)}_{p}\|\leq 2L_{0}e^{-2Tp\hat{\epsilon}}.

Notice that the above and (3.14) imply

\|u^{(1)}_{0}-Cw^{(1)}_{0}\|\leq\Gamma_{0}e^{p\hat{\epsilon}}\frac{2L_{0}e^{-2Tp\hat{\epsilon}}}{\mu_{1}^{p}}\leq\frac{e^{-Tp\hat{\epsilon}}}{\lambda_{1}^{p}},

(5.53)

assuming that
T
is sufficiently large.

We will now use (
5.53
) to get an estimate for
|\Delta(z,y_{0})|
by means of
\|u^{(1)}_{0}\|
.
It follows from (5.53), (5.7) with
x_{0}
replaced by
z
and
u^{(1)}_{0}
replaced by

that

\displaystyle|\Delta(z,y_{0})|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle|d\omega_{z_{0}}(w^{(1)}_{0},v^{(1)}_{0})|+C_{0}L_{0}\|w^{(1)}_{0}\|\,\|v_{0}\|^{1+\beta}+C_{4}\,(\mbox{\rm diam}(\widetilde{{\mathcal{C}}}))^{1+\tilde{\beta}}

(5.54)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{C}|d\omega_{z_{0}}(u^{(1)}_{0},v^{(1)}_{0})|+\frac{C_{0}L_{0}}{C}\|u^{(1)}_{0}\|\,\|v_{0}\|^{1+\beta}+C_{4}\,(\mbox{\rm diam}(\widetilde{{\mathcal{C}}}))^{1+\tilde{\beta}}

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{C}\frac{e^{-Tp\hat{\epsilon}}}{\lambda_{1}^{p}}+\frac{C_{0}L_{0}}{C}\frac{e^{-Tp\hat{\epsilon}}}{\lambda_{1}^{p}}.

Since

satisfies (
5.44
), as in the proof of Lemma 5.4 we have (

(5.55)

For the second term in the right-hand-side of (5.54), it follows from (

(5.56)

Next, notice that if we choose
T>1
sufficiently large, then

4C_{0}L_{0}\Gamma_{0}\frac{e^{-Tp\hat{\epsilon}}}{\lambda_{1}^{p}}<\frac{c_{0}\delta_{0}}{8}\,\kappa\,\mbox{\rm diam}(\widetilde{{\mathcal{C}}})\leq\frac{c_{0}\delta_{0}}{8}\,\|u^{(1)}_{0}\|.

(5.57)

Indeed, using

\kappa=d_{2}e^{-m\hat{\epsilon}_{13}}\geq d_{2}e^{-2p\tau_{0}\hat{\epsilon}_{13}}.

Now it follows from (
4.5
) that

\displaystyle\frac{c_{0}\delta_{0}}{8}\,\kappa\,\mbox{\rm diam}(\widetilde{{\mathcal{C}}})

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle c_{0}\delta_{0}\,d_{2}e^{-2p\tau_{0}\hat{\epsilon}_{13}}\frac{e^{-q\hat{\epsilon}_{7}}}{8C_{3}\lambda_{1}^{q}}>c_{0}\delta_{0}\,d_{2}e^{-2p\tau_{0}\hat{\epsilon}_{13}}\frac{e^{-2p\hat{\epsilon}_{7}}}{8C_{3}\lambda_{1}^{p/(1-\hat{\epsilon}^{\prime}_{6})}}

(5.58)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle c_{0}\delta_{0}\,d_{2}\frac{e^{-2p(\tau_{0}\hat{\epsilon}_{13}+\hat{\epsilon}_{7})}\lambda_{1}^{-p\hat{\epsilon}^{\prime}_{6}/(1-\hat{\epsilon}^{\prime}_{6})}}{8C_{3}\lambda_{1}^{p}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle c_{0}\delta_{0}\,d_{2}\frac{e^{-p(2\tau_{0}\hat{\epsilon}_{13}+2\hat{\epsilon}_{7}+\hat{\epsilon}^{\prime}_{6}(\log\lambda_{1})/(1-\hat{\epsilon}^{\prime}_{6}))}}{8C_{3}\lambda_{1}^{p}}>4C_{0}L_{0}\Gamma_{0}\frac{e^{-Tp\hat{\epsilon}}}{\lambda_{1}^{p}},

assuming e.g. that

and

T\hat{\epsilon}/2>2\tau_{0}\hat{\epsilon}_{13}+2\hat{\epsilon}_{7}+\hat{\epsilon}^{\prime}_{6}(\log\lambda_{1})/(1-\hat{\epsilon}^{\prime}_{6})
, which will be satisfied if we take

sufficiently large.
Thus, (5.57) holds.

Combining (5.54), (5.55), (5.56) and (5.57) implies

assuming e.g. that

.

assuming

This, (5.59) and (5.60) now imply

Next, we will get estimates similar to (5.42) and (
5.61
) replacing
11
11

11

We avoid here the notation

and

.

x
by

Recall that the function

and

We

with this property
so that

Fix for a moment a small constant

(we will determine later how small) and assume

.
It follows from (
4.5
) that we have the following estimate (in terms of

\displaystyle\kappa\,\mbox{\rm diam}(\widetilde{{\mathcal{C}}})\geq d_{2}e^{-m\hat{\epsilon}_{13}}\frac{e^{-m\hat{\epsilon}_{7}/\tilde{\tau}_{0}}}{C_{3}\lambda_{1}^{m\tau_{0}}}=\frac{d_{2}}{C_{3}}\,e^{-m(\hat{\epsilon}_{13}+\hat{\epsilon}_{7}/\tilde{\tau}_{0}+\tau_{0}\log\lambda_{1})}\geq\frac{d_{2}}{C_{3}}\,e^{-m(1+1/\tilde{\tau}_{0}+\log\lambda_{1})},
