Assume now that
S=S(m)\geq 1
is a given constant.

We will determine
\delta
so that

\displaystyle\frac{c_{0}\delta_{0}\,\kappa}{S}\,\mbox{\rm diam}(\widetilde{{\mathcal{C}}})\geq 8D^{\prime}_{3}\delta^{\alpha},

that is

\delta\leq\left(\frac{c_{0}\delta_{0}}{8\,S\,D^{\prime}_{3}}\kappa\,\mbox{\rm diam}(\widetilde{{\mathcal{C}}})\right)^{1/\alpha},

(5.62)

for which it is enough to have

\displaystyle\delta\leq D^{\prime\prime}_{3}\,e^{-m(1+1/\tilde{\tau}_{0}+\log\lambda_{1})/\alpha}
,
where
D^{\prime\prime}_{3}=\left(\frac{c_{0}\delta_{0}d_{2}}{8S\,D^{\prime}_{3}\,C_{3}}\right)^{1/\alpha}>0
is a global constant.
It follows from Lemma 4.1(c) that the sub-cylinders
\widetilde{\Gamma}(x)
and
\widetilde{\Lambda}(z)

of
\widetilde{{\mathcal{C}}}
, containing
x
and
z
, respectively, and having lengths not less than

\tilde{n}=\frac{1}{|\log\theta|}(-\log\delta+\log C_{2})

have diameters not exceeding
\delta
. Indeed, if
\widetilde{X}
is a cylinder in
\widetilde{{\mathcal{C}}}
of length

, then by
Lemma 4.2(c),

\mbox{\rm diam}(\widetilde{X})\leq C_{2}\,\mbox{\rm diam}_{\theta}(\widetilde{X})=C_{2}\theta^{n}=C_{2}e^{-n|\log\theta|}\leq C_{2}e^{\log\delta-\log C_{2}}=\delta.

Since

-\log\delta\geq m(1+1/\tilde{\tau}_{0}+\log\lambda_{1})/\alpha-\log D^{\prime\prime}_{3},

it is enough to have
\tilde{n}\geq r^{\prime}_{m}=T^{\prime}_{1}m+T^{\prime}_{2},

where
T^{\prime}_{1}=\frac{1}{\alpha|\log\theta|}(1+1/\tilde{\tau}_{0}+\log\lambda_{1})>0
and
T^{\prime}_{2}=\frac{\log C_{2}}{|\log\theta|}
.

In this way we have shown that if the sub-cylinders
\widetilde{\Gamma}(x)
and
\widetilde{\Lambda}(z)

of
\widetilde{{\mathcal{C}}}
, containing
x
and
z
, respectively, having lengths
\geq r^{\prime}_{m}
, then they have diameters not exceeding
the number
\delta
satisfying (
5.62
). Since

, and

for sufficiently large

,

5.62
) implies

and similarly
\mbox{\rm diam}(\widetilde{\Lambda}(z))\leq\frac{1}{S}(\mbox{\rm diam}(\widetilde{{\mathcal{C}}}))^{1/(\alpha_{2}\alpha_{3})}\leq\frac{1}{S}\mbox{\rm diam}(\widetilde{{\mathcal{C}}})
.
Moreover,
it follows from Lemma 4.1 and the above that

\mbox{\rm diam}_{\theta}(\widetilde{\Gamma}(x))\leq C_{1}\,(\mbox{\rm diam}(\widetilde{\Gamma}(x))^{\alpha_{2}}\leq\frac{C_{1}}{S^{\alpha_{2}}}(\mbox{\rm diam}(\widetilde{{\mathcal{C}}}))^{1/\alpha_{3}}\leq\frac{C_{1}}{S^{\alpha_{2}}}(C_{1}\,\mbox{\rm diam}_{\theta}(\widetilde{{\mathcal{C}}}))^{1/\alpha_{3}}\leq\frac{1}{\tilde{S}}(\mbox{\rm diam}_{\theta}(\widetilde{{\mathcal{C}}}))^{1/\alpha_{3}},

where
\tilde{S}=\frac{C_{1}^{1+1/\alpha_{3}}}{S^{\alpha_{2}}}
. A similar estimate holds for
\widetilde{\Lambda}(z)
, so

\mbox{\rm diam}_{\theta}(\widetilde{\Gamma}(x))\leq\frac{1}{\tilde{S}}(\mbox{\rm diam}_{\theta}(\widetilde{{\mathcal{C}}}))^{1/\alpha_{3}}\hskip 10.00002pt,\hskip 10.00002pt\mbox{\rm diam}(\widetilde{\Lambda}(z))\leq\frac{1}{\tilde{S}}(\mbox{\rm diam}_{\theta}(\widetilde{{\mathcal{C}}}))^{1/\alpha_{3}}.

(5.63)

With such a choice of
\widetilde{\Gamma}(x)
and
\widetilde{\Lambda}(z)
it follows from (
5.61
) that
for any
b_{1},b_{2}\in W^{s}_{R}(z_{0})
with

(5.64)

for all
z^{\prime\prime}\in\Lambda(z)
. Indeed, by the choice of
\delta
, for every
z^{\prime\prime}\in\widetilde{\Lambda}(z)
we have

|\Delta(z^{\prime\prime},b_{1})-\Delta(z,b_{1})|\leq D^{\prime}_{3}\delta^{\alpha}\hskip 10.00002pt,\hskip 10.00002pt|\Delta(z^{\prime\prime},b_{2})-\Delta(z,b_{2})|\leq D^{\prime}_{3}\delta^{\alpha},

and now (
5.61
) implies

|\Delta(z^{\prime\prime},b_{1})-\Delta(z^{\prime\prime},b_{2})|\leq|\Delta(z,b_{1})-\Delta(z,b_{2})|+2D^{\prime}_{3}\delta^{\alpha}\leq 2c_{0}\delta_{0}\|u^{(1)}_{0}\|+\frac{1}{4}c_{0}\delta_{0}\,\kappa\,\mbox{\rm diam}(\widetilde{{\mathcal{C}}})\leq 3c_{0}\delta_{0}\|u^{(1)}_{0}\|.

In a similar way, for
b_{1},b_{2}
as above, we can use (5.42) to derive

|\Delta(x^{\prime\prime},b_{1})-\Delta(x^{\prime\prime},b_{2})|\geq 12c_{0}\delta_{0}\,\|u^{(1)}_{0}\|

(5.65)

for all
x^{\prime\prime}\in\Gamma(x)
. Indeed, using (5.42) with
x_{0}
replaced by
x
and

|\Delta(x^{\prime\prime},b_{1})-\Delta(x,b_{1})|\leq D^{\prime}_{3}\delta^{\alpha}\hskip 10.00002pt,\hskip 10.00002pt|\Delta(x^{\prime\prime},b_{2})-\Delta(x,b_{2})|\leq D^{\prime}_{3}\delta^{\alpha},

we get

|\Delta(x^{\prime\prime},b_{1})-\Delta(x^{\prime\prime},b_{2})|\geq|\Delta(x,b_{1})-\Delta(x,b_{2})|-2D^{\prime}_{3}\delta^{\alpha}\geq 13c_{0}\delta_{0}\,\|u^{(1)}_{0}\|-\frac{1}{4}c_{0}\delta_{0}\,\kappa\,\mbox{\rm diam}(\widetilde{{\mathcal{C}}})\geq 12c_{0}\delta_{0}\,\|u^{(1)}_{0}\|.

We will now use the above as follows. Take an arbitrary
\hat{z}\in\widetilde{\Lambda}
and fix it.

Using the above with
z=\hat{z}
, we construct a corresponding
x_{0}=x_{0}(\hat{z})\in\widetilde{\Gamma}
as follows: define

w_{0}=(\Phi^{u}_{z_{0}})^{-1}(\mathcal{T}_{z_{0}}(\hat{z}))
, then
w_{p}=\hat{\varphi}^{p}_{z_{0}}(w_{0})\in E^{u}(z_{p})
. This defines

u_{p}=Cw_{p}=\hat{\varphi}^{p}_{z_{0}}(u_{0})
for some
u_{0}\in E^{u}(z_{0})
. Then define
x_{0}=x_{0}(\hat{z})=(\mathcal{T}_{z_{0}})^{-1}(\Phi^{u}_{z_{0}}(u_{0}))

so that
u_{0}=(\Phi^{u}_{z_{0}})^{-1}(\mathcal{T}_{z_{0}}(x_{0}))
.

Next, we will use a construction from the proof of Lemma 5.4.
Take a unit vector
\tilde{v}\in E^{s}_{1}(z_{0})
with
d\omega_{z_{0}}(u^{(1)}_{0},\tilde{v})\geq 2c_{0}\|u^{(1)}_{0}\|
, and then define
v_{0}\in E^{s}_{1}(z_{0})
and

y_{0}=\Phi^{s}_{z_{0}}(v_{0})\in W^{s}_{\epsilon_{1}}(z_{0})
as in (
5.36
).
Then (
5.33
) holds, that is there exist a point
y_{0}=y_{0}(z_{0},x_{0})\in B^{s}(z_{0},\epsilon_{1})
such that we have

|\Delta(x_{0},b_{1})-\Delta(x_{0},b_{2})|\geq 12c_{0}\delta_{0}\,\|u^{(1)}_{0}\|\geq 12c_{0}\delta_{0}\,\kappa\,\mbox{\rm diam}(\widetilde{{\mathcal{C}}})

(5.66)

for any
b_{1},b_{2}\in W^{s}_{R}(z_{0})
with
d(z_{0},b_{1})<2\delta^{\prime}
and
d(y_{0},b_{2})<2\delta^{\prime}
.

Similarly, (
5.61
) holds with
z
replaced by
\hat{z}
for such
b_{1},b_{2}
.

Next, fix a large integer
\tilde{q}\geq 1
.
We now choose a sub-cylinder
\widetilde{\Gamma}(x_{0})
of
\widetilde{{\mathcal{C}}}
of co-length
\leq\tilde{q}
with
x_{0}\in\widetilde{\Gamma}(x_{0})
and a
sub-cylinder
\widetilde{\Lambda}(\hat{z})
of
\widetilde{{\mathcal{C}}}
of co-length
\leq\tilde{q}
with
\hat{z}\in\widetilde{\Lambda}(\hat{z})

both of length
r^{\prime}_{m}
so that for all
b_{1},b_{2}\in W^{s}_{R}(z_{0})
with
d(z_{0},b_{1})<\delta^{\prime}
and
d(y_{0},b_{2})<\delta^{\prime}
,
(5.64) holds with
z^{\prime\prime}
replaced by any
z\in\widetilde{\Lambda}(\hat{z})
and
(5.65) holds with
x^{\prime\prime}
replaced by any
x\in\widetilde{\Gamma}(x_{0})
. It then follows that, for such
b_{1},b_{2}
, we have

\displaystyle\big|\,\left|\Delta(x,b_{1})-\Delta(x,b_{2})\right|-\left|\Delta(z,b_{1})-\Delta(z,b_{2})\right|\,\big|

(5.67)

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle 12c_{0}\delta_{0}\,\|u^{(1)}_{0}\|-3c_{0}\delta_{0}\,\kappa\,\mbox{\rm diam}(\widetilde{{\mathcal{C}}})\geq 9c_{0}\delta_{0}\,\|u^{(1)}_{0}\|\geq 9c_{0}\delta_{0}\,\kappa\,\mbox{\rm diam}(\widetilde{{\mathcal{C}}})

for all
x\in\widetilde{\Gamma}(x_{0})
and all
z\in\widetilde{\Lambda}(\hat{z})
.
Notice that we now no longer require that the sub-cylinders

\widetilde{\Gamma}(x_{0})
and
\widetilde{\Lambda}(\hat{z})
are related by
C\hat{\varphi}^{p}_{z_{0}}(F^{(0)}(\widetilde{\Lambda}(\hat{z})))\subset F^{(p)}(\widetilde{\Gamma}(x_{0})
;

only the ”central points
x_{0}
and
\hat{z}
satisfy
C(F^{(0)}(\hat{z}))=x_{0}
. On the rest of the sub-cylinders
\widetilde{\Gamma}(x_{0})

and
\widetilde{\Lambda}(\hat{z})
we just use the fact that each of them has diameter
<\delta
.

We can now apply the above construction to the whole set
\widetilde{\Lambda}
obtained from Lemma 4.4,
which itself is a union of sub-cylinders of
\widetilde{{\mathcal{C}}}
. Given an arbitrary
\hat{z}\in\widetilde{\Lambda}
take
x=x(\hat{z})\in\widetilde{\Gamma}

so that
C(F^{(0)}(\hat{z}))=x(\hat{z})
, and then consider sub-cylinders
\widetilde{\Lambda}(\hat{z})

of
\widetilde{{\mathcal{C}}}
of co-length
\leq\tilde{q}
and total length
\geq r^{\prime}_{m}
and a corresponding sub-cylinder
\widetilde{\Gamma}(x(\hat{z}))

of
\widetilde{{\mathcal{C}}}
of co-length
\leq\tilde{q}
and total length
\geq r^{\prime}_{m}
contained in
\widetilde{\Gamma}
such that

C\hat{\varphi}^{p}_{z_{0}}(F^{(0)}(\widetilde{\Lambda}(\hat{z})))\subset F^{(p)}(\widetilde{\Gamma}(x(\hat{z})))

and so that the following condition is satisfied:

Binding condition:

there exist a point
y=y(\hat{z})\in B^{s}(z_{0},\epsilon_{1})
such that
for all
b_{1},b_{2}\in W^{s}_{R}(z_{0})
with
d(z_{0},b_{1})<\delta^{\prime}
and
d(y,b_{2})<\delta^{\prime}
we have

\displaystyle\big|\,\left|\Delta(x,b_{1})-\Delta(x,b_{2})\right|-\left|\Delta(z,b_{1})-\Delta(z,b_{2})\right|\,\big|\geq 9c_{0}\delta_{0}\,\kappa\,\mbox{\rm diam}(\widetilde{{\mathcal{C}}})

(5.68)

for all
x\in\widetilde{\Gamma}(x(\hat{z}))
and all
z\in\widetilde{\Lambda}(\hat{z})
.

Let us make the obvious remark that if
\widetilde{\Gamma}(x(\hat{z}))
and
\widetilde{\Lambda}(\hat{z})
are as above, then
for any sub-cylinder
\widetilde{\Gamma}_{1}
of
\widetilde{\Gamma}(x(\hat{z}))
and any sub-cylinder
\widetilde{\Lambda}_{1}
of
\widetilde{\Lambda}(\hat{z})
,
(
5.68
) holds for all
z\in\widetilde{\Lambda}_{1}
and all
x\in\widetilde{\Gamma}_{1}
.

In general
\nu(\widehat{\Lambda}(\hat{z}))
and
\nu(\widehat{\Gamma}(x(z)))
will not be the same. We apply the following

Procedure:

There are two possible cases to consider.

Case 1.

\nu(\widetilde{\Lambda}^{\prime}(\hat{z}))\leq\nu(\widetilde{\Gamma}^{\prime}(x(z)))
, where
\widetilde{\Lambda}^{\prime}(\hat{z})=\pi^{(U)}(\widetilde{\Lambda}(\hat{z}))
and

\widetilde{\Gamma}^{\prime}(x(z))=\pi^{(U)}(\widetilde{\Gamma}(x(z)))
.
Denote by
\widetilde{\Gamma}_{1}(x(z))
the sub-cylinder of
\widetilde{\Gamma}(x(z))
containing
x(z)
and
having maximal possible length so that
\nu(\widetilde{\Lambda}^{\prime}(\hat{z}))\leq\nu(\widetilde{\Gamma}^{\prime}_{1}(x(z)))
.
If
t
is the length of
\widetilde{\Gamma}_{1}(x(z))
, then its sub-cylinder

\widetilde{\Gamma}_{2}(x(z))
of length
t+1
containing
x(z)
satisfies
\nu(\widetilde{\Gamma}^{\prime}_{2}(x(z)))<\nu(\widetilde{\Lambda}^{\prime}(\hat{z}))
,
so using the projection of these cylinders to
U
via
\pi^{(U)}
and the point
x^{\prime}=\pi^{(U)}(x(z))
, (4.2) implies

1\leq\frac{\nu(\widetilde{\Gamma}^{\prime}_{1}(x(z)))}{\nu(\widetilde{\Gamma}^{\prime}_{2}(x(z)))}\leq\frac{c_{2}}{c_{1}}\frac{e^{g_{t}(x^{\prime})}}{e^{g_{t+1}(x^{\prime})}}=\frac{c_{2}}{c_{1}}\frac{1}{e^{g(\sigma(x^{\prime}))}}\leq d_{3}

for some global constant
d_{3}>1
.
Moreover it is easy to see, using (4.2), the fact that
\widetilde{\Gamma}^{\prime}_{1}(x(z))
has maximal
possible length with
\nu(\widetilde{\Lambda}^{\prime}(\hat{z}))\leq\nu(\widetilde{\Gamma}^{\prime}_{1}(x(z)))
, and the fact that
the length of
\widetilde{\Lambda}(\hat{z})
is
r^{\prime}_{m}=T^{\prime}_{1}m+T^{\prime}_{2}
, that the length
t

of
\widetilde{\Gamma}_{1}(x(z))
is still bounded above by
r_{m}=T_{1}m+T_{2}
for some global constants
T_{1},T_{2}>0
.
We now replace
\widetilde{\Gamma}(x(z))
by
\widetilde{\Gamma}_{1}(x(z))
.
Then the pair of cylinders
(\widetilde{\Lambda}(\hat{z}),\widetilde{\Gamma}_{1}(x(z)))
satisfies

\frac{1}{d_{3}}<1\leq\frac{\nu(\widetilde{\Gamma}^{\prime}_{1}(x(z)))}{\nu(\Lambda^{\prime}(\hat{z}))}\leq d_{3}.

As mentioned above, (
5.68
) clearly holds for all
z\in\widetilde{\Lambda}(\hat{z})
and all
x\in\widetilde{\Gamma}_{1}(x(z))
.

Case 2.

\nu(\widetilde{\Lambda}^{\prime}(\hat{z}))>\nu(\widetilde{\Gamma}^{\prime}(x(z)))
. Repeating the argument in Case 1 above, changing the roles of
\widetilde{\Lambda}(\hat{z})

and
\widetilde{\Gamma}(x(z))
, we construct a sub-cylinder
\widetilde{\Lambda}_{1}(\hat{z})
of
\widetilde{\Lambda}(\hat{z})
containing
\hat{z}
of length not exceeding
r_{m}

and such that

\frac{1}{d_{3}}<1\leq\frac{\nu(\widetilde{\Lambda}^{\prime}_{1}(\hat{z}))}{\nu(\widetilde{\Gamma}^{\prime}(x(z)))}\leq d_{3}.

We then replace
\widetilde{\Lambda}(\hat{z})
by
\widetilde{\Lambda}_{1}(\hat{z})
, and consider the pair
\widetilde{\Lambda}_{1}(\hat{z})
,
\widetilde{\Gamma}(x(z))
.
Again, (
5.68
) holds for all
z\in\widetilde{\Lambda}_{1}(\hat{z})
and all
x\in\widetilde{\Gamma}(x(z))
.

Thus, reducing either
\widetilde{\Lambda}(\hat{z})
or
\widetilde{\Gamma}(x(\hat{z})
we get sub-cylinders of
\widetilde{{\mathcal{C}}}
of
of co-length
\leq\tilde{q}
and total length
\geq r^{\prime}_{m}
, containing
\hat{z}
and
x(\hat{z})
, respectively,
have total length
\geq r^{\prime}_{m}
, satisfy the Binding condition and (5.47) as well for some global constant
d_{3}>1
.

Applying the above Procedure to each of the initial pairs
(\widetilde{\Lambda}(\hat{z}),\widetilde{\Gamma}(x(z)))
of cylinders in
\widetilde{\Lambda}
and
\widetilde{\Gamma}
,
we construct families
\widetilde{\Lambda}_{j}=\widetilde{\Lambda}_{j}(z_{0}^{(j)})
,
\widetilde{\Gamma}_{j}=\widetilde{\Gamma}_{j}(x_{0}^{(j)})
(
1\leq j\leq j_{0}
)
of sub-cylinders of
\widetilde{{\mathcal{C}}}_{m}
of lengths
\leq r_{m}
, so that

\cup_{j=1}^{j_{0}}\widetilde{\Lambda}_{j}(z_{0}^{(j)})=\widetilde{\Lambda}\hskip 10.00002pt,\hskip 10.00002pt\cup_{j=1}^{j_{0}}\widetilde{\Gamma}_{j}(x_{0}^{(j)})\subset\widetilde{\Gamma},

where
\widetilde{\Lambda}
is exactly the initially constructed
\widetilde{\Lambda}
. In general the construction will only produce
sub-cylinders
\widetilde{\Gamma}_{j}(x_{0}^{(j)})
whose union is contained in
the initially constructed
\widetilde{\Gamma}
. Importantly, for all
j=1,\ldots,j_{0}

there exists
y_{0}^{(j)}=y_{0}^{(j)}(z_{0}^{(j)},x_{0}^{(j)})\in B^{s}(z_{0}^{(j)},\epsilon_{1})
such that
the relation (5.67) holds for all
x\in\widetilde{\Gamma}_{j}(x_{0}^{(0)})
, all
z\in\widetilde{\Lambda}_{j}(z_{0}^{(j)})
, and all

b_{1},b_{2}\in W^{s}_{R}(z_{0}^{(j)})
with
d(z_{0}^{(j)},b_{1})<\delta^{\prime}
and
d(y_{0}^{(j)},b_{2})<\delta^{\prime}
.
Moreover,

\displaystyle\frac{1}{d_{3}}\leq\frac{\nu(\widetilde{\Gamma}^{\prime}_{j})}{\nu(\widetilde{\Lambda}^{\prime}_{j})}\leq d_{3}

(5.69)

for all
j=1,\ldots,j_{0}
.

Since
\cup_{j=1}^{j_{0}}\widetilde{\Lambda}_{j}=\widetilde{\Lambda}
and
\widetilde{\Lambda}
is a sub-cylinder of
\widetilde{{\mathcal{C}}}_{m}
of co-length
q_{1}
,
there exists a global constant
d_{1}\in(0,1)
so that

\nu(\widetilde{\Lambda}^{\prime})\geq d_{1}\nu(\widetilde{{\mathcal{C}}}^{\prime}).

(See e.g. Remark 4(a) in Sect. 6.3 below.) It follows now from this and (5.69) that we have

\displaystyle\nu(\cup_{j=1}^{j_{0}}\widetilde{\Gamma}^{\prime}_{j})\geq\frac{d_{1}}{d_{3}}\nu(\widetilde{{\mathcal{C}}}^{\prime})
.
Thus, replacing the initial global constant
d_{1}>0
by
d_{4}=d_{1}/d_{3}
, we get

\nu(\cup_{j=1}^{j_{0}}\widetilde{\Gamma}^{\prime}_{j})\geq d_{4}\,\nu(\widetilde{{\mathcal{C}}}^{\prime})\hskip 10.00002pt,\hskip 10.00002pt\nu(\cup_{j=1}^{j_{0}}\widetilde{\Lambda}_{j}^{\prime})\geq d_{4}\,\nu(\widetilde{{\mathcal{C}}}^{\prime}).

In general, the number
j_{0}
will depend on the cylinder
\widetilde{{\mathcal{C}}}
. However
d_{3}>1
and
d_{4}\in(0,1)
are
global constants
,
independent of
m
,
\widetilde{{\mathcal{C}}}
and
z_{0}\in\widetilde{P}_{0}
.

Let
N\geq N_{0}
.
Consider now an arbitrary
j=1,2,\ldots,j_{0}
. As we remarked earlier, (5.67) holds for every
x\in\widetilde{\Gamma}_{j}
and every
z\in\widetilde{\Lambda}_{j}

for some choice of the point
y_{0}\in W^{s}_{\epsilon_{1}}(z_{0})
which we will now
denote by
y_{0}^{(j)}
.
As in Lemma 5.4, we construct corresponding points

y^{(j)}_{1}=y^{(j)}_{1}(z_{0},x_{0})\in\widetilde{{\mathcal{P}}}^{N}(B^{u}(z_{0},\epsilon^{\prime}))\cap B^{s}(z_{0},\delta^{\prime})\>\>,\>\>y^{(j)}_{2}=y^{(j)}_{2}(z_{0},x_{0})\in\widetilde{{\mathcal{P}}}^{N}(B^{u}(z_{0},\epsilon^{\prime}))\cap B^{s}(y_{0},\delta^{\prime}).

so that (5.33) holds
for any
b_{1},b_{2}\in W^{s}_{\widetilde{R}}(z_{0})
with
d(y^{(j)}_{1},b_{1})<\delta^{\prime}
and
d(y^{(j)}_{2},b_{2})<\delta^{\prime}
, where as in Lemma 5.4,
\delta^{\prime}
is
the constant given by (
5.41
).

Given
i=1,2
, there exists a cylinder
L^{(j)}_{i}=L^{(j)}_{i}(z_{0})
of length
N
in
W^{u}_{\widetilde{R}_{i_{0}}}(z_{0})
so that

\widetilde{{\mathcal{P}}}^{N}:L^{(j)}_{i}\longrightarrow W^{u}_{\widetilde{R}_{i_{0}}}(y^{(j)}_{i})

is a bijection; then it is a bi-Hölder homeomorphism. Consider its inverse and its Hölder continuous
extension

\widetilde{{\mathcal{P}}}^{-N}:W^{u}_{\widetilde{R}_{i_{0}}}(y^{(j)}_{i})\longrightarrow L^{(j)}_{i}

and the cylinder

M^{(j)}_{i}=M^{(j)}_{i}(z_{0})=\pi^{(U)}(L^{(j)}_{i}(z_{0}))\subset U

of length
N
in
U_{i_{0}}
. Define the maps

\tilde{v}_{i,j}(z_{0},\cdot):U_{i_{0}}\longrightarrow L^{(j)}_{i}\subset B^{u}(z_{0},\epsilon^{\prime\prime})\hskip 10.00002pt,\hskip 10.00002ptv_{i,j}(z_{0},\cdot):U_{i_{0}}\longrightarrow M^{(j)}_{i}\subset U

by

\tilde{v}_{i,j}(z_{0},y)=\widetilde{{\mathcal{P}}}^{-N}(\phi_{\Delta(z_{0},y)}(\pi_{y^{(j)}_{i}}(y))\hskip 10.00002pt,\hskip 10.00002ptv_{i,j}(z_{0},y)=\pi^{(U)}(\tilde{v}_{i,j}(z_{0},y)).

Then

\widetilde{{\mathcal{P}}}^{N}(\tilde{v}_{i,j}(z_{0},y))=\phi_{\Delta(z_{0},y)}(\pi_{y^{(j)}_{i}}(y))=\phi_{[-\epsilon_{0},\epsilon_{0}]}(W^{s}_{\epsilon_{0}}(y))\cap W^{u}_{\widetilde{R}_{i_{0}}}(y^{(j)}_{i}),

and

\widetilde{{\mathcal{P}}}^{N}(v_{i,j}(z_{0},y))=\phi_{[-\epsilon_{0},\epsilon_{0}]}(W^{s}_{\epsilon_{0}}(y))\cap\widetilde{{\mathcal{P}}}^{N}(M^{(j)}_{i})\in W^{u}_{\widetilde{R}_{i_{0}}}(b^{(j)}_{i}),

(5.70)

where
b^{(j)}_{i}=b^{(j)}_{i}(z_{0})\in W^{s}_{R}(z_{0})
is such that
\widetilde{{\mathcal{P}}}^{N}(M^{(j)}_{i})=W^{u}_{\widetilde{R}_{i_{0}}}(b^{(j)}_{i})
.
Thus,
\sigma^{N}((v_{i,j}(z_{0},y))=y
.
Next, there exist
x^{\prime}\in M^{(j)}_{i}
and
y^{\prime}\in L^{(j)}_{i}
with
\pi^{(U)}(y^{\prime})=x^{\prime}
,
\widetilde{{\mathcal{P}}}^{N}(x^{\prime})=b^{(j)}_{i}
and

\widetilde{{\mathcal{P}}}^{N}(y^{\prime})=y^{(j)}_{i}
. Since stable leaves shrink exponentially fast, using (2.1) we get

\displaystyle d(b^{(j)}_{i},y^{(j)}_{i})\leq\frac{1}{c_{0}\gamma^{N}}d(x^{\prime},y^{\prime})\leq\frac{1}{\gamma^{N}}<\delta^{\prime}.

As in the proof of Lemma 5.4(b) we derive that
(5.67) holds for
x\in\widetilde{\Gamma}_{j}
with
b_{i}
replaced by
b^{(j)}_{i}
for
i=1,2
, that is

9c_{0}\delta_{0}\|u^{(1)}_{0}\|\leq|\Delta(x,b^{(j)}_{1})-\Delta(x,b^{(j)}_{2})|

(5.71)

for any
x\in\widetilde{\Gamma}_{j}
.

Set
z^{\prime}_{0}=\pi^{(U)}(z_{0})\in U_{i_{0}}
.
If
x,z\in\widetilde{{\mathcal{C}}}
, and
x^{\prime}=\pi^{(U)}(x)
,
z^{\prime}=\pi^{(U)}(z^{\prime})
, then

\displaystyle I_{N}(x^{\prime},z^{\prime})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle|[\tau_{N}(v_{1,j}(z_{0},x^{\prime}))-\tau_{N}(v_{2,j}(z_{0},x^{\prime}))]-[\tau_{N}(v_{1,j}(z_{0},z^{\prime}))-\tau_{N}(v_{2,j}(z_{0},z^{\prime}))]|

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left|\,\Delta({\mathcal{P}}^{N}(v_{1,j}(z_{0},x^{\prime})),{\mathcal{P}}^{N}(v_{1,j}(z_{0},z^{\prime})))-\Delta({\mathcal{P}}^{N}(v_{2,j}(z_{0},x^{\prime})),{\mathcal{P}}^{N}(v_{2,j}(z_{0},z^{\prime})))\,\right|

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left|\,\Delta(\pi_{b^{(j)}_{1}}(x^{\prime}),\pi_{b^{(j)}_{1}}(z^{\prime}))-\Delta(\pi_{b^{(j)}_{2}}(x^{\prime}),\pi_{b^{(j)}_{2}}(z^{\prime}))\,\right|

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left|\,\Delta(x^{\prime},\pi_{b^{(j)}_{1}}(z^{\prime}))-\Delta(x^{\prime},\pi_{b^{(j)}_{2}}(z^{\prime}))\,\right|.

In particular when
z=z_{0}
and
z^{\prime}=z^{\prime}_{0}
the latter gives

I_{N}(x^{\prime},z^{\prime}_{0})=\left|\,\Delta(x^{\prime},b^{(j)}_{1})-\Delta(x^{\prime},b^{(j)}_{2})\,\right|=\left|\,\Delta(x,b^{(j)}_{1})-\Delta(x,b^{(j)}_{2})\,\right|.

Since
\Delta(x,\pi_{y}(z))=\Delta(x,y)-\Delta(z,y)
for any
y\in W^{s}_{\epsilon}(z_{0})
, it follows from (5.71)
and (5.67)
that for any
x\in\widetilde{\Gamma}_{j}
and any
z\in\widetilde{\Lambda}_{j}
we have

\displaystyle I_{N}(x^{\prime},z^{\prime})

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\left|\,\left|\Delta(x,b^{(j)}_{1})-\Delta(x,b^{(j)}_{2})\right|-\left|\Delta(z,b^{(j)}_{1})-\Delta(z,b^{(j)}_{2})\right|\,\right|

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle 9c_{0}\delta_{0}\,\|u^{(1)}_{0}\|-3c_{0}\delta_{0}\|u^{(1)}_{0}\|\geq 6c_{0}\delta_{0}\|u^{(1)}_{0}\|\geq 6c_{0}\delta_{0}\kappa\,\mbox{\rm diam}(\widetilde{{\mathcal{C}}}).

This proves (5.48) and thus completes the proof of the lemma.

6
Contraction operators and their main properties

As in Sect. 5 here we assume that
M
is a
C^{2}
compact Riemannian manifold and
\phi_{t}
is
a
C^{2}
contact Anosov flow on
M
.

We will use the notation in Sects. 2, 3 and 4. In particular,
{\mathcal{R}}=\{R_{i}\}_{i=1}^{k_{0}}
will be a fixed
pseudo-Markov family for the flow and
\widetilde{{\mathcal{R}}}
will be the related Markov family as in Sect. 2.

As in Sect. 4,
we assume that
\hat{\epsilon}>0
is a small constant as in Sect. 3 (that can be taken smaller if necessary),
and we will again assume that
R(x)
,
\Gamma(x)
,
D(x)
and
L(x)
are Lyapunov
\hat{\epsilon}
-regularity functions,
while
\hat{r}(x)
and
r(x)
are
\hat{\epsilon}
-slowly varying radius function so that they satisfy (3.7) – (3.14) and the
conclusions of Lemma 3.1 with
\hat{\epsilon}^{\prime}
replaced by
\hat{\epsilon}
.
Replacing
r(x)
with the smaller
\hat{\epsilon}
-regularity function
\hat{r}(x)
,
without loss of generality we will assume that the conclusions of Lemmas 3.1 and 3.2
hold with
\hat{r}(x)
replaced by
r(x)
.

6.1
Constructing families of cylinders covering the given Pesin set

As in Sect. 4.2, we will assume that
F_{0}:M\longrightarrow{\mathbb{R}}
is a
fixed Hölder continuous function
and

{\sf m}
is the Gibbs measure determined by
F_{0}
on
M
, while

\mu
is the related Gibbs measure on
R
with respect to the Poincar’e map
{\mathcal{P}}:R\longrightarrow R
.
We will identify
\mu
with a measure on
\widetilde{R}
so that
\widetilde{\Psi}:R\longrightarrow\widetilde{R}
is an isomorphism.
As in Sect. 4.2,
\nu
will be the Gibbs measure on
U
determined by the function
F_{0}
.

Let
P_{0}
be the Pesin set in
{\mathcal{L}}\cap R
fixed in Sect. 4.2 and let
K_{0}=\pi^{(U)}(P_{0})
. As in Sect.4.2, there exist constants

r_{0}>0
,
R_{0}>0
,
\Gamma_{0}>0
,
L_{0}>0
with
r(x)\geq r_{0}
and
R(x)\leq R_{0}
,
\Gamma(x)\leq\Gamma_{0}
,

L(x)\leq L_{0}
for all
x\in P_{0}
. Then
\widetilde{P}_{0}=\widetilde{\Psi}(P_{0})
is a Pesin set for
\widetilde{{\mathcal{P}}}
on
\widetilde{{\mathcal{R}}}
.
Replacing
r_{0}
with a smaller positive constant,
we may assume that for every
x\in\widetilde{P}_{0}\cap\widetilde{R}_{j}
for any rectangle
\widetilde{R}_{j}
in
\widetilde{{\mathcal{R}}}
we have
B^{u}(x,r_{0})\subset\mbox{\rm Int}(\widetilde{R}_{j})
.

Recall from Sect. 2 the constants
\alpha_{1}>0
and
\gamma_{1}>\gamma>1
.
We will assume that
\alpha_{2}\in(0,1)
and
\theta\in(0,1)
are so that

\frac{1}{\gamma^{\alpha_{1}\beta}}\leq\theta\leq\frac{1}{\gamma_{1}^{\alpha_{2}}}<1,

(6.1)

where
\beta\in(0,1)
is as in Lemma 5.3.
In particular
\alpha_{2}\in(0,1)
satisfies
\alpha_{2}\,\log\gamma_{1}\leq\alpha_{1}\beta\,\log\gamma
, i.e.

0<\alpha_{2}\leq\frac{\alpha_{1}\beta\,\log\gamma}{\log\gamma_{1}}.

We will also need the constant
\beta_{0}>0
such that
\theta=e^{-\beta_{0}}
.
and a constant
\alpha_{3}
with
0<\alpha_{3}<\alpha_{2}
so that

\theta_{1}=\theta^{1/\alpha_{3}}>\theta_{0},

(6.2)

the initially chosen small constant.

In what follows
b\in{\mathbb{R}}
will be such that
|b|>b_{0}
,
where
b_{0}>e
is a fixed large constant.
We will impose some conditions on
b_{0}
later.

Recall the constants
C_{3}>0
and
\hat{\epsilon}_{7}>0
from Lemma 4.3 and the constant
\hat{\epsilon}_{13}
from Lemma 5.5. Set

\hat{\epsilon}_{14}=\hat{\epsilon}_{7}+\hat{\epsilon}_{13}/\tilde{\tau}_{0}.

This is still a constant with
\hat{\epsilon}_{14}\leq\mbox{\rm const }\hat{\epsilon}
that can be made arbitrarily small with
\hat{\epsilon}
.
Assuming
\tilde{\tau}_{0}<1/2
, we have
\hat{\epsilon}_{14}>2\hat{\epsilon}_{13}
.

Let
\hat{q}\geq 1
be the smallest integer
so that

\displaystyle\frac{C_{3}e^{-2\hat{q}\hat{\epsilon}_{14}}}{\lambda_{1}^{\hat{q}}}\leq\frac{1}{|b|}.

Then

\displaystyle\frac{1}{|b|}<\frac{C_{3}e^{-2(\hat{q}-1)\hat{\epsilon}_{14}}}{\lambda_{1}^{\hat{q}-1}},

so

\displaystyle\frac{\epsilon^{\prime}_{2}}{|b|}<\frac{e^{-2\hat{q}\hat{\epsilon}_{14}}}{\lambda_{1}^{\hat{q}}},

for some constant
\epsilon^{\prime}_{2}=\frac{e^{-2\hat{\epsilon}_{14}}}{C_{3}\lambda_{1}}>0
. Thus, our choice of
\hat{q}
is so that

\frac{\epsilon^{\prime}_{2}}{|b|}<\frac{e^{-2\hat{q}\hat{\epsilon}_{14}}}{\lambda_{1}^{\hat{q}}}\leq\frac{\epsilon^{\prime}_{1}}{|b|}

(6.3)

for some constants
\epsilon^{\prime}_{1}=\frac{1}{C_{3}}>\epsilon^{\prime}_{2}>0
.
Notice that
\hat{q}
depends on
b
.

Next, for any
z\in\widetilde{P}_{0}
denote by
m=m(z)
the length of the cylinder
\widetilde{{\mathcal{Q}}}(z)
in
\widetilde{R}
containing
z
so that
\tilde{\tau}_{m}(z)\leq\hat{q}

and
m
is maximal with this property, i.e.
\hat{q}<\tilde{\tau}_{m+1}(z)
, so
\hat{q}<\tilde{\tau}_{m+1}(z)\leq\tilde{\tau}_{m}(z)+\tau_{0}
.
Thus,
\hat{q}<[\tilde{\tau}_{m}(z)]+1+\tau_{0}
, so

q(z)=[\tilde{\tau}_{m}(z)]\leq\hat{q}<q(z)+2.

Now
\tilde{\tau}_{m+1}(z)>\hat{q}
implies
(m+1)\tau_{0}>\hat{q}
.
Then
m\tilde{\tau}_{0}\leq\hat{q}\leq(m+1)\tau_{0}
, so

\hat{m}_{1}=[\hat{q}/\tau_{0}-1]\leq\hat{q}/\tau_{0}-1\leq m(z)\leq\hat{m}_{2}=[\hat{q}/\tilde{\tau}_{0}].

In what follows we will be considering cylinders in
\widetilde{R}
intersecting
\widetilde{P}_{0}
that have lengths in the interval
[\hat{m}_{1},\hat{m}_{2}]
.
Notice that
\hat{m}_{1}
and
\hat{m}_{2}
depend on
b
.

Given
z\in\widetilde{P}_{0}
, let again
\widetilde{{\mathcal{Q}}}(z)
be the cylinder of length
m(z)
in
\widetilde{R}
containing
z
.
Then (
6.3
), the formula for
q(z)
, (
4.5
) and
\hat{\epsilon}_{7}<\hat{\epsilon}_{14}
imply

\mbox{\rm diam}(\widetilde{{\mathcal{Q}}}(z))\geq\frac{e^{-q(z)\hat{\epsilon}_{7}}}{\lambda_{1}^{q(z)}}\geq\frac{e^{-\hat{q}\hat{\epsilon}_{14}}}{\lambda_{1}^{\hat{q}}}=e^{\hat{q}\hat{\epsilon}_{14}}\frac{e^{-2\hat{q}\hat{\epsilon}_{14}}}{\lambda_{1}^{\hat{q}}}\geq e^{\hat{q}\hat{\epsilon}_{14}}\,\frac{\epsilon^{\prime}_{2}}{|b|}\geq e^{q(z)\hat{\epsilon}_{14}}\,\frac{\epsilon^{\prime}_{2}}{|b|}.

Similarly, using (
6.3
) and (
4.5
) again, we get

\mbox{\rm diam}(\widetilde{{\mathcal{Q}}}(z))\leq\frac{C_{3}e^{q(z)\hat{\epsilon}_{7}}}{\lambda_{1}^{q(z)}}\leq\frac{C_{3}e^{\hat{q}\hat{\epsilon}_{14}}}{\lambda_{1}^{\hat{q}-2}}\leq C_{3}\lambda_{1}^{2}\,\frac{e^{-2\hat{q}\hat{\epsilon}_{14}}}{\lambda_{1}^{\hat{q}}}e^{3\hat{q}\hat{\epsilon}_{14}}\leq C_{3}\lambda^{2}_{1}\frac{\epsilon^{\prime}_{1}}{|b|}e^{3(q(z)+2)\hat{\epsilon}_{14}}\leq\frac{C^{\prime}_{5}e^{3q(z)\hat{\epsilon}_{14}}}{|b|},

where

C^{\prime}_{5}=C_{3}\lambda_{1}^{2}e^{6}>0

is a global constant.

Let
{\mathcal{Q}}^{\prime}(z)=\pi^{(U)}({\mathcal{Q}}(z))
be the corresponding projection of
{\mathcal{Q}}(z)
in
U
. Here
{\mathcal{Q}}(z)
is the cylinder in
R
corresponding
to
\widetilde{{\mathcal{Q}}}(z)
, i.e.
\widetilde{\Psi}({\mathcal{Q}}(z))=\widetilde{{\mathcal{Q}}}(z)
.
Choose a finite set of points
Z_{1},Z_{2},\ldots,Z_{k_{0}}
in
\widetilde{P}_{0}
such that the projections
{\mathcal{Q}}^{\prime}(Z_{k})
cover completely
K_{0}
.
If
{\mathcal{Q}}^{\prime}(Z_{k})
and
{\mathcal{Q}}^{\prime}(Z_{k^{\prime}})
have common interior points (in the topology of
U
), then one of these
cylinders contains the other. So, omitting some of the cylinders, we may assume the points
Z_{1},Z_{2},\ldots,Z_{k_{0}}
in
\widetilde{P}_{0}

are chosen so that
{\mathcal{Q}}^{\prime}(Z_{k})\cap{\mathcal{Q}}^{\prime}(Z_{k^{\prime}})\cap\widehat{U}=\emptyset
for all
k\neq k^{\prime}
, and we still have

K_{0}\subset\cup_{k=1}^{k_{0}}{\mathcal{Q}}^{\prime}(Z_{k})
.
Then for each
k=1,2,\ldots,k_{0}
,

\widetilde{{\mathcal{Q}}}_{k}=\widetilde{{\mathcal{Q}}}(Z_{k})
is a cylinder in
\widetilde{R}
containing
Z_{k}\in\widetilde{P}_{0}
with
{\bf\pi^{(U)}({\mathcal{Q}}_{k})={\mathcal{Q}}^{\prime}_{k}}.

Denote by
t_{k}
the length of the cylinder
{\mathcal{Q}}_{k}
.
It follows from the above discussion, the construction of the cylinders
{\mathcal{Q}}_{k}
and
t_{k}\tilde{\tau}_{0}\leq q(Z_{k})\leq t_{k}\,\tau_{0}+1
that
there exist global constants
C_{5}>0
and
\epsilon_{2}>0
such that

\frac{\epsilon_{2}}{|b|}\,e^{t_{k}\hat{\epsilon}_{15}}\leq\mbox{\rm diam}(\widetilde{{\mathcal{Q}}}_{k})\leq\frac{C_{5}e^{t_{k}\hat{\epsilon}_{16}}}{|b|}

(6.4)

for all
k=1,2,\ldots,k_{0}
, where
\hat{\epsilon}_{15}=\hat{\epsilon}_{14}\tilde{\tau}_{0}
and
\hat{\epsilon}_{16}=3\hat{\epsilon}_{14}>3\hat{\epsilon}_{15}
.
It follows from the definition of
\hat{\epsilon}_{14}
that
\hat{\epsilon}_{15}>\hat{\epsilon}_{13}
, the constant from Lemma 5.5 and

t_{k}\geq\tilde{m}_{0}
, the integer from Lemma 5.5.

Let us mention that (
4.5
) and the above imply estimates for
t_{k}
by means of
|b|
.
Indeed, by (
6.4
) and (
4.5
) we get

-\log|b|+\log\epsilon_{2}+t_{k}\hat{\epsilon}_{15}\leq\log C_{3}+t_{k}\tau_{0}\hat{\epsilon}_{7}-t_{k}\tilde{\tau}_{0}\log\lambda_{1},

therefore

t_{k}(\tilde{\tau}_{0}\log\lambda_{1}+\hat{\epsilon}_{15}-\tau_{0}\hat{\epsilon}_{7})\leq\log|b|-\log\epsilon_{2}+\log C_{3},

so

\displaystyle t_{k}=t_{k}(b)\leq\frac{1}{\tilde{\tau}_{0}\log\lambda_{1}-\tau_{0}\hat{\epsilon}_{7}}(\log|b|-\log\epsilon_{2}+\log C_{3})\leq D_{1}\log|b|

for some global constant
D_{1}>0
, assuming
|b|
is sufficiently large.
In a similar way from the other sides of (
6.4
) and (
4.5
) we get

t_{k}(\tau_{0}\log\lambda_{1}+\hat{\epsilon}_{7}/\tilde{\tau}_{0}+\hat{\epsilon}_{16})\geq\log|b|-\log C_{3}-\log C_{5}
, that is there exists a global constant

D_{2}>0
so that (for sufficiently large
|b|
)
we have
t_{k}\geq D_{2}\log|b|
. Thus,

D_{2}\log|b|\leq t_{k}(b)\leq D_{1}\log|b|.

(6.5)

As a result of the construction in this section, we obtain a family
\{{\mathcal{Q}}_{k}\}_{k=1}^{k_{0}}
of cylinders
{\mathcal{Q}}_{k}={\mathcal{Q}}_{k}(b)
,
where
k_{0}=k_{0}(b)
also depends on
b
, such that the length
t_{k}=t_{k}(b)
of
{\mathcal{Q}}_{k}
satisfies (6.4) and (6.5) and

{\bf K_{0}=\pi^{(U)}(P_{0})\subset\cup_{k=1}^{k_{0}}{\mathcal{Q}}^{\prime}_{k}\subset U.}

Notice that
\displaystyle\gamma_{2}=\frac{1}{2}\nu(K_{0})>0
is a constant independent of
b
.

If
{\mathcal{C}}
is a cylinder in
U
, by Lemma 4.1(c) and our choice of
\alpha_{2}
we have

\mbox{\rm diam}_{\theta}(\widetilde{{\mathcal{C}}})\leq C_{1}(\mbox{\rm diam}(\widetilde{{\mathcal{C}}}))^{\alpha_{2}},

where
\widetilde{{\mathcal{C}}}=\widetilde{\Psi}({\mathcal{C}})\ \subset\widetilde{R}
. Consequently, if
r
is the length of
{\mathcal{C}}
, then

\mbox{\rm diam}_{\theta_{1}}(\widetilde{{\mathcal{C}}})=\theta_{1}^{r}<\theta^{r}=\mbox{\rm diam}_{\theta}(\widetilde{{\mathcal{C}}})\leq C_{1}\,(\mbox{\rm diam}(\widetilde{{\mathcal{C}}}))^{\alpha_{2}}.

A more precise estimate (which will be used below) is:

\mbox{\rm diam}_{\theta_{1}}(\widetilde{{\mathcal{C}}})=\theta_{1}^{r}=\theta^{r/\alpha_{3}}=(\mbox{\rm diam}_{\theta}(\widetilde{{\mathcal{C}}}))^{1/\alpha_{3}}\leq(C_{1}\mbox{\rm diam}(\widetilde{{\mathcal{C}}}))^{\alpha_{2}/\alpha_{3}}.

For the cylinders
{\mathcal{Q}}_{k}
it follows from the above and (
6.5
) that

\displaystyle\mbox{\rm diam}_{\theta}(\widetilde{{\mathcal{Q}}}_{k})

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C_{1}\left(\frac{C_{5}e^{t_{k}\hat{\epsilon}_{16}}}{|b|}\right)^{\alpha_{2}}\leq C_{1}\left(\frac{C_{5}e^{\hat{\epsilon}_{16}D_{1}\log|b|}}{|b|}\right)^{\alpha_{2}}\leq\frac{C_{1}(C_{5})^{\alpha_{2}}}{|b|^{\alpha_{2}(1-D_{1}\hat{\epsilon}_{16})}}\leq\frac{C_{7}}{|b|^{\alpha_{3}}},

where
C_{7}\geq C_{1}(C_{5})^{\alpha_{2}}
and
0<\alpha_{3}<\alpha_{2}
.
Thus,

\mbox{\rm diam}_{\theta}({\mathcal{Q}}_{k})\leq\frac{C_{7}}{|b|^{\alpha_{3}}}

for all
k=1,\ldots,k_{0}(b)
and all
|b|\geq b_{0}
.
Apart from that, the above and (
6.5
) yield

\displaystyle\mbox{\rm diam}_{\theta_{1}}({\mathcal{Q}}_{k})

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C_{1}\left(\frac{C_{5}e^{t_{k}\hat{\epsilon}_{16}}}{|b|}\right)^{\alpha_{2}/\alpha_{3}}\leq C_{1}\left(\frac{C_{5}e^{\hat{\epsilon}_{16}D_{1}\log|b|}}{|b|}\right)^{\alpha_{2}/\alpha_{3}}\leq\frac{C_{1}(C_{5})^{\alpha_{2}/\alpha_{3}}}{|b|^{(\alpha_{2}/\alpha_{3})(1-D_{1}\hat{\epsilon}_{16})}}\leq\frac{C_{7}}{|b|},

assuming that
C_{1}(C_{5})^{\alpha_{2}/\alpha_{3}}\leq C_{7}
and
D_{1}\hat{\epsilon}_{16}
is sufficiently small so that

(\alpha_{2}/\alpha_{3})(1-D_{1}\hat{\epsilon}_{16})>1
.

We will now slightly enlarge our cylinders
\widetilde{{\mathcal{Q}}}_{k}
. For every
k
, consider a cylinder
\widetilde{{\mathcal{C}}}_{k}

such that
\widetilde{{\mathcal{Q}}}_{k}\subset\widetilde{{\mathcal{C}}}_{k}
and

\frac{\epsilon_{4}}{|b|}\leq\mbox{\rm diam}_{\theta_{1}}(\widetilde{{\mathcal{C}}}_{k})\leq\frac{C_{7}}{|b|}

for some global constant
\epsilon_{4}>0
(we can take e.g.
\epsilon_{4}=\theta_{1}
).
Clearly such a cylinder
\widetilde{{\mathcal{C}}}_{k}
exists. In general the projections
\widetilde{{\mathcal{C}}}^{\prime}_{k}=\pi^{(U)}(\widetilde{{\mathcal{C}}}_{k})
are not necessarily disjoint.
We now choose a disjoint sub-family of
\{\widetilde{{\mathcal{C}}}_{k}\}_{k=1}^{k_{0}}
that covers the set
K_{0}
, and re-numbering its
elements, we get a family
\{\widetilde{{\mathcal{C}}}_{m}\}_{m=1}^{m_{0}}
of cylinders in
\widetilde{R}
for some
m_{0}=m_{0}(b)\leq k_{0}

such that their projections
\widetilde{{\mathcal{C}}}^{\prime}_{m}
in
\widehat{U}
are disjoint and cover the compact set
K_{0}
. Then for each
m=1,\ldots,m_{0}

there exists
k=1,\ldots,k_{0}
with
\widetilde{{\mathcal{Q}}}_{k}\subset\widetilde{{\mathcal{C}}}_{m}
, therefore the length
s_{m}
of
\widetilde{{\mathcal{C}}}_{m}

satisfies
s_{m}\leq t_{k}
, and it follows from (6.4) that

\frac{\epsilon_{2}e^{s_{m}\hat{\epsilon}_{15}}}{|b|}\leq\frac{\epsilon_{2}e^{t_{k}\hat{\epsilon}_{15}}}{|b|}\leq\mbox{\rm diam}(\widetilde{{\mathcal{Q}}}_{k})\leq\mbox{\rm diam}(\widetilde{{\mathcal{C}}}_{m}).

We have proved the following lemma.

Lemma 6.1.

There exists cylinders
\widetilde{{\mathcal{C}}}_{1},\ldots,\widetilde{{\mathcal{C}}}_{m_{0}}
in
\widetilde{R}
for some
m_{0}=m_{0}(b)\geq 1
such that
each of them has a common point with
\widetilde{P}_{0}
,
\widetilde{{\mathcal{C}}}^{\prime}_{m}\cap\widetilde{{\mathcal{C}}}^{\prime}_{m^{\prime}}\cap\widehat{U}=\emptyset
for any
m\neq m^{\prime}
,

{\bf K_{0}=\pi^{(U)}(P_{0})\subset V_{b}=\cup_{m=1}^{m_{0}}{\mathcal{C}}^{\prime}_{m}},

(6.6)

and for all
m=1,\ldots,m_{0}
we have

\frac{\epsilon_{4}}{|b|}\leq\mbox{\rm diam}_{\theta_{1}}(\widetilde{{\mathcal{C}}}_{m})\leq\frac{C_{7}}{|b|}

(6.7)

and

\frac{\epsilon_{2}}{|b|}\,e^{s_{m}\hat{\epsilon}_{15}}\leq\mbox{\rm diam}(\widetilde{{\mathcal{C}}}_{m}),

(6.8)

where
s_{m}
is the length of the cylinder
\widetilde{{\mathcal{C}}}_{m}
.

We should stress that
\epsilon_{4}>0
and
C_{7}>0
are global constants, independent of
b
.

Since
\theta=\theta_{1}^{\alpha_{3}}
, it follows from (
6.7
) that

\frac{\epsilon_{4}}{|b|^{\alpha_{3}}}\leq\mbox{\rm diam}_{\theta}(\widetilde{{\mathcal{C}}}_{m})\leq\frac{C_{7}}{|b|^{\alpha_{3}}}

(6.9)

for all
m=1,\ldots,m_{0}
.

6.2
A large deviation estimate

Let
P_{0}
be the fixed Pesin set in
R\cap{\mathcal{L}}
from Sect. 4.2 and let
K_{0}=\pi^{(U)}(P_{0})\subset U
.

Fix for a moment a large constant
b_{0}>1
and assume that
|b|\geq b_{0}
. Recalling the construction of
the sets
V_{b}
in Sect. 6.1, we have
K_{0}\subset V_{b}
, and
\cap_{|b|\geq b_{0}}V_{b}=K_{0}
.
Hence
\nu(V_{b})\geq\nu(K_{0})=2\gamma_{2}
. Here, as in Sect. 4.2,
\nu
is the Gibbs measure defined
by the function
g=f-P_{f}\,\tau
on
U
with
\mbox{\rm Pr}_{\sigma}(g)=0
.

Consider the function

\displaystyle\Psi^{(b)}=\chi_{V_{b}}.

Remark 1.
Let
\epsilon>0
be an expansivity constant (see e.g.
[
Ba
]
) for
\sigma_{A}:\Sigma^{+}_{{\mathcal{A}}}\longrightarrow\Sigma^{+}_{{\mathcal{A}}}

with respect to the metric
D_{\theta}
; one can take e.g.
\epsilon=\theta/2
. Notice that if
n\geq 1
is an integer and
x=(x_{0},x_{1},\ldots)
,

y=(y_{0},y_{1},\ldots)\in\Sigma^{+}_{{\mathcal{A}}}
are such that
D_{\theta}(\sigma^{i}(x),\sigma^{i}(y))<\epsilon
for all
i=0,1,\ldots,n-1
,
then
x_{i}=y_{i}
for all
i<n
, so
D_{\theta}(x,y)\leq\theta^{n}
. Hence

|\psi_{n}(x)-\psi_{n}(y)|\leq\sum_{i=0}^{n-1}|\psi(\sigma^{i}(x))-\psi(\sigma^{i}(y))|\leq|\psi|_{\theta}\sum_{i=0}^{n-1}\theta^{n-i}<\frac{|\psi|_{\theta}}{1-\theta}.

Notice that
|\Psi^{(b)}|_{\theta}
is rather large. Indeed, by Lemma 4.1(a) the natural estimate for
|\Psi^{(b)}|_{\theta}
is

\displaystyle|\Psi^{(b)}|_{\theta}\leq\frac{1}{\theta^{s_{m}(b)}}=1/\mbox{\rm diam}_{\theta}(\widetilde{{\mathcal{C}}}_{m})
which is of size
\mbox{\rm Const}\;|b|^{\alpha_{3}}
by (
6.9
).
The latter is much larger than
\mbox{\rm Const}\;\log|b|
. In what follows we will avoid using this norm, employing some simple
estimates instead – see e.g. (
6.11
) below.

It is easy to see that
\Psi^{(b)}
is not co-homologous to a constant, i.e. there do not exist a constant
c\in{\mathbb{R}}
and
h\in C(U)

such that
\Psi^{(b)}=c+h-h\circ\sigma
on
U
. This follows e.g. from Livsic’s Theorem (see Proposition 3.7 in
[
PP
]
).
Indeed, if
\Psi^{(b)}
is co-homologous to
c
, then by Livsic’s Theorem we must have
\Psi^{(b)}_{n}(\eta)=nc
for all
n\geq 1
and
all
\eta\in U
with
\sigma^{n}(\eta)=\eta
. Since
\Psi^{(b)}_{n}(\eta)
is always an integer and
0\leq\Psi^{(b)}_{n}(\eta)\leq n
,
this implies
c=p/n
for some integer
p=0,1,\ldots,n
. However,
V_{b}
is a union of cylinders and

\nu(V_{b})>0
, so
V_{b}
contains non-periodic points that can be approximated by periodic points in
V_{b}
with
arbitrarily large periods. This implies
c=0
which leads to a contradiction.

In what follows we will use the shift space
\Sigma^{+}_{{\mathcal{A}}}
and the shift
\sigma_{A}:\Sigma^{+}_{{\mathcal{A}}}\longrightarrow\Sigma^{+}_{{\mathcal{A}}}
. We will identify
\nu
with
a Gibbs measure on
\Sigma^{+}_{{\mathcal{A}}}
using the natural projection
\pi^{+}:\Sigma^{+}_{{\mathcal{A}}}\longrightarrow U
which conjugates
\sigma_{A}
and
\sigma

(see Sects. 2 and 4.1). We will also identify the set
V_{b}
with a subset of
\Sigma^{+}_{{\mathcal{A}}}
via this isomorphism, using the same notation for it.

Recall that
V_{b}
is a union of cylinders
{\mathcal{C}}^{\prime}_{m}
of length
s_{m}(b)\leq t_{k}(b)\leq L_{b}
, where by (
6.5
) we can take

L_{b}=D_{1}\,\log|b|.

(6.10)

For two cylinders in
\Sigma^{+}_{{\mathcal{A}}}
, either
one of them contains the other, or they are disjoint. It follows from this that if
X
is a cylinder in
\Sigma^{+}_{{\mathcal{A}}}
of length
n>L_{b}
,
then either
X\subset V_{b}
or
X\cap V_{b}=\emptyset
. Hence either
\Psi^{(b)}(x)=1
for all
x\in X
or
\Psi^{(b)}(x)=0
for all
x\in X
.
This implies a certain improvement of the estimate in Remark 1 in the special case
\psi=\Psi^{(b)}
.
Assume
n>L_{b}
and
D_{\theta}(x,y)\leq\theta^{n}
for some
x,y\in\Sigma^{+}_{{\mathcal{A}}}
. Then for any integer
i<n-L_{b}
we have

D_{\theta}(\sigma^{i}x,\sigma^{i}y)\leq\theta^{L_{b}}
, so
\sigma^{i}y
belongs to the cylinder of length
L_{b}
determined by
\sigma^{i}x
.
Thus, either both
\sigma^{i}x
and
\sigma^{i}y
belong to
V_{b}
or both do not belong to
V_{b}
. That is
\Psi^{(b)}(\sigma^{i}x)=\Psi^{(b)}(\sigma^{i}y)
.
This implies

\displaystyle|\Psi^{(b)}_{n}(x)-\Psi^{(b)}_{n}(y)|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sum_{i=0}^{n-1}|\Psi^{(b)}(\sigma^{i}(x))-\Psi^{(b)}(\sigma^{i}(y))|

(6.11)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{i=n-L_{b}}^{n-1}|\Psi^{(b)}(\sigma^{i}(x))-\Psi^{(b)}(\sigma^{i}(y))|\leq L_{b}

for all
n>L_{b}
and all
x,y\in\Sigma^{+}_{{\mathcal{A}}}
with
D_{\theta}(x,y)\leq\theta^{n}
.
Notice that (
6.11
) also holds for all
n=1,\ldots,L_{b}
and all
x,y\in\Sigma^{+}_{{\mathcal{A}}}
.

Given functions
\Psi,\Phi\in{\mathcal{F}}_{\theta}(\Sigma^{+}_{{\mathcal{A}}})
, set

\displaystyle{\mathcal{I}}(\Psi)=\left\{\int\Psi\,d\mu:\mu\in{\mathcal{M}}\right\},

where
{\mathcal{M}}
is the set of all
\sigma_{A}
-invariant Borel probability measures on
\Sigma^{+}_{{\mathcal{A}}}
, and let
\mu_{\Phi}
be the

equilibrium state
of
\Phi
(see e.g. ch. 9 in
[
Wa
]
). Assume
\mu_{\Psi}
is not the measure of maximal entropy
for
\sigma_{A}:\Sigma^{+}_{{\mathcal{A}}}\longrightarrow\Sigma^{+}_{{\mathcal{A}}}
. It
follows from general large deviation principles (see e.g.
[
Kif
]
,
[
Y1
]
,
[
OP
]
) that
there exists a real-analytic
rate function

I=I_{\Psi}:\mbox{\rm Int}({\mathcal{I}}(\Psi))\longrightarrow[0,\infty)
such that for any interval
\Delta
in
{\mathbb{R}}

we have

\lim_{n\to\infty}\frac{1}{n}\log\left(\mu_{\Phi}\left\{\eta\in\Sigma^{+}_{{\mathcal{A}}}:\frac{\Psi_{n}(\eta)}{n}\in\Delta\right\}\right)=-\inf\left\{I(p):p\in\Delta\cap\mbox{\rm Int}({\mathcal{I}}(\Psi))\right\}.

If
\Psi
is not co-homologous to a constant,
{\mathcal{I}}(\Psi)
is a non trivial, closed interval and

\mbox{\rm Int}({\mathcal{I}}(\Psi))=\left\{\int_{\Sigma^{+}_{{\mathcal{A}}}}\Psi\,d\mu_{\Phi+q\Psi}:q\in{\mathbb{R}}\right\},

where
\mu_{\Phi+q\Psi}
is the
equilibrium state
of the function
\Phi+q\Psi
.
Moreover,
I(p)=0
if and only if
\displaystyle p=\int_{\Sigma^{+}_{{\mathcal{A}}}}\Psi\,d\mu_{\Phi}.

It is known that

-I(p)=\inf\left\{\mbox{\rm Pr}_{\sigma}(\Phi+q\,\Psi)-\mbox{\rm Pr}_{\sigma}(\Phi)-q\,p:\>q\in{\mathbb{R}}\right\}=\inf\left\{-J(p,q):\>q\in{\mathbb{R}}\right\},

(6.12)

where
J=J_{\Psi}:{\mathbb{R}}\times{\mathbb{R}}\longrightarrow{\mathbb{R}}
is defined by

J(p,q)=q\,p+\mbox{\rm Pr}_{\sigma}(\Phi)-\mbox{\rm Pr}_{\sigma}(\Phi+q\,\Psi).

Moreover,

\left[\frac{d}{dq}\,\mbox{\rm Pr}_{\sigma}(\Phi+q\,\Psi)\right]_{q=\eta}=\int_{\Sigma^{+}_{{\mathcal{A}}}}\Psi\,d\mu_{\Phi+\eta\,\Psi},

(6.13)

and for any
p\in{\mathcal{I}}(\Psi)
there exists a unique number
q(p)=q_{\Psi}(p)\in{\mathbb{R}}
such that

-I(p)=\mbox{\rm Pr}_{\sigma}(\Phi+q(p)\,\Psi)-\mbox{\rm Pr}_{\sigma}(\Phi)-q(p)p=-J(p,q(p))

(6.14)

and

\displaystyle p=\int_{\Sigma^{+}_{{\mathcal{A}}}}\Psi\,d\mu_{\Phi+q(p)\,\Psi}.

(6.15)

It is worth mentioning that
q(p)
in (
6.14
) is the unique number satisfying (
6.15
)
.

Remark 2.
Let
\Psi\in{\mathcal{F}}_{\theta}(\Sigma^{+}_{{\mathcal{A}}})
be as above and let
\Phi=g
; then
\mu_{\Phi}=\nu

and
\mbox{\rm Pr}_{\sigma}(g)=0
. Let
p\in{\mathcal{I}}(\Psi)
,
p\neq\omega_{\Psi}=\int_{\Sigma^{+}_{{\mathcal{A}}}}\Psi\,d\nu
. Then

\displaystyle q_{\Psi}(p)=\frac{-I_{\Psi}(p)}{\omega_{\Psi}-p},

so
q_{\Psi}(p)<0
when
p<\omega_{\Psi}
and
q_{\Psi}(p)>0
when
p>\omega_{\Psi}
.
Indeed, it follows from (
6.14
) with
\Phi=g
that for any
p\in{\mathcal{I}}(\Psi)
we have

\displaystyle-I(p)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mbox{\rm Pr}_{\sigma}(g+q_{\Psi}(p)\,\Psi)-q_{\Psi}(p)p=\mbox{\rm Pr}_{\sigma}(g+q_{\Psi}(p)\,\Psi)-q_{\Psi}(p)\omega_{\Psi}+q_{\Psi}(p)(\omega_{\Psi}-p)

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\inf\left\{\mbox{\rm Pr}_{\sigma}(g+q\,\Psi)-q\,\omega_{\Psi}:\>q\in{\mathbb{R}}\right\}+q_{\Psi}(p)(\omega_{\Psi}-p)=q_{\Psi}(p)(\omega_{\Psi}-p),

since
I(\omega_{\Psi})=0
. Thus,
q_{\Psi}(p)(\omega_{\Psi}-p)=-I(p)<0
.

For any integer
n\geq 1
, any
|b|\geq b_{0}
and any
q\in{\mathbb{R}}
set

f^{(b)}_{n}(q)=\frac{1}{n}\log\int_{U}e^{q\Psi^{(b)}_{n}(x)}\,d\nu(x).

The following lemma can be derived from the proof of Lemma 4.1 in
[

]
.

Lemma 6.2.

There exist a global constant
C_{6}=C(\epsilon)\geq 2
with the following property: if

\Psi\in{\mathcal{F}}_{\theta}(\Sigma^{+}_{{\mathcal{A}}})
and the constant
K>0

are so that

|\Psi_{n}(x)-\Psi_{n}(y)|\leq K
for all
n\geq 1
and all
x,y\in\Sigma^{+}_{{\mathcal{A}}}
with
D_{\theta}(x,y)\leq\theta^{n}
,
then the function

f_{n}(q)=\frac{1}{n}\log\int_{U}e^{q\Psi_{n}(x)}\,d\nu(x)\hskip 10.00002pt,\hskip 10.00002ptq\in{\mathbb{R}},

satisfies

f_{n}(q)\leq\mbox{\rm Pr}_{\sigma}(g+q\Psi)+\frac{2|q|K}{n}+\frac{1}{n}\log C_{6}

(6.16)

for all
q\in{\mathbb{R}}
and all
n\geq 1
.

It follows from Lemma 4.1 in
[
MV
]
(see also Proposition 3.1 in
[
Den
]
) that for the function

in the above lemma there exists

\lim_{n\to\infty}f_{n}(q)=\mbox{\rm Pr}_{\sigma}(g+q\Psi)

for every
q\in{\mathbb{R}}
. Here we only need one half of that argument.
For completeness we prove Lemma 6.2 in Appendix II using part of the argument in
[
MV
]
and some lemmas from
[
B3
]
.

In what follows it is more convenient to work with

Given real numbers
1<b_{0}\leq|b|
, set

\varphi^{(b)}_{b_{0}}=\Psi^{(b_{0})}-\Psi^{(b)}=\chi_{V_{b_{0}}}-\chi_{V_{b}}.

Then

\int_{U}\varphi^{(b)}_{b_{0}}\,d\nu=\nu(V_{b_{0}})-\nu(V_{b})\leq\nu(V_{b_{0}})-\nu(K_{0})\to 0

as
b_{0}\to\infty
.

Notice that

\mbox{\rm Pr}_{\sigma}(g+q\,\Psi^{(b)})\leq\frac{1}{2}\mbox{\rm Pr}_{\sigma}(g+2q\,\Psi^{(b_{0})})+\frac{1}{2}\mbox{\rm Pr}_{\sigma}(g+2|q|\varphi^{(b)}_{b_{0}})

(6.17)

for all
|b|\geq b_{0}
and all
q<0
. Indeed,
\Psi^{(b)}=\Psi^{(b_{0})}-\varphi^{(b)}_{b_{0}}
and the convexity of
\mbox{\rm Pr}_{\sigma}
yield

\displaystyle\mbox{\rm Pr}_{\sigma}(g+q\,\Psi^{(b)})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mbox{\rm Pr}_{\sigma}(g+q\,\Psi^{(b_{0})}-q\varphi^{(b)}_{b_{0}})=\mbox{\rm Pr}_{\sigma}\left(\frac{1}{2}(g+2q\,\Psi^{(b_{0})})+\frac{1}{2}(g-2q\varphi^{(b)}_{b_{0}})\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\mbox{\rm Pr}_{\sigma}(g+2q\,\Psi^{(b_{0})})+\frac{1}{2}\mbox{\rm Pr}_{\sigma}(g-2q\varphi^{(b)}_{b_{0}}).

This proves (
6.17
).

For a given
b
, the function corresponding to (

) for
\Psi^{(b)}
and
\Phi=g
is

-J_{b}(p,q)=\mbox{\rm Pr}_{\sigma}(g+q\,\Psi^{(b)})-q\,p.

Moreover,

-I_{b}(p)=-J_{b}(p\,,\,q^{(b)}(p))=\mbox{\rm Pr}_{\sigma}(g+q^{(b)}(p)\,\Psi^{(b)})-q^{(b)}(p)\,p

. Set

\displaystyle\omega_{b}=\int_{U}\Psi^{(b)}\,d\nu=\nu(V_{b}).

Then
\omega_{b}>\nu(K_{0})=2\gamma_{2}
.

J_{b}
by means of

.

Lemma 6.3.
(
[
St4
]
)
Assuming
b_{0}>1
is chosen sufficiently large, for any
|b|\geq b_{0}
, any
\delta_{0}
with

and any
q

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{|\Psi^{(b)}|_{\theta}}\leq q<0

we have

J_{b}(p,q)\geq\frac{\delta_{0}|q|}{2}

(6.18)

for all

with

.

Remark 3.
Although Theorem 1.1 and Lemma 2.3 in
[
St4
]
are stated for some particular value of
q=q_{0}
the proof
actually works for all

with

. For example, the argument in pp. 1021-1022 in
[
St4
]
shows that,
for the given
q_{0}>0
satisfying the conditions in Lemma 2.3, for the function
\Gamma(q)=pq-\mbox{\rm Pr}(\phi+q\,\psi)
we have

\Gamma^{\prime}(q)\leq-\delta_{0}/2
for all
q\in[-q_{0},0]
which implies
\Gamma(q)\geq\delta_{0}|q|/2
for all
q\in[-q_{0},0]
.

Lemma 6.3 implies an useful estimate for
\mbox{\rm Pr}_{\sigma}(g+q_{0}\Psi^{(b)})
for
q_{0}<0
.

Fix now
b_{0}>1
with the property in Lemma 6.3. Set

\displaystyle\epsilon_{0}=\frac{\nu(K_{0})}{8}=\frac{\gamma_{2}}{4}\hskip 10.00002pt,\hskip 10.00002pt\delta_{0}=4\epsilon_{0}<\omega_{b_{0}}.

In what follows we will consider values of
q_{0}<0
and
p
with

-\frac{1}{|\Psi^{(b_{0})}|_{\theta}}\leq q_{0}<0\hskip 10.00002pt,\hskip 10.00002pt0<p\leq\gamma_{2}=4\epsilon_{0}.

(6.19)

It follows from (
6.18
) that

J_{b_{0}}(p,q_{0})\geq\frac{4\epsilon_{0}|q_{0}|}{2}=2\epsilon_{0}|q_{0}|,

and then
the definition of
J_{b_{0}}(p,q)
yields

q_{0}p-\mbox{\rm Pr}_{\sigma}(g+q_{0}\,\Psi^{(b_{0})})=J_{b_{0}}(p,q_{0})\geq 2\epsilon_{0}|q_{0}|.

Using this with
\displaystyle p=3\epsilon_{0}<\omega_{b_{0}}-\delta_{0}
, gives

\mbox{\rm Pr}_{\sigma}(g+q_{0}\Psi^{(b_{0})})\leq q_{0}3\epsilon_{0}-2\epsilon_{0}|q_{0}|=5q_{0}\epsilon_{0}<0.

(6.20)

Given
|b|\geq b_{0}
, it follows from the continuous dependence of equilibrium states on functions (see e.g. Theorem 4.2.11 in
[
K
]
)
that

\lim_{\alpha\searrow 0}\int_{U}\varphi^{(b)}_{b_{0}}\,d\mu_{g+\alpha\,\varphi^{(b)}_{b_{0}}}=\int_{U}\varphi^{(b)}_{b_{0}}\,d\mu_{g}=\int_{U}\varphi^{(b)}_{b_{0}}\,d\nu=\nu(V_{b_{0}})-\nu(V_{b})\leq\nu(V_{b_{0}})-\nu(K_{0}).

We will
assume that the fixed
b_{0}>1
with the property in Lemma 6.3 is so large that

\nu(V_{b_{0}})-\nu(K_{0})<\frac{\epsilon_{0}}{2}=\frac{\gamma_{2}}{8}.

(6.21)

Notice that
\nu(V_{b_{0}})>\nu(K_{0})=8\epsilon_{0}
.
Fix a small
\hat{\delta}_{0}>0
so that

0<\hat{\delta}_{0}<\frac{1}{2}(\nu(V_{b_{0}})-8\epsilon_{0})<\frac{\epsilon_{0}}{4}.

Then
\nu(V_{b_{0}})>8\epsilon_{0}+2\hat{\delta}_{0}
.

Lemma 6.4.

There exist
\alpha_{0}>0
and
\tilde{b}_{0}\geq b_{0}
such that

\int_{U}\varphi^{(b)}_{b_{0}}\,d\mu_{g+\alpha\,\varphi^{(b)}_{b_{0}}}\leq\nu(V_{b_{0}})-8\epsilon_{0}+\hat{\delta}_{0}

(6.22)

for all
\alpha\in(0,\alpha_{0}]
and all
b\in{\mathbb{R}}
with
|b|\geq\tilde{b}_{0}
.

Proof.
Assume that the claim is not true; then here exists sequences
\alpha_{n}\searrow 0
and
b_{n}\nearrow\infty
such that

\int_{U}\varphi^{(b_{n})}_{b_{0}}\,d\mu_{g+\alpha_{n}\,\varphi^{(b_{n})}_{b_{0}}}>\nu(V_{b_{0}})-8\epsilon_{0}+\hat{\delta}_{0}

(6.23)

for all
n\geq 1
. Given a
fixed integer
n
, it follows from the continuous dependence of equilibrium states
on functions (see e.g. Theorem 4.2.11 in
[
K
]
) that

\lim_{\beta\to 0}\int_{U}\varphi^{(b_{n})}_{b_{0}}\,d\mu_{g+\beta\,\varphi^{(b_{n})}_{b_{0}}}=\int_{U}\varphi^{(b_{n})}_{b_{0}}\,d\mu_{g}=\int_{U}\left(\Psi^{(b_{0})}-\Psi^{(b_{n})}\right)\,d\nu=\nu(V_{b_{0}})-\nu(V_{b_{n}})<\nu(V_{b_{0}})-\nu(K_{0}).

Therefore for sufficiently small
\beta>0
we have

\int_{U}\varphi^{(b_{n})}_{b_{0}}\,d\mu_{g+\beta\,\varphi^{(b_{n})}_{b_{0}}}<\nu(V_{b_{0}})-\nu(K_{0})=\nu(V_{b_{0}})-8\epsilon_{0}.

For the fixed integer
n
, choose an arbitrary
\beta_{n}
such that
0<\beta_{n}<\alpha_{n}
and

\int_{U}\varphi^{(b_{n})}_{b_{0}}\,d\mu_{g+\beta_{n}\,\varphi^{(b_{n})}_{b_{0}}}<\nu(V_{b_{0}})-8\epsilon_{0}.

(6.24)

Fix
\beta_{n}
with this property
.
Then
\gamma_{n}=\alpha_{n}-\beta_{n}\to 0
as
n\to\infty
.

Using (
6.14
) with
\Psi=\varphi^{(b_{n})}_{b_{0}}

and
\Phi=g+\beta_{n}\varphi^{(b_{n})}_{b_{0}}
, for the corresponding rate function
I_{\Psi}
and the number

\displaystyle p_{n}=\int_{U}\Psi\,d\mu_{\Phi+\gamma_{n}\Psi},

there exists a unique
q_{n}=q_{n}(p_{n})
with

-I(p_{n})=\inf\{\mbox{\rm Pr}_{\sigma}(\Phi+q\,\Psi)-\mbox{\rm Pr}_{\sigma}(\Phi)-q\,p_{n}\,:\,q\in{\mathbb{R}}\}=\mbox{\rm Pr}_{\sigma}(\Phi+q_{n}\,\Psi)-\mbox{\rm Pr}_{\sigma}(\Phi)-q_{n}\,p_{n}.

The number
q_{n}
is the unique number with
\displaystyle p_{n}=\int_{U}\Psi\,d\mu_{\Phi+q_{n}\Psi}
,
and it now follows from the above that we must have
q_{n}=\gamma_{n}
.

Therefore

-I(p_{n})=\mbox{\rm Pr}_{\sigma}(\Phi+\gamma_{n}\,\Psi)-\mbox{\rm Pr}_{\sigma}(\Phi)-\gamma_{n}\,p_{n},

which gives

\mbox{\rm Pr}_{\sigma}(\Phi+\gamma_{n}\,\Psi)-\mbox{\rm Pr}_{\sigma}(\Phi)-\gamma_{n}\,p_{n}=-I(p_{n})\leq 0.

Notice that

\Phi+\gamma_{n}\,\Psi=g+\beta_{n}\varphi^{(b_{n})}_{b_{0}}+\gamma_{n}\varphi^{(b_{n})}_{b_{0}}=g+\alpha_{n}\varphi^{(b_{n})}_{b_{0}}
,
so

\displaystyle p_{n}=\int_{U}\Psi\,d\mu_{\Phi+\gamma_{n}\Psi}=\int_{U}\varphi^{(b_{n})}_{b_{0}}\,d\mu_{g+\alpha_{n}\,\varphi^{(b_{n})}_{b_{0}}}.

It follows from the above that

\mbox{\rm Pr}_{\sigma}(g+\alpha_{n}\varphi^{(b_{n})}_{b_{0}})=\mbox{\rm Pr}_{\sigma}(\Phi+\gamma_{n}\,\Psi)\leq\mbox{\rm Pr}_{\sigma}(\Phi)+\gamma_{n}\,p_{n}=\mbox{\rm Pr}_{\sigma}(g+\beta_{n}\varphi^{(b_{n})}_{b_{0}})+\gamma_{n}\,p_{n}

for all
n\geq 1
. Combing this with (6.23) and (6.24) implies

\nu(V_{b_{0}})-8\epsilon_{0}+\hat{\delta}_{0}<\mbox{\rm Pr}_{\sigma}(g+\alpha_{n}\varphi^{(b_{n})}_{b_{0}})\leq\mbox{\rm Pr}_{\sigma}(g+\beta_{n}\varphi^{(b_{n})}_{b_{0}})+\gamma_{n}\,p_{n}<\nu(V_{b_{0}})-8\epsilon_{0}+\gamma_{n}\,p_{n}

for all
n\geq 1
. Hence

\hat{\delta}_{0}<\gamma_{n}\,p_{n}

for all
n\geq 1
. However for all
n
we have
\varphi^{(b_{n})}_{b_{0}}=\Psi^{(b_{0})}-\Psi^{(b_{n})}\leq\Psi^{(b_{0})}\leq 1
, therefore

\displaystyle p_{n}=\int_{U}\varphi^{(b_{n})}_{b_{0}}\,d\mu_{g+\alpha_{n}\,\varphi^{(b_{n})}_{b_{0}}}\leq 1

for all
n
. This and the above yield

\hat{\delta}_{0}<\gamma_{n}
for all
n\geq 1
, which is a contradiction, since
\gamma_{n}\to 0
as
n\to\infty
.

This proves that there exist
\alpha_{0}>0
and
\tilde{b}_{0}\geq b_{0}
such that (
6.22
) holds for all

\alpha\in(0,\alpha_{0}]
and all
|b|\geq\tilde{b}_{0}
.

Fix a for a moment
\alpha\in(0,\alpha_{0}]
and
b\in{\mathbb{R}}
with
|b|\geq\tilde{b}_{0}
.
It follows from Lemma 6.3 that

p=p(\alpha)=\int_{U}\varphi^{(b)}_{b_{0}}\,d\mu_{g+\alpha\,\varphi^{(b)}_{b_{0}}}<\nu(V_{b_{0}})-8\epsilon_{0}+\hat{\delta}_{0}<\frac{\epsilon_{0}}{2}+\hat{\delta}_{0}<\epsilon_{0}.

(6.25)

Then for the rate function
I_{b_{0},b}
for the potential
\Psi=\varphi^{(b)}_{b_{0}}
and
\Phi=g

there exists a unique
q_{\alpha}=q_{\alpha}(p)
with

-I_{b_{0},b}(p)=\inf\{\mbox{\rm Pr}_{\sigma}(g+q\,\Psi)-\mbox{\rm Pr}_{\sigma}(g)-q\,p\,:\,q\in{\mathbb{R}}\}=\mbox{\rm Pr}_{\sigma}(g+q_{\alpha}\,\Psi)-q_{\alpha}\,p.

The number
q_{\alpha}
is the unique number with

\displaystyle p=\int_{U}\Psi\,d\mu_{g+q_{\alpha}\Psi}=\int_{U}\varphi^{(b)}_{b_{0}}\,d\mu_{g+q_{\alpha}\,\varphi^{(b)}_{b_{0}}}

and it follows from (
6.25
) that we must have
q_{\alpha}=\alpha
.
Thus,

\displaystyle-I_{b_{0},b}(p)=\mbox{\rm Pr}_{\sigma}(g+\alpha\,\varphi^{(b)}_{b_{0}})-\alpha\,p,

where
p=p(\alpha)
is as in (
6.25
). Hence

\mbox{\rm Pr}_{\sigma}(g+\alpha\,\varphi^{(b)}_{b_{0}})=-I_{b_{0},b}(p)+\alpha\,p\leq\alpha\,p<\alpha\,\epsilon_{0}.

Combining all of the above, we get the following.

Lemma 6.5.

Let
\epsilon_{0}=\frac{\gamma_{2}}{4}>0
and let
\alpha_{0}>0
and
\tilde{b}_{0}>b_{0}
be
so that
(
6.22
)
holds for all
\alpha\in(0,\alpha_{0}]
and all
b\in{\mathbb{R}}
with
|b|\geq\tilde{b}_{0}
.
Choose
q_{0}<0
with
(
6.19
)
so that
\alpha=2|q_{0}|\leq\alpha_{0}
. Then

\mbox{\rm Pr}_{\sigma}(g+q_{0}\,\Psi^{(b)})\leq 4q_{0}\epsilon_{0}<0

(6.26)

for all
b\in{\mathbb{R}}
with
|b|\geq\tilde{b}_{0}
.

Proof.
Define
p=p(\alpha)
by (
6.25
). It follows from (
6.17
) and (
6.20
)
with
q_{0}
replaced by
2q_{0}
and the estimate

derived above that

\displaystyle\mbox{\rm Pr}_{\sigma}(g+q_{0}\,\Psi^{(b)})

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\mbox{\rm Pr}_{\sigma}(g+2q_{0}\,\Psi^{(b_{0})})+\frac{1}{2}\mbox{\rm Pr}_{\sigma}(g+2|q_{0}|\varphi^{(b)}_{b_{0}})

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 5q_{0}\epsilon_{0}+\frac{1}{2}\mbox{\rm Pr}_{\sigma}(g+2|q_{0}|\,\varphi^{(b)}_{b_{0}})\leq 5q_{0}\epsilon_{0}+\frac{1}{2}2|q_{0}|\,\epsilon_{0}\leq 5q_{0}\epsilon_{0}+|q_{0}|\epsilon_{0}=4q_{0}\epsilon_{0}.

This proves (
6.26
).

Let now
\tilde{b}_{0}>b_{0}>1
and
q_{0}<0
be as in Lemma 6.5, and let
|b|\geq\tilde{b}_{0}
and
n\geq 1
.
We will now use Lemma 6.2 with
\Psi=q_{0}\Psi^{(b)}
and
K=|q_{0}|L_{b}
.
It follows from (
6.16
) with
\Psi=q_{0}\Psi^{(b)}
and (
6.11
) that

f^{(b)}_{n}(q_{0})\leq\mbox{\rm Pr}_{\sigma}(g+q_{0}\Psi^{(b)})+\frac{2|q_{0}|}{n}L_{b}+\frac{1}{n}\log C_{6}\leq 4q_{0}\epsilon_{0}+\frac{2|q_{0}|L_{b}}{n}+\frac{1}{n}\log C_{6}

for all
n\geq 1
.
Using (
6.10
), we have
L_{b}=D_{1}\log|b|
. Define an integer
n_{0}(b)
by

n_{0}(b)=D_{3}\,\log|b|,

(6.27)

where
D_{3}>1
is a
fixed constant
with

\displaystyle D_{3}\geq\max\left\{\frac{2D_{1}}{\epsilon_{0}}\,,\,\frac{\log C_{6}}{|q_{0}|\epsilon_{0}}\right\}.

Then for every
|b|\geq\tilde{b}_{0}
and every integer
n\geq n_{0}(b)
we have

f^{(b)}_{n}(q_{0})\leq 4q_{0}\epsilon_{0}+2|q_{0}|\epsilon_{0}\leq 2q_{0}\epsilon_{0}<0.

(6.28)

Set

A^{(b)}(n)=\{x\in U:\Psi^{(b)}_{n}(x)\leq n\epsilon_{0}\}.

Since
q_{0}<0
, we have

\displaystyle A^{(b)}(n)=\{x\in U:e^{q_{0}\Psi^{(b)}_{n}(x)}\geq e^{nq_{0}\epsilon_{0}}\}.

Using Chebyshev’s inequality
13
13

13

Estimates of this kind appear e.g. in
[
BLM
]
or Sect. 2.4 in
[
Var
]
.

(see e.g. Ch. 3 in
[
G
]
), it follows that

\nu(A^{(b)}(n))\leq\frac{\int_{U}e^{q_{0}\Psi^{(b)}_{n}(x)}\,d\nu(x)}{e^{nq_{0}\epsilon_{0}}}=e^{-nq_{0}\epsilon_{0}}\,\int_{U}e^{q_{0}\Psi^{(b)}_{n}(x)}\,d\nu(x).

Assuming
n\geq n_{0}(b)
and combining this with (
6.28
) gives

\frac{1}{n}\log\nu(A^{(b)}(n))\leq-q_{0}\epsilon_{0}+\frac{1}{n}\log\int_{U}e^{q_{0}\Psi^{(b)}_{n}(x)}\,d\nu(x)=-q_{0}\epsilon_{0}+f^{(b)}_{n}(q_{0})\leq-q_{0}\epsilon_{0}+2q_{0}\epsilon_{0}=q_{0}\epsilon_{0}

for all
|b|\geq\tilde{b}_{0}
,
n\geq n_{0}(b)
.
Thus, for such
b
and
n
we have
\nu(A^{(b)}(n))\leq e^{nq_{0}\epsilon_{0}}
.

Set

c=|q_{0}|\epsilon_{0}>0.

It follows from our discussion that for the set

U_{b}(n)=\left\{x\in U:\frac{\Psi^{(b)}_{n}(x)}{n}\leq\epsilon_{0}\right\},

we have

\displaystyle\nu\left(U_{b}(n)\right)\leq e^{-nc}

for all
|b|\geq\tilde{b}_{0}
and all integers
n\geq n_{0}(b)
.

We will use the above for integers of the form
n=MN
, where
M\geq n_{0}(b)
and
N\geq 1
.
For any
x\in U
set

\widetilde{S}^{(b)}_{M,N}(x)=\sharp\{j:1\leq j\leq M\;,\;\sigma^{jN}(x)\in\cup_{r=0}^{N-1}\sigma^{-r}(V_{b})\},

and

\widetilde{U}_{b}(M,N)=\left\{x\in U:\frac{\widetilde{S}^{(b)}_{M,N}(x)}{M}\leq\epsilon_{0}\right\}.

As a consequence of the above we obtain

Corollary 6.6.

For any
b\in{\mathbb{R}}
with
|b|\geq\tilde{b}_{0}
we have

\nu(\widetilde{U}_{b}(M,N))\leq e^{-MNc}

(6.29)

for all integers
M\geq n_{0}(b)
and
N\geq 1
, where
n_{0}(b)
is defined by
(
6.27
).

Proof.
Let
x\in\widetilde{U}_{b}(M,N)
. Then
x\in U_{b}(MN)
. Indeed, if
\Psi^{(b)}_{MN}(x)>\epsilon_{0}\,MN
,
then
\sigma^{k}(x)\in V_{b}
for more than
\epsilon_{0}MN
values of
k=0,1,\ldots,MN-1
.
So, for more than
\epsilon_{0}M
values of

j=1,\ldots,M
there exists
r=0,1,\ldots,N-1
such that
\sigma^{jN+r}(x)\in V_{b}
, that is
\widetilde{S}^{(b)}_{M,N}(x)>\epsilon_{0}M
.
The latter means
x\notin\widetilde{U}_{b}(M,N)
, a contradiction. Thus,
\widetilde{U}_{b}(M,N)\subset U_{b}(MN)
, so (
6.29
) holds.

The above will play an important role in Sect. 8.

6.3
The contraction operators

Let
|b|\geq\tilde{b}_{0}
, where
\tilde{b}_{0}>b_{0}
is as in Lemma 6.4, and let
c=|q_{0}|\epsilon_{0}>0

be the constant from Corollary 6.6
so that (
6.29
) holds for all integers
M\geq n_{0}(b)
and
N\geq 1
.
In what follows we will fix several more objects that will stay the same throughout the rest of the paper.

In what follows we will use the constants
\theta
with (6.1) and
\theta_{1}
defined by (
6.2
)
for some
0<\alpha_{3}<\alpha_{2}<1
.
As in Sect. 6.1 we will also need the constant
\beta_{0}>0
such that
\theta=e^{-\beta_{0}}
.

The constants
\theta_{1}
and
\theta
will stay fixed throughout the rest of the paper.

Remarks 4:
(a)
It follows from (4.2) that
if
X
and
Y
are cylinders in
U
of lengths
\ell_{X}
and
\ell_{Y}
,
X\subset Y

and
\nu(Y)\leq d\,\nu(X)
for some constant
d>0
, then
\ell_{X}\leq\ell_{Y}+d^{\prime}
,
where we can take
d^{\prime}=\frac{1}{|g_{0}|}\log(dc_{2}/c_{1})
.
Here
g=f-P_{f}\tau
is the function from Sect. 4.2 with
g_{0}=\max g<0
, while
c_{1}
and
c_{2}

are the constants from (4.2).
Indeed, take any
x\in X
and use (4.2) to get

d\geq\frac{\nu(Y)}{\nu(X)}\geq\frac{c_{1}\,e^{g_{\ell_{Y}(x)}}}{c_{2}\,e^{g_{\ell_{X}(x)}}}=\frac{c_{1}}{c_{2}\,e^{g_{(\ell_{X}-\ell_{Y})(x)}}}\geq\frac{c_{1}}{c_{2}\,e^{(\ell_{X}-\ell_{Y})\,g_{0}}}.

Therefore
e^{(\ell_{X}-\ell_{Y})\,g_{0}}\geq\frac{c_{1}}{dc_{2}}
, so
(\ell_{X}-\ell_{Y})\,g_{0}\geq\log\frac{c_{1}}{dc_{2}}
.
Thus,
(\ell_{X}-\ell_{Y})|g_{0}|\leq\log\frac{dc_{2}}{c_{1}}
, and therefore
\ell_{X}-\ell_{Y}\leq d^{\prime}
.

(b) Notice also that if
X
is a cylinder in
\widehat{U}
and
d\in(0,1)
is a given constant, then there exists an integer

\ell=\ell(d)\geq 1
, uniquely determined by

, such that if
X_{1},\ldots,X_{k}
are any disjoint sub-cylinders
of
X
with
\sum_{i=1}^{k}\nu(X_{i})\geq d\,\nu(X)
, then the smallest sub-cylinder
Y
of
X
containing

\cup_{i=1}^{k}X_{i}
has co-length at most
\ell_{1}
in
X
. Indeed, for such
Y
we must have
\nu(Y)\geq d\,\nu(X)
,
and as in part (a), this gives an upper bound for
\ell
.

(c) Using the last Remark, there exists a global constant
\ell_{0}
such that if
\widetilde{X}_{1},\ldots,\widetilde{X}_{k}
are disjoint sub-cylinders of

\widetilde{{\mathcal{C}}}_{m}
for some

, and
\sum_{i=1}^{k}\nu(\widetilde{X}_{i})\geq\frac{d_{4}}{8d_{3}}\nu({\mathcal{C}}^{\prime}_{m})
, where
d_{3}>1

and

are as in Lemma 5.5, then the co-length
of the smallest sub-cylinder
\widetilde{Y}
of
\widetilde{{\mathcal{C}}}_{m}
containing
\cup_{i=1}^{k}\widetilde{X}_{i}
is
\leq\ell_{0}
.

Fix
\ell_{0}\geq 1
with this property.

Next, let

, where

is as in Sect. 2. We will also assume that

(6.30)

Again by (

S_{0}e^{-c}=e^{a_{0}NT_{0}-c}\leq e^{-c/2}<1.

Also notice that the above estimate implies

\rho_{3}^{\epsilon_{0}}S_{0}^{1-\epsilon_{0}}=\frac{e^{a_{0}NT_{0}}}{\left(1+\frac{\mu_{0}\,e^{-NT_{0}}}{C_{10}}\right)^{\epsilon_{0}}}<1.

Thus,

(6.40)

Using again

for

Combining it with

, which follows from (

Hence
\rho_{3}^{\epsilon_{0}}S_{0}^{1-\epsilon_{0}}<e^{-4\beta_{4}}
.
As we observed earlier,
S_{0}e^{-c}\leq e^{-c/2}
, so the choice of
\beta_{4}

now yields
S_{0}e^{-c}<e^{-4\beta_{4}}
. Therefore (
6.40
) implies

\rho_{4}<e^{-4\beta_{4}}.

(6.41)

We now apply the construction at the end of the proof of Lemma 5.5.

Given
|b|\geq\tilde{b}_{0}
and

, for any
j=1,\ldots,j_{m}
and any

, using the fixed point

B^{u}(Z_{m},\epsilon^{\prime\prime})\ni x\mapsto v^{(m)}_{i,j}(x)=v^{(m)}_{i,j}(Z_{m},x)\in U,

such that
\sigma^{N}(v^{(m)}_{i,j}(x))=x
for all

and the following property holds:

I_{N}(x^{\prime},z^{\prime})=\left|\psi_{m}(x^{\prime})-\psi_{m}(z^{\prime})\right|\geq\delta_{1}\,e^{-s_{m}\hat{\epsilon}_{13}}\mbox{\rm diam}(\widetilde{{\mathcal{C}}}_{m})

(6.42)

for all

, where

,

, and

\psi_{m}(x)=\tau_{N}(v^{(m)}_{1,j}(x))-\tau_{N}(v^{(m)}_{2,j}(x)).

In the above we use the constant

,

For every

By construction the sets

and

\displaystyle\mbox{\rm Lip}_{\theta}(\omega^{(m,t)}_{i,j})\leq 1/\mbox{\rm diam}_{\theta}(X^{(m,t)}_{i,j}).

The so called contraction operators are now defined similarly to what was done in
[
St5
]
.
A subset
J
of the set

\Pi(b)=\{\;(t,m,i,j)\;:\;t=1,2\;,\;1\leq m\leq m_{0}\;,\;i=1,2\;,\;1\leq j\leq j_{m}\;\;\}

will be called

(6.43)

or all elements of
J
have the form
(2,m,i,j)
and

\sum_{(2,m,i,j)\in J}\nu(\widehat{{\mathcal{D}}}^{(m,2)}_{j})\geq\frac{d_{4}}{8d_{3}}\nu(\widetilde{{\mathcal{C}}}^{\prime}_{m}).

(6.44)

It follows from Remark 4(c) above that
whenever

is representative and its elements are of type
(1,m,i,j)
, then for
the smallest sub-cylinder

of

that contains all

for all

has co-length

in
{\mathcal{C}}^{\prime}_{m}
. Similarly, for

Y
of
\widehat{{\mathcal{D}}}^{(m,2)}
that contains all
\widehat{{\mathcal{D}}}^{(m,2)}_{j}
for all

(2,m,i,j)\in J
has co-length
\leq\ell_{0}
in
{\mathcal{C}}^{\prime}_{m}
.

Let
{\mathcal{J}}(b)
be the
family of all representative subsets

J
of
\Pi(b)
.
Given
J\in{\mathcal{J}}(b)
, define the function
\omega_{J}:\widehat{U}\longrightarrow[0,1]
by

\displaystyle\omega_{J}=1-\mu_{0}\,\sum_{(t,m,i,j)\in J}\omega^{(m,t)}_{i,j}.

Then
\omega_{J}\in{\mathcal{F}}_{\theta}(\widehat{U})
and

\frac{3}{4}\leq 1-\mu_{0}\leq\omega_{J}(u)\leq 1
for all

Define the
contraction operator

{\mathcal{N}}={\mathcal{N}}_{J}(a,b):{\mathcal{F}}_{\theta}(\widehat{U})\longrightarrow{\mathcal{F}}_{\theta}(\widehat{U})\hskip 10.00002pt\mbox{\rm by }\hskip 10.00002pt{\mathcal{N}}h={\mathcal{M}}_{a}^{N}(\omega_{J}\cdot h),

where
