is defined in Sect. 4.2.

Remark 5.
The contraction operators
{\mathcal{N}}_{J}
look similar to the operators
defined and studied by Dolgopyat in Sect. 7 in
[
D1
]
,
although here the construction is necessarily much more complicated. In fact it is significantly more complicated than the constructions
used in
[
St2
]
and
[
St5
]
as well.

We will now prove some basic properties of the contraction operators.
Our exposition here is similar to that in Sect. 6.2 in
[
St5
]
, however there are some substantial differences.

6.4
Estimates for eigenfunctions and Lasota-York type inequality

For any
u,u^{\prime}\in\widehat{U}
, we denote by
\ell(u,u^{\prime})\geq 0

the length of the smallest cylinder
Y(u,u^{\prime})

in
\widehat{U}
containing

u
and
u^{\prime}
. For later use,
notice that for every
p\geq 1
,
\sigma^{p}(Y(u,u^{\prime}))
is the smallest cylinder
15
15

15

Indeed, let
X
be a cylinder in

U
containing both
\sigma^{p}(u)
and
\sigma^{p}(u^{\prime})
and let
X\subset\sigma^{p}(Y(u,u^{\prime}))
. Then every
x\in X

has the form
x=\sigma^{p}(y)
for some
y\in Y(u,u^{\prime})
. Let

Y^{\prime}=\{y\in Y(u,u^{\prime}):\sigma^{p}(y)\in X\}.

Since
X
is a cylinder in
U
,
Y^{\prime}
is a cylinder in
U
, too. Now
u,u^{\prime}\in Y^{\prime}
imply

Y(u,u^{\prime})\subset Y^{\prime}
, therefore
\sigma^{p}(Y(u,u^{\prime}))\subset X
. This proves that

\sigma^{p}(Y(u,u^{\prime}))=Y(\sigma^{p}(u),\sigma^{p}(u^{\prime}))
.

Y(\sigma^{p}(u),\sigma^{p}(u^{\prime}))
in
U
containing both
\sigma^{p}(u)
and
\sigma^{p}(u^{\prime})
.

Consider the following

Assumption:
for points
u,u^{\prime}\in{\mathcal{C}}^{\prime}_{m}
for some
m=1,\ldots,m_{0}(b)
,

i=1,2
,
j=1,\ldots,j_{m}(b)
, an integer
p\geq 0
and points
v,v^{\prime}\in U
we have:

\sigma^{p}(v)=v^{(m)}_{i,j}(u)\;,\;\sigma^{p}(v^{\prime})=v^{(m)}_{i,j}(u^{\prime})\;,\;\ell(v,v^{\prime})\geq p.

(6.45)

Notice that the latter implies
\ell(v,v^{\prime})\geq N+p
,
\sigma^{N+p}(v)=u
and
\sigma^{N+p}(v^{\prime})=u^{\prime}
.

The following estimate is similar to the one in Lemma 6.6 in
[
St5
]
.

Lemma 6.6.

If the points
u,u^{\prime}\in U
, the cylinder
{\mathcal{C}}^{\prime}_{m}
, the integer

p\geq 0
and the points
v,v^{\prime}\in U
satisfy
(
6.45
)
for some
j=1,\ldots,j_{m}
and

i=1,2
, and
w,w^{\prime}\in U
are such that
\sigma^{N}w=v
,

\sigma^{N}w^{\prime}=v^{\prime}
and
\ell(w,w^{\prime})\geq N
, then

|\tau_{N}(w)-\tau_{N}(w^{\prime})|\leq\frac{C_{4}}{d_{0}^{2}}\,\theta^{p+N}\,\mbox{\rm diam}(\widetilde{{\mathcal{C}}}_{m})\leq\frac{C_{1}C_{4}}{d_{0}^{2}}\,\theta^{p+N}\,\mbox{\rm diam}_{\theta}(\widetilde{{\mathcal{C}}}_{m}),

where
d_{0}\in(0,1]
is the constant fixed at the end of Sect. 2 and
C_{4}\geq 1
is the constant from
Lemma 5.3.
If we assume in addition that
u,u^{\prime}\in\widehat{{\mathcal{D}}}^{(m,t)}_{j}
for some
t=1,2
and some
j=1,\ldots,j_{m}
, then

|\tau_{N}(w)-\tau_{N}(w^{\prime})|\leq\frac{C_{4}}{d_{0}^{2}}\,\theta^{p+N}\,\mbox{\rm diam}(\widetilde{{\mathcal{D}}}^{(m,t)}_{j})\leq\frac{C_{1}C_{4}}{d_{0}^{2}}\,\theta^{p+N}\,\mbox{\rm diam}_{\theta}(\widetilde{{\mathcal{D}}}^{(m,t)}_{j}).

Proof.
Assume that the points
u,u^{\prime},v,v^{\prime},w,w^{\prime}
and the cylinder
{\mathcal{C}}^{\prime}_{m}

satisfy the assumptions in the lemma. Clearly,
\ell(w,w^{\prime})\geq p+2N
and

\tau_{N}(w)-\tau_{N}(w^{\prime})=[\tau_{p+2N}(w)-\tau_{p+2N}(w^{\prime})]-[\tau_{p+N}(v)-\tau_{p+N}(v^{\prime})].

(6.46)

Consider now some fixed
j=1,\ldots,j_{m}
and
i=1,2
.
Recall the construction of the map
v^{(m)}_{i,j}
from the proof of Lemma 5.5. In particular by (5.70),

{\mathcal{P}}^{N}(v^{(m)}_{i,j}(u))=\phi_{[-\epsilon_{0},\epsilon_{0}]}(W^{s}_{\epsilon_{0}}(u))\cap W^{u}_{R}(b^{(j)}_{i}),

where we set
b^{(j)}_{i}=b^{(j)}_{i}(Z_{m})\in W^{s}_{R}(Z_{m})
for brevity.
Since
\sigma^{p}(v)=v^{(m)}_{i,j}(u)
and
\sigma^{p}(v^{\prime})=v^{(m)}_{i,j}(u^{\prime})
, we have
\sigma^{p+2N}(w)=\sigma^{p+N}(v)=u
and

\sigma^{p+2N}(w^{\prime})=\sigma^{p+N}(v^{\prime})=u^{\prime}
, so both

x^{\prime}={\mathcal{P}}^{p+N}(v)
and
z^{\prime}={\mathcal{P}}^{p+N}(v^{\prime})
belong to
W^{u}_{R}(b^{\prime})

for some
b^{\prime}\in W^{s}_{R}(Z_{m})
. Then
\pi^{(U)}(x^{\prime})=u
and
\pi^{(U)}(z^{\prime})=u^{\prime}
.
Moreover,
{\mathcal{P}}^{p}(v)\in W^{s}_{R}(v^{(m)}_{i,j}(u))
and the choice of
N
imply (as in the proof of Lemma 5.5) that

d(b^{(j)}_{i},b^{\prime})<\delta^{\prime}
, the constant with (
5.41
) from Lemmas 5.4 and 5.5.
Similarly,

x^{\prime\prime}={\mathcal{P}}^{p+2N}(w)
and
z^{\prime\prime}={\mathcal{P}}^{p+2N}(w^{\prime})
belong to
W^{u}_{R}(b^{\prime\prime})

for some
b^{\prime\prime}\in W^{s}_{R}(Z_{m})
with
d(b^{(j)}_{i},b^{\prime\prime})<\delta^{\prime}
, and
\pi^{(U)}(x^{\prime\prime})=u
,
\pi^{(U)}(z^{\prime\prime})=u^{\prime}
.
Thus,
x^{\prime},x^{\prime\prime}\in W^{s}_{R}(u)
and
z^{\prime},z^{\prime\prime}\in W^{s}_{R}(u^{\prime})
.
Moreover, since the local stable/unstable holonomy maps are uniformly

\alpha_{1}
-Hölder, by the choice of
d_{0}\in(0,1]
at the end of Sect. 2,

d(b^{\prime},b^{\prime\prime})\leq\frac{1}{d_{0}}(d({\mathcal{P}}^{p+N}(v),{\mathcal{P}}^{p+2N}(w)))^{\alpha_{1}}.

Using this and (2.1) for points on local stable manifolds, i.e. going backwards along the flow, we get

d(b^{\prime},b^{\prime\prime})\leq\frac{1}{d_{0}}(d({\mathcal{P}}^{p+N}(v),{\mathcal{P}}^{p+2N}(w)))^{\alpha_{1}}\leq\frac{1}{d_{0}}\,\left(\frac{d(v,{\mathcal{P}}^{N}(w))}{d_{0}\gamma^{p+N}}\right)^{\alpha_{1}}\leq\frac{1}{d_{0}^{2}\gamma^{\alpha_{1}(p+N)}}.

(6.47)

By the choice of
\theta
(see Sect. 6.1) we have
\displaystyle\frac{1}{\gamma^{\alpha_{1}\beta}}\leq\theta
, hence

(d(b^{\prime},b^{\prime\prime}))^{\beta}\leq(1/d_{0}^{2})^{\beta}(1/\gamma^{p+N})^{\alpha_{1}\beta}\leq\frac{\theta^{p+N}}{d_{0}^{2}}.

We are preparing to use Lemma 5.3. Let
\tilde{u}\in\widetilde{R}
and
\tilde{u}^{\prime}\in\widetilde{R}
be the shifts along the flow of
the points
\pi_{Z_{m}}(u)
and
\pi_{Z_{m}}(u^{\prime})
.
Then we have
\tilde{u}=\phi_{t(u)}(\pi_{Z_{m}}(u))
and
\tilde{u}^{\prime}=\phi_{t(u^{\prime})}(\pi_{Z_{m}}(u^{\prime}))

for some small
t(u),t(u^{\prime})\in{\mathbb{R}}
. So

\displaystyle\tau_{p+N}(v)-\tau_{p+N}(v^{\prime})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\Delta({\mathcal{P}}^{p+N}(v),{\mathcal{P}}^{p+N}(v^{\prime}))=\Delta(x^{\prime},z^{\prime})=\Delta(u,\pi_{b^{\prime}}(u^{\prime}))

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\Delta(\pi_{Z_{m}}(u),\pi_{b^{\prime}}(\pi_{Z_{m}}(u^{\prime})))=\Delta(\tilde{u},\pi_{b^{\prime}}(\tilde{u}^{\prime}))+t(u)-t(u^{\prime}),

and similarly

\displaystyle\tau_{p+2N}(w)-\tau_{p+2N}(w^{\prime})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\Delta({\mathcal{P}}^{p+2N}(w),{\mathcal{P}}^{p+2N}(w^{\prime}))=\Delta(\tilde{u},\pi_{b^{\prime\prime}}(\tilde{u}^{\prime}))+t(u)-t(u^{\prime}).

This, the above estimate and Lemma 5.3(a) yield

\displaystyle|\tau_{N}(w)-\tau_{N}(w^{\prime})|=|\Delta(\tilde{u},\pi_{b^{\prime}}(\tilde{u}^{\prime}))-\Delta(\tilde{u},\pi_{b^{\prime\prime}}(\tilde{u}^{\prime}))|\leq C_{4}\mbox{\rm diam}(\widetilde{{\mathcal{C}}}_{m})\,(d(b^{\prime},b^{\prime\prime}))^{\beta}\leq\frac{C_{4}}{d_{0}^{2}}\theta^{p+N}\mbox{\rm diam}(\widetilde{{\mathcal{C}}}_{m}).

Finally, by Lemma 4.1 we have
\mbox{\rm diam}(\widetilde{{\mathcal{C}}}_{m})\leq C_{1}\mbox{\rm diam}_{\theta}(\widetilde{{\mathcal{C}}}_{m})
.

Next, assume that
u,u^{\prime}\in\widehat{{\mathcal{D}}}^{(m,t)}
for some
t
. We can then apply the above argument
replacing
{\mathcal{C}}^{\prime}_{m}
by
\widehat{{\mathcal{D}}}^{(m,t)}_{j}
to get

\displaystyle|\tau_{N}(w)-\tau_{N}(w^{\prime})|\leq\frac{C_{4}}{d_{0}^{2}}\,\theta^{p+N}\,\mbox{\rm diam}(\widetilde{{\mathcal{D}}}^{(m,t)}_{j}).

This proves the lemma.

Set

\displaystyle E_{1}=\frac{E}{120},

where
E
is as in (
6.32
). Then
E_{1}\geq C_{8}e^{C_{8}}
, where

C_{8}=\frac{T_{0}C_{7}C_{4}}{1-\theta_{1}}+\frac{M_{1}C_{4}}{d_{0}^{2}}
, as defined in Sect. 6.3.
Here
M_{1}
is a constant so that
M_{1}\geq 2|P_{f}|+1
(see Sect. 4.2 for
P_{f}
).

Denote by
{\mathcal{K}}_{0}

the set of all
h\in{\mathcal{F}}_{\theta}(U)
such that
h\geq 0
on
U
and for any

u,u^{\prime}\in U
contained in some cylinder
\widetilde{{\mathcal{D}}}^{(m,t)}_{j}
for some
t=1,2
, some
1\leq m\leq m_{0}

and some
j=1,\ldots,j_{m}
,
any integer
p\geq 0
and any points
v,v^{\prime}\in U
satisfying
(
6.45
)
we have

|h(v)-h(v^{\prime})|\leq E_{1}\,\theta^{p+N}\,h(v^{\prime})\,\mbox{\rm diam}_{\theta}(\widetilde{{\mathcal{D}}}^{(m,t)}_{j}).

(6.48)

It turns out that the eigenfunctions
h_{a}\in{\mathcal{K}}_{0}
for
|a|\leq a_{0}
(see Sect. 4.2).
This follows from the following lemma whose proof is almost identical, modulo the different assumption
in (
6.45
), with that of Lemma 6.7 in
[
St5
]
.
See Appendix I for a sketch of the proof.

Lemma 6.7.

For any real constant
s
with
|s|\leq M_{1}
we have

L^{mN}_{f-s\tau}({\mathcal{K}}_{0})\subset{\mathcal{K}}_{0}
for all
m\geq 1
.

Corollary 6.8.

For any real constant
a
with
|a|\leq a_{0}
we have
h_{a}\in{\mathcal{K}}_{0}
.

Proof.
Let
|a|\leq a_{0}
. Since the constant function
h=1\in{\mathcal{K}}_{0}
, it follows from
Lemma 6.7 that
L^{mN}_{f-(P+a)\tau}1\in{\mathcal{K}}_{0}
for all
m\geq 1
. Now the Ruelle-Perron-Frobenius
Theorem (see e.g.
[
PP
]
) and the fact that
{\mathcal{K}}_{0}
is closed in
{\mathcal{F}}_{\theta}(\widehat{U})
imply
h_{a}\in{\mathcal{K}}_{0}
.

The following lemma is similar to Lemma 6.4 in
[
St5
]
.

Lemma 6.9.

Assume that
u,u^{\prime}\in\widehat{U}
,
u\neq u^{\prime}
, and
\sigma^{N}(v)=u
,
\sigma^{N}(v^{\prime})=u^{\prime}

for some
v,v^{\prime}\in\widehat{U}
with
\ell(v,v^{\prime})\geq N
.
Let
\omega_{J}(v)<1
and
\omega_{J}(v^{\prime})=1
for some
J\in{\mathcal{J}}(b)
. Then

\displaystyle|\omega_{J}(v)-\omega_{J}(v^{\prime})|\leq\frac{\mu_{0}\theta_{1}^{-\ell_{0}}}{\epsilon_{4}}\,|b|\,D_{\theta_{1}}(u,u^{\prime}),

where
\ell_{0}\geq 1
is the constant from Remark
4(c).

Proof.
Under the given assumption we have
|\omega_{J}(v)-\omega_{J}(v^{\prime})|=\mu_{0}
.
Assume e.g. that all elements of
J
have the form
(1,m,i,j)
and (
6.43
) holds.
Let
\omega_{J}(v)<1
; then
v\in X^{(m,t)}_{1,j}
for some
(1,m,i,j)\in J
, so
u=\sigma^{N}(v)\in\widetilde{{\mathcal{D}}}^{(m,t)}_{j}
.
However
u^{\prime}=\sigma^{N}(v^{\prime})\notin\widetilde{{\mathcal{D}}}^{(m,t)}_{j^{\prime}}
for any
j^{\prime}
with
(1,m,i,j^{\prime})\in J
.
Using (
6.43
), it follows from Remark 4(c) that
the smallest sub-cylinder
X
of
\widehat{{\mathcal{D}}}^{(m,1)}
that contains all
\widehat{{\mathcal{D}}}^{(m,1)}_{j}
for all

(1,m,i,j)\in J
has co-length
\leq\ell_{0}
in
{\mathcal{C}}^{\prime}_{m}
. Since
u^{\prime}\notin X
and
u\in X
, it follows from (
6.7
) that

D_{\theta_{1}}(u,u^{\prime})\geq\mbox{\rm diam}_{\theta_{1}}(X)\geq\theta_{1}^{\ell_{0}}\mbox{\rm diam}_{\theta_{1}}(\widetilde{{\mathcal{C}}}_{m})\geq\frac{\theta_{1}^{\ell_{0}}\,\epsilon_{4}}{|b|}.

Hence

\displaystyle|\omega_{J}(v)-\omega_{J}(v^{\prime})|=\mu_{0}\,\frac{D_{\theta_{1}}(u,u^{\prime})}{D_{\theta_{1}}(u,u^{\prime})}\leq\mu_{0}\frac{D_{\theta_{1}}(u,u^{\prime})}{\theta_{1}^{\ell_{0}}\,\epsilon_{4}/|b|}\leq\frac{\mu_{0}\theta_{1}^{-\ell_{0}}}{\epsilon_{4}}|b|\,D_{\theta_{1}}(u,u^{\prime}).

This proves the lemma.

Recall the large constant
E
with (
6.32
).

Definition 6.10.

Denote by
{\mathcal{K}}_{E|b|}
be
the set of all functions
H\in{\mathcal{F}}_{\theta}(\widehat{U})
such that

0<H\leq 1
on
\widehat{U}
and

\frac{|H(u)-H(u^{\prime})|}{H(u^{\prime})}\leq E\,|b|D_{\theta_{1}}(u,u^{\prime})

for all
u,u^{\prime}\in\widehat{U}
.

We can now derive a Lasota-Yorke type inequality for functions in
{\mathcal{K}}_{E|b|}
.
Its proof is similar to that of Proposition 6 in
[
D1
]
and that of Lemma 5.6 in
[
St2
]
.
We prove it in Appendix I for completeness.

Lemma 6.11.

Let
f\in{\mathcal{F}}_{\theta_{1}}(\widehat{U})
. Then for any
J\in{\mathcal{J}}(b)
we have
{\mathcal{N}}_{J}({\mathcal{K}}_{E|b|})\subset{\mathcal{K}}_{E|b|}
.

7
Iteration procedure – the role of the contact structure

We continue here with the notation and the assumptions in Sect. 6.
Let
|b|\geq\tilde{b}_{0}
.

Denote by
{\mathcal{K}}_{b}
the set of all pairs
(h,H)
such that
h\in{\mathcal{F}}_{\theta}(\widehat{U})
,

H\in{\mathcal{K}}_{E|b|}
, and the following two conditions are satisfied:

(T1)

|h|\leq H\leq 1
on
\widehat{U}
,

(T2)
for any
u,u^{\prime}\in\widehat{{\mathcal{D}}}^{(m,t)}_{j}=\widehat{{\mathcal{D}}}^{(m,t)}_{j}(b)
for some
m=1,\ldots,m_{0}
, some
t=1,2

and some
j=1,\ldots,j_{m}
, any integer

p\geq 0
and any points
v,v^{\prime}\in\widehat{U}
satisfying (
6.45
) for
m
we have

|h(v)-h(v^{\prime})|\leq E\,|b|\,\theta^{p+N}\,H(v^{\prime})\,\mbox{\rm diam}_{\theta}(\widetilde{{\mathcal{D}}}^{(m,t)}_{j}).

(7.1)

Notice that (
6.36
) implies

E\,|b|\,\mbox{\rm diam}_{\theta}(\widetilde{{\mathcal{D}}}^{(m,t)}_{j})<\frac{\epsilon_{3}}{32}.

(7.2)

The following lemma is fundamental for the iteration procedure that will be used in Sect. 8 which will show that
the so called contraction operators are ”eventually” contracting. The idea behind all this is in Lemma
10^{\prime\prime}
in
[
D1
]

(see Sects. 6, 7 and 8 in
[
D1
]
), although here we have to proceed in a slightly more complicated way.

Lemma 7.1.

For any
|a|\leq a_{0}
, any
|b|\geq\tilde{b}_{0}
, and any
(h,H)\in{\mathcal{K}}_{b}

there exists
J\in{\mathcal{J}}(b)
such that
(L_{ab}^{N}h,{\mathcal{N}}_{J}H)\in{\mathcal{K}}_{b}
.

To prove this we need the following lemma, whose proof is very similar to that of
Lemma 14 in
[
D1
]
(and essentially the same as that of Lemma 6.10 in
[
St5
]
).
For completeness we prove it in Appendix I.

Lemma 7.2.

Let
(h,H)\in{\mathcal{K}}_{b}
. Then for any
m=1,\ldots,m_{0}
, any
j=1,\ldots,j_{m}
, any
t=1,2

and any
i=1,2
we have:

(a)
\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\leq\frac{H(v^{(m)}_{i,j}(u^{\prime}))}{H(v^{(m)}_{i,j}(u^{\prime\prime}))}\leq 2
for all

u^{\prime},u^{\prime\prime}\in\widehat{{\mathcal{D}}}^{(m,t)}_{j}
;

(b)
Either
H(v^{(m)}_{1,j}(u))\geq H(v^{(m)}_{2,j}(u))/4
for all
u\in\widehat{{\mathcal{D}}}^{(m,t)}_{j}
or
H(v^{(m)}_{2,j}(u))\geq H(v^{(m)}_{1,j}(u))/4

for all
u\in\widehat{{\mathcal{D}}}^{(m,t)}_{j}
.

(c)
Either for all
u\in\widehat{{\mathcal{D}}}^{(m,t)}_{j}
we have

|h(v^{(m)}_{i,j}(u))|\leq\frac{3}{4}H(v^{(m)}_{i,j}(u))
, or
|h(v^{(m)}_{i,j}(u))|\geq\frac{1}{4}H(v^{(m)}_{i,j}(u))

for all
u\in\widehat{{\mathcal{D}}}^{(m,t)}_{j}
.

Proof of Lemma
7.1.
Let
|a|\leq a_{0}
,
|b|\geq\tilde{b}_{0}
and let
(h,H)\in{\mathcal{K}}_{b}
. We will construct a representative set

J\in{\mathcal{J}}(b)
such that
(L_{ab}^{N}h,{\mathcal{N}}_{J}H)\in{\mathcal{K}}_{b}
.

Notice that, since
1/2\leq\omega_{J}\leq 1
, we have

\frac{1}{2}{\mathcal{M}}_{a}^{N}(H)\leq{\mathcal{N}}_{J}(H)={\mathcal{M}}_{a}^{N}(\omega_{J}\,H)
.

Consider for a moment an arbitrary (at this stage) representative set
J\in{\mathcal{J}}(b)
.
We will first show that
(L_{ab}^{N}h,{\mathcal{N}}_{J}H)
has property
(T2)
. This is done as in the proof
of Lemma 6.9 in
[
St5
]
.

Assume that the points
u,u^{\prime}
, the cylinder
\widetilde{{\mathcal{D}}}^{(m,t)}_{j}
in
U
, the integer
p\geq 0
and the points

v,v^{\prime}\in\widehat{U}
are satisfying (
6.45
) and
(
7.1
) for some
t=1,2
,
m=1,\ldots,m_{0}
and
j=1,\ldots,j_{m}
.

From the definition of
f^{(a)}
, for any
w,w^{\prime}
with
\sigma^{N}w=v
,
\sigma^{N}(w^{\prime})=v^{\prime}
and
\ell(w,w^{\prime})\geq N
we have

f^{(a)}_{N}(w)=f_{N}(w)-(P+a)\tau_{N}(w)+\ln h_{a}(w)-\ln h_{a}(v)-N\lambda_{a}.

Since
h_{a}\in{\mathcal{K}}_{0}
by Corollary 6.8,

\displaystyle|\ln h_{a}(w)-\ln h_{a}(w^{\prime})|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{|h_{a}(w)-h_{a}(w^{\prime})|}{\min\{|h_{a}(w)|,|h_{a}(w^{\prime})|\}}\leq E_{1}\,\theta^{p+2N}\,\mbox{\rm diam}_{\theta}(\widetilde{{\mathcal{D}}}^{(m,t)}_{j}).

and similarly,
|\ln h_{a}(v)-\ln h_{a}(v^{\prime})|\leq E_{1}\,\theta^{p+N}\,\mbox{\rm diam}_{\theta}(\widetilde{{\mathcal{D}}}^{(m,t)}_{j})
.
Using
|f|_{\theta}\leq T_{0}
, we get

\displaystyle|f_{N}(w)-f_{N}(w^{\prime})|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sum_{j=0}^{N-1}|f(\sigma^{j}(w))-f(\sigma^{j}(w^{\prime}))|\leq\sum_{j=0}^{N-1}|f|_{\theta}\,\theta^{N-j}\,D_{\theta}(v,v^{\prime})

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{T_{0}}{1-\theta}\,\theta^{p+N}\,D_{\theta}(u,u^{\prime})\leq\frac{T_{0}}{1-\theta}\theta^{p+N}\,\mbox{\rm diam}_{\theta}(\widetilde{{\mathcal{D}}}^{(m,t)}_{j})

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle E_{1}\,\theta^{p+N}\,\mbox{\rm diam}_{\theta}(\widetilde{{\mathcal{D}}}^{(m,t)}_{j}).

Apart from that it follows from Lemma 6.6 and the choice of
E_{1}
that

|P+a|\,|\tau_{N}(w)-\tau_{N}(w^{\prime})|\leq\frac{2|P+a|\,C_{4}}{d_{0}^{2}}\,\theta^{p+N}\,\mbox{\rm diam}_{\theta}(\widetilde{{\mathcal{D}}}^{(m,t)}_{j})\leq E_{1}\,\theta^{p+N}\,\mbox{\rm diam}_{\theta}(\widetilde{{\mathcal{D}}}^{(m,t)}_{j}).

Using the above we get

\displaystyle|f^{(a)}_{N}(w)-f^{(a)}_{N}(w^{\prime})|\leq 4E_{1}\,\theta^{p+N}\,\mbox{\rm diam}_{\theta}(\widetilde{{\mathcal{D}}}^{(m,t)}_{j})<\frac{\epsilon_{3}}{32},

(7.3)

using (
7.2
) for the last estimate. This implies

\left|(f^{(a)}_{N}-{\bf i}b\tau_{N})(w)-(f^{(a)}_{N}-{\bf i}b\tau_{N})(w^{\prime})\right|\leq\left(4E_{1}+E_{1}\,|b|\right)\,\theta^{p+N}\,\mbox{\rm diam}_{\theta}(\widetilde{{\mathcal{D}}}^{(m,t)}_{j})<\frac{\epsilon_{3}}{16}<\epsilon_{3},

using (
7.2
) again for the latter.

Notice that (7.1) for the pair
w,w^{\prime}
gives

|h(w)-h(w^{\prime})|\leq E\,|b|\,\theta^{p+2N}\,H(w^{\prime})\,\mbox{\rm diam}_{\theta}(\widetilde{{\mathcal{D}}}^{(m,t)}_{j}).

Using this and (7.3) we now derive (as we did in the proof of Lemma 6.9 in
[
St5
]
)

\displaystyle|(L_{ab}^{N}h)(v)-(L_{ab}^{N}h)(v^{\prime})|=\left|\sum_{\sigma^{N}w=v}e^{(f^{(a)}_{N}-{\bf i}b\tau_{N})(w)}\,h(w)-\sum_{\sigma^{N}w=v}e^{(f^{(a)}_{N}-{\bf i}b\tau_{N})(w^{\prime}(w))}\,h(w^{\prime}(w))\right|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\left|\sum_{\sigma^{N}w=v}e^{(f^{(a)}_{N}-{\bf i}b\tau_{N})(w)}\,[h(w)-h(w^{\prime})]\right|+\sum_{\sigma^{N}w=v}\left|e^{(f^{(a)}_{N}-{\bf i}b\tau_{N})(w)}-e^{(f^{(a)}_{N}-{\bf i}b\tau_{N})(w^{\prime})}\right|\,|h(w^{\prime})|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sum_{\sigma^{N}w=v}e^{(f^{(a)}_{N}(w)-f^{(a)}_{N}(w^{\prime})}e^{f^{(a)}_{N}(w^{\prime})}\,E|b|\,\theta^{p+2N}\,H(w^{\prime})\,\mbox{\rm diam}_{\theta}(\widetilde{{\mathcal{D}}}^{(m,t)}_{j})

\displaystyle+\sum_{\sigma^{N}w=v}\left|e^{(f^{(a)}_{N}-{\bf i}b\tau_{N})(w)-(f^{(a)}_{N}-{\bf i}b\tau_{N})(w^{\prime})}-1\right|\,e^{f^{(a)}_{N}(w^{\prime})}H(w^{\prime})

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle e^{\epsilon_{3}}\,E|b|\,\theta^{p+2N}\,\mbox{\rm diam}_{\theta}(\widetilde{{\mathcal{D}}}^{(m,t)}_{j})\,({\mathcal{M}}_{a}^{N}H)(v^{\prime})+e^{\epsilon_{3}}\,(4E_{1}+E_{1}|b|)\theta^{p+N}\,\mbox{\rm diam}_{\theta}(\widetilde{{\mathcal{D}}}^{(m,t)}_{j})\,({\mathcal{M}}_{a}^{N}H)(v^{\prime}).

As remarked earlier,
{\mathcal{M}}_{a}^{N}H\leq 2{\mathcal{M}}_{a}^{N}(\omega_{J}H)=2{\mathcal{N}}_{J}H
.
This and the above yield

\displaystyle|(L_{ab}^{N}h)(v)-(L_{ab}^{N}h)(v^{\prime})|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 3E|b|\,\theta^{p+2N}\,\mbox{\rm diam}_{\theta}(\widetilde{{\mathcal{D}}}^{(m,t)}_{j})\,2({\mathcal{N}}_{J}H)(v^{\prime})

\displaystyle+30E_{1}\,|b|\,\theta^{p+N}\,\mbox{\rm diam}_{\theta}(\widetilde{{\mathcal{D}}}^{(m,t)}_{j})\,2({\mathcal{N}}_{J}H)(v^{\prime})

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle(6\,\theta^{N}+60E_{1}/E)\,E\,|b|\,\theta^{p+N}\,\mbox{\rm diam}_{\theta}(\widetilde{{\mathcal{D}}}^{(m,t)}_{j})\,({\mathcal{N}}_{J}H)(v^{\prime})

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle E\,|b|\,\theta^{p+N}\,\mbox{\rm diam}_{\theta}(\widetilde{{\mathcal{D}}}^{(m,t)}_{j})\,({\mathcal{N}}_{J}H)(v^{\prime}),

since
6\,\theta^{N}+60E_{1}/E\leq 1
by (
6.33
) and the choice of
E_{1}
.
Thus,
(L_{ab}^{N}h,{\mathcal{N}}_{J}H)
has property
(T2)
.

So far the choice of
J
was not important. We will now construct a representative set
J={\mathcal{J}}(b)
so that

(L_{ab}^{N}h,{\mathcal{N}}_{J}H)
has property
(T1)
, namely

|L_{ab}^{N}h|(u)\leq({\mathcal{N}}_{J}H)(u)

(7.4)

for all
u\in\widehat{U}
.

Notice that (
7.4
) is trivially satisfied for
u\notin V_{b}
for any choice of
J\in{\mathcal{J}}(b)
.
So we need to deal with those
u
that belong to
{\mathcal{C}}^{\prime}_{m}={\mathcal{C}}^{\prime}_{m}(b)
for some
m=1,\ldots,m_{0}=m_{0}(b)
.

Fix an arbitrary
m=1,\ldots,m_{0}
.

We will construct a family of triples
(t,i,j)
with
t=1,2
,
i=1,2
and
j=1,\ldots,j_{m}

so that
(t,m,i,j)
will be included in
J
, namely a family which satisfies (
6.43
)
or (
6.44
).
That is, for the given
m
, we need to construct either a family

{\mathcal{F}}^{(1)}_{m}\subset\{(1,i,j):i=1,2\;;\;1\leq j\leq j_{m}\},

such that

\sum_{(1,m,i,j)\in{\mathcal{F}}^{(1)}_{m}}\nu(\widehat{{\mathcal{D}}}^{(m,1)}_{j})\geq\frac{d_{4}}{8d_{3}}\nu(\widetilde{{\mathcal{C}}}^{\prime}_{m}),

(7.5)

and (
7.4
) holds for all
u\in\widehat{{\mathcal{D}}}^{(m,1)}_{j}
whenever
(1,i,j)\in{\mathcal{F}}_{m}
,
or a family

{\mathcal{F}}^{(2)}_{m}\subset\{(2,i,j):i=1,2\;;\;1\leq j\leq j_{m}\},

such that

\sum_{(2,m,i,j)\in{\mathcal{F}}^{(2)}_{m}}\nu(\widehat{{\mathcal{D}}}^{(m,2)}_{j})\geq\frac{d_{4}}{8d_{3}}\nu(\widetilde{{\mathcal{C}}}^{\prime}_{m}),

(7.6)

and (
7.4
) holds for all
u\in\widehat{{\mathcal{D}}}^{(m,1)}_{j}
whenever
(2,i,j)\in{\mathcal{F}}_{m}
.

Once
{\mathcal{F}}^{(1)}_{m}
or
{\mathcal{F}}^{(2)}_{m}
is constructed, we will set
J={\mathcal{F}}^{(1)}_{m}
or
J={\mathcal{F}}^{(2)}_{m}
, respectively.
Set

\widetilde{{\mathcal{F}}}_{m}=\{(t,i,j):t=1,2\;,\;i=1,2\;;\;1\leq j\leq j_{m}\}.

Define the functions
\tilde{\psi}_{m},\gamma^{(1)}_{m},\gamma^{(2)}_{m}:\widehat{U}\longrightarrow{\mathbb{C}}
by

\displaystyle\tilde{\psi}_{m}(u)=e^{(f^{(a)}_{N}+{\bf i}b\tau_{N})(v^{(m)}_{1,j}(u))}h(v^{(m)}_{1,j}(u))+e^{(f^{(a)}_{N}+{\bf i}b\tau_{N})(v^{(m)}_{2,j}(u))}h(v^{(m)}_{2,j}(u)),

\displaystyle\gamma^{(1)}_{m}(u)=(1-\mu_{0})\,e^{f^{(a)}_{N}(v^{(m)}_{1,j}(u))}H(v^{(m)}_{1,j}(u))+e^{f^{(a)}_{N}(v^{(m)}_{2,j}(u))}H(v^{(m)}_{2,j}(u)),

while
\gamma^{(2)}_{m}(u)
is defined similarly with a coefficient
(1-\mu_{0})

in front of the second term.
Recall the functions

\displaystyle\psi_{m}(u)=\tau_{N}(v^{(m)}_{1,j}(u))-\tau_{N}(v^{(m)}_{2,j}(u))\hskip 10.00002pt,\hskip 10.00002ptu\in U,

that appear in (
6.42
).

Next, denote by
{\mathcal{F}}^{\prime}_{m}
the set of those
(t,i,j)\in\widetilde{{\mathcal{F}}}_{m}
so that
the first alternative in Lemma 7.2(c) holds for
t,i,j
, and by
{\mathcal{F}}^{\prime\prime}_{m}
the set of those
(t,i,j)\in\widetilde{{\mathcal{F}}}_{m}\setminus{\mathcal{F}}^{\prime}_{m}
so that
the second alternative in Lemma 7.2(c) holds for
t,i,j
.

Case 1.
Assume that

\displaystyle\sum_{(1,1,j)\in{\mathcal{F}}^{\prime}_{m}}\nu(\widehat{{\mathcal{D}}}^{(m,1)}_{j})\geq\frac{d_{4}}{8d_{3}}\nu(\widetilde{{\mathcal{C}}}^{\prime}_{m})
.
Consider an arbitrary
(1,1,j)\in{\mathcal{F}}^{\prime}_{m}
. Given
u\in\widehat{{\mathcal{D}}}^{(m,1)}_{j}
,
1-\mu_{0}\geq 3/4
and
|h(v^{(m)}_{1,j}(u))|\leq\frac{3}{4}H(v^{(m)}_{1,j}(u))

imply
|\tilde{\psi}_{m}(u)|\leq\gamma^{(1)}_{m}(u)
. So if
(1,m,1,j)\in J
with
(1,1,j)\in{\mathcal{F}}^{\prime}_{m}
, then

\displaystyle\left|(L_{ab}^{N}h)(u)\right|\leq\left|\sum_{\sigma^{N}v=u,\;v\neq v^{(m)}_{1,j}(u),v^{(m)}_{2,j}(u)}e^{(f^{(a)}_{N}+{\bf i}b\tau_{N})(v)}h(v)\right|+|\tilde{\psi}_{m}(u)|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sum_{\sigma^{N}v=u,\;v\neq v^{(m)}_{1,j}(u),v^{(m)}_{2,j}(u)}e^{f^{(a)}_{N}(v)}|h(v)|+\gamma^{(1)}_{m}(u)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sum_{\sigma^{N}v=u,\;v\neq v^{(m)}_{1,j}(u),v^{(m)}_{2,j}(u)}e^{f^{(a)}_{N}(v)}\omega_{J}(v)H(v)

\displaystyle+\left[e^{f^{(a)}_{N}(v_{1}(u))}\omega_{J}(v^{(m)}_{1,j}(u))H(v^{(m)}_{1,j}(u))+e^{f^{(a)}_{N}(v^{(m)}_{2,j}(u))}\omega_{J}(v^{(m)}_{2,j}(u))H(v^{(m)}_{2,j}(u))\right]\leq({\mathcal{N}}_{J}H)(u).

Thus, in this case we can simply take
{\mathcal{F}}^{(1)}_{m}=\{(1,j):(1,1,j)\in{\mathcal{F}}^{\prime}_{m}\}
and then (
7.4
) and (
7.5
) will be satisfied.

Case 2.
Assume that

\displaystyle\sum_{(1,1,j)\in{\mathcal{F}}^{\prime}_{m}}\nu(\widehat{{\mathcal{D}}}^{(m,1)}_{j})<\frac{d_{4}}{8d_{3}}\nu(\widetilde{{\mathcal{C}}}^{\prime}_{m})
.
Since
\sum_{j=1}^{j_{m}}\nu(\widehat{{\mathcal{D}}}^{(m,1)}_{j})\geq d_{4}\nu(\widetilde{{\mathcal{C}}}^{\prime}_{m})
by (
6.35
) and
(1,i,j)\notin{\mathcal{F}}^{\prime}_{m}

implies
(1,i,j)\in{\mathcal{F}}^{\prime\prime}_{m}
, it follows that

\sum_{(1,1,j)\in{\mathcal{F}}^{\prime\prime}_{m}}\nu(\widehat{{\mathcal{D}}}^{(m,1)}_{j})\geq\frac{7d_{4}}{8}\nu(\widetilde{{\mathcal{C}}}^{\prime}_{m}).

(7.7)

Sub-case 2.1.
Assume that

\sum_{(1,2,j)\in{\mathcal{F}}^{\prime}_{m}}\nu(\widehat{{\mathcal{D}}}^{(m,1)}_{j})\geq\frac{d_{4}}{8d_{3}}\nu(\widetilde{{\mathcal{C}}}^{\prime}_{m}).

(7.8)

As in Case 1 one shows that for all
j
with
(1,2,j)\in{\mathcal{F}}^{\prime}_{m}
, including
(1,2,m,j)
in
J
, we have

\left|(L_{ab}^{N}h)(u)\right|\leq({\mathcal{N}}_{J}H)(u)
for all
u\in\widehat{{\mathcal{D}}}^{(m,1)}_{j}
.
Thus, setting

{\mathcal{F}}^{(1)}_{m}=\{(1,2,j):(1,2,j)\in{\mathcal{F}}^{\prime}_{m}\},

(
7.4
) and (
7.5
) will be satisfied.

Sub-case 2.2.
Assume that (7.8) does not hold, that is

\displaystyle\sum_{(1,2,j)\in{\mathcal{F}}^{\prime}_{m}}\nu(\widehat{{\mathcal{D}}}^{(m,1)}_{j})<\frac{d_{4}}{8d_{3}}\nu(\widetilde{{\mathcal{C}}}^{\prime}_{m})
.
It follows now that

\sum_{(1,1,j)\in{\mathcal{F}}^{\prime}_{m}}\nu(\widehat{{\mathcal{D}}}^{(m,1)}_{j})+\sum_{(1,2,j)\in{\mathcal{F}}^{\prime}_{m}}\nu(\widehat{{\mathcal{D}}}^{(m,1)}_{j})<\frac{d_{4}}{4d_{3}}\nu(\widetilde{{\mathcal{C}}}^{\prime}_{m}).

Using (
6.37
) we can write

\sum_{(1,1,j)\in{\mathcal{F}}^{\prime}_{m}}\nu(\widehat{{\mathcal{D}}}^{(m,2)}_{j})+\sum_{(1,2,j)\in{\mathcal{F}}^{\prime}_{m}}\nu(\widehat{{\mathcal{D}}}^{(m,2)}_{j})<\frac{d_{4}}{4}\nu(\widetilde{{\mathcal{C}}}^{\prime}_{m}).

(7.9)

Denote by
J^{\prime}_{m}
the set of those
j\in J_{m}
such that both
(1,1,j)\in{\mathcal{F}}^{\prime\prime}_{m}
and
(1,2,j)\in{\mathcal{F}}^{\prime\prime}_{m}
.
Then the above and (
6.35
) with
t=1
imply

\sum_{j\in J^{\prime}_{m}}\nu(\widehat{{\mathcal{D}}}^{(m,2)}_{j})>\frac{3d_{4}}{4}\nu(\widetilde{{\mathcal{C}}}^{\prime}_{m}).

(7.10)

In what follows we assume (7.10).

Sub-case 2.2.1.
Assume now that

\displaystyle\sum_{(2,1,j)\in{\mathcal{F}}^{\prime}_{m}}\nu(\widehat{{\mathcal{D}}}^{(m,2)}_{j})\geq\frac{d_{4}}{8d_{3}}\nu(\widetilde{{\mathcal{C}}}^{\prime}_{m}).

Consider an arbitrary
(2,1,j)\in{\mathcal{F}}^{\prime}_{m}
. Given
u\in\widehat{{\mathcal{D}}}^{(m,2)}_{j}
,
1-\mu_{0}\geq 3/4
and
|h(v^{(m)}_{1,j}(u))|\leq\frac{3}{4}H(v^{(m)}_{1,j}(u))

imply
|\tilde{\psi}_{m}(u)|\leq\gamma^{(1)}_{m}(u)
. So if
(2,m,1,j)\in J
with
(2,1,j)\in{\mathcal{F}}^{\prime}_{m}
, then as in Case 1 we derive

\left|(L_{ab}^{N}h)(u)\right|\leq({\mathcal{N}}_{J}H)(u)
.
Thus, in this case we can take
{\mathcal{F}}^{(1)}_{m}=\{(1,j):(2,1,j)\in{\mathcal{F}}^{\prime}_{m}\}
and then (
7.4
) and (
7.5
) will be satisfied.

Sub-case 2.2.2.
Assume that

\displaystyle\sum_{(2,1,j)\in{\mathcal{F}}^{\prime}_{m}}\nu(\widehat{{\mathcal{D}}}^{(m,2)}_{j})<\frac{d_{4}}{8d_{3}}\nu(\widetilde{{\mathcal{C}}}^{\prime}_{m}).

(7.11)

Next, consider the case when

\displaystyle\sum_{(2,2,j)\in{\mathcal{F}}^{\prime}_{m}}\nu(\widehat{{\mathcal{D}}}^{(m,2)}_{j})\geq\frac{d_{4}}{8d_{3}}\nu(\widetilde{{\mathcal{C}}}^{\prime}_{m}).

(7.12)

As in Sub-case 2.2.1 we observe that if we include in
J
those
(2,m,2,j)\in J
with
(2,2,j)\in{\mathcal{F}}^{\prime}_{m}
, then we derive

\left|(L_{ab}^{N}h)(u)\right|\leq({\mathcal{N}}_{J}H)(u)
.
Thus, we can take
{\mathcal{F}}^{(1)}_{m}=\{(2,j):(2,2,j)\in{\mathcal{F}}^{\prime}_{m}\}
and then (
7.4
) and (
7.5
) will be satisfied.

Sub-case 2.2.3.
Assume next that

\displaystyle\sum_{(2,2,j)\in{\mathcal{F}}^{\prime}_{m}}\nu(\widehat{{\mathcal{D}}}^{(m,2)}_{j})<\frac{d_{4}}{8d_{3}}\nu(\widetilde{{\mathcal{C}}}^{\prime}_{m}).

This and (7.11) yield

\sum_{(2,1,j)\in{\mathcal{F}}^{\prime}_{m}}\nu(\widehat{{\mathcal{D}}}^{(m,2)}_{j})+\sum_{(2,2,j)\in{\mathcal{F}}^{\prime}_{m}}\nu(\widehat{{\mathcal{D}}}^{(m,2)}_{j})<\frac{d_{4}}{4d_{3}}\nu(\widetilde{{\mathcal{C}}}^{\prime}_{m})<\frac{d_{4}}{4}\nu(\widetilde{{\mathcal{C}}}^{\prime}_{m}).

Denote by
J^{\prime\prime}_{m}
the set of those
j\in J_{m}
such that both
(2,1,j)\in{\mathcal{F}}^{\prime\prime}_{m}
and
(2,2,j)\in{\mathcal{F}}^{\prime\prime}_{m}
.
Then the above and (
6.35
) with
t=2
imply

\sum_{j\in J^{\prime\prime}_{m}}\nu(\widehat{{\mathcal{D}}}^{(m,2)}_{j})>\frac{3d_{4}}{4}\nu(\widetilde{{\mathcal{C}}}^{\prime}_{m}).

(7.13)

Finally, denote by
J^{\prime\prime\prime}_{m}
the set of those
j\in J_{m}
such that both
(1,1,j)\in{\mathcal{F}}^{\prime\prime}_{m}
and
(1,2,j)\in{\mathcal{F}}^{\prime\prime}_{m}

and both
(2,1,j)\in{\mathcal{F}}^{\prime\prime}_{m}
and
(2,2,j)\in{\mathcal{F}}^{\prime\prime}_{m}
,
i.e.
J^{\prime\prime\prime}_{m}=J^{\prime}_{m}\cap J^{\prime\prime}_{m}
. It now follows from
(7.10), (7.13) and (
6.35
) with
t=2
that

\sum_{j\in J^{\prime\prime\prime}_{m}}\nu(\widehat{{\mathcal{D}}}^{(m,2)}_{j})>\frac{d_{4}}{2}\nu(\widetilde{{\mathcal{C}}}^{\prime}_{m}).

(7.14)

Fix now an arbitrary
j\in J^{\prime\prime\prime}_{m}
.

Then
(1,1,j)\in{\mathcal{F}}^{\prime\prime}_{m}
,
(1,2,j)\in{\mathcal{F}}^{\prime\prime}_{m}
,
(2,1,j)\in{\mathcal{F}}^{\prime\prime}_{m}

and
(2,2,j)\in{\mathcal{F}}^{\prime\prime}_{m}
, so for any
t=1,2
the second alternative in Lemma 7.2(c) holds for
(t,1,j)
and
(t,2,j)
, that is

|h(v^{(m)}_{i,j}(u))|\geq\frac{1}{4}\,H(v^{(m)}_{i,j}(u))>0

(7.15)

for all
u\in\widehat{{\mathcal{D}}}^{(m,t)}_{j}
for both
i=1
and
i=2
.

We will prove the following

Claim:
Either (
7.4
) holds for all
u\in\widehat{{\mathcal{D}}}^{(m,1)}_{j}
or (
7.4
) holds for all
u\in\widehat{{\mathcal{D}}}^{(m,2)}_{j}
.

Proof of Claim.

Let
u,u^{\prime}\in\widehat{{\mathcal{D}}}^{(m,t)}_{j}
for some
t=1,2
.
Using the assumption
(h,H)\in{\mathcal{K}}_{b}
, and in particular property

(T2)
with
p=0
,
v=v^{(m)}_{i,j}(u)
and
v^{\prime}=v^{(m)}_{i,j}(u^{\prime})
, and assuming e.g.

\min\{|h(v^{(m)}_{i,j}(u))|,|h(v^{(m)}_{i,j}(u^{\prime}))|\}=|h(v^{(m)}_{i,j}(u^{\prime}))|,

it follows from (
7.1
), (7.2) and (7.15) that

\displaystyle\frac{|h(v^{(m)}_{i,j}(u))-h(v^{(m)}_{i,j}(u^{\prime}))|}{\min\{|h(v^{(m)}_{i,j}(u))|,|h(v^{(m)}_{i,j}(u^{\prime}))|\}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{E|b|\,\theta^{N}H(v^{(m)}_{i,j}(u^{\prime}))}{|h(v^{(m)}_{i,j}(u^{\prime}))|}\mbox{\rm diam}_{\theta}(\widetilde{{\mathcal{D}}}^{(m,t)}_{j})

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 4E|b|\,\theta^{N}\mbox{\rm diam}_{\theta}(\widetilde{{\mathcal{D}}}^{(m,t)}_{j})<\frac{\epsilon_{3}}{8}.

So, the difference between the arguments of the complex numbers

h(v^{(m)}_{i,j}(u))
and
h(v^{(m)}_{i,j}(u^{\prime}))
(regarded as vectors in
{\mathbb{R}}^{2}
) is
<\frac{\epsilon_{3}}{8}<\frac{\pi}{8}
.
In particular, for any
i=1,2
we can choose a real continuous function

\theta^{(m)}_{i,j}(u)
,
u\in\widehat{{\mathcal{D}}}^{(m,t)}_{j}
, with values in
[0,\epsilon_{3}/8]
and
\lambda^{(m)}_{i,j}\in[0,2\pi)
such that

\displaystyle h(v^{(m)}_{i,j}(u))=e^{{\bf i}(\lambda^{(m)}_{i,j}+\theta^{(m)}_{i,j}(u))}|h(v^{(m)}_{i,j}(u))|\hskip 10.00002pt,\hskip 10.00002ptu\in\widehat{{\mathcal{D}}}^{(m,t)}_{j}.

(7.16)

The above,
yields

|\theta^{(m)}_{i,j}(u)-\theta^{(m)}_{i,j}(u^{\prime})|\leq\frac{\epsilon_{3}}{4}<\frac{\pi}{4}

(7.17)

for all
u,u^{\prime}\in\widehat{{\mathcal{D}}}^{(m,t)}_{j}
.

Choose an arbitrary point
u^{(m)}_{i,j}\in\widehat{{\mathcal{D}}}^{(m,t)}_{j}
, and set

{\tilde{\lambda}^{(m)}}_{i,j}=|b|\psi_{m}(u^{(m)}_{i,j})+2k\pi,

where we choose
k=k(i,m,j)\in{\mathbb{Z}}
so that

|\lambda^{(m)}_{2,j}-\lambda^{(m)}_{1,j}+{\tilde{\lambda}^{(m)}}_{i,j}|\leq\pi.

(7.18)

By (7.16), the difference between the arguments of the complex numbers

e^{{\bf i}\,b\,\tau_{N}(v^{(m)}_{1,j}(u))}h(v^{(m)}_{1,j}(u))
and
e^{{\bf i}\,b\,\tau_{N}(v^{(m)}_{2,j}(u))}h(v^{(m)}_{2,j}(u))

is given by the function

\displaystyle\Omega^{(m)}_{j}(u)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle[b\,\tau_{N}(v^{(m)}_{2,j}(u))+\theta^{(m)}_{2,j}(u)+\lambda^{(m)}_{2,j}]-[b\,\tau_{N}(v^{(m)}_{1,j}(u))+\theta^{(m)}_{1,j}(u)+\lambda^{(m)}_{1,j}]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(\lambda^{(m)}_{2,j}-\lambda^{(m)}_{1,j})-b\psi_{m}(u)+(\theta^{(m)}_{2,j}(u)-\theta^{(m)}_{1,j}(u)).

It follows from Lemma 6.6 (or rather its proof) and (7.2), using (7.3) as well, that for all
u,u^{\prime}\in\widehat{{\mathcal{D}}}^{(m,t)}_{j}
we have

\displaystyle|b|\,|\psi_{m}(u)-\psi_{m}(u^{\prime})|

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle|b|\,\left|[\tau_{N}(v^{(m)}_{1,j}(u))-\tau_{N}(v^{(m)}_{2,j}(u))]-[\tau_{N}(v^{(m)}_{1,j}(u^{\prime}))-\tau_{N}(v^{(m)}_{2,j}(u^{\prime}))]\right|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle|b|\,\frac{2C_{4}}{d_{0}^{2}}\,\theta^{N}\,\mbox{\rm diam}_{\theta}(\widetilde{{\mathcal{D}}}^{(m,t)}_{j})\leq\frac{2C_{4}}{d_{0}^{2}}\,\frac{\epsilon_{3}}{32E}<\frac{\epsilon_{3}}{16}.

The latter, (7.17), (7.18) and the choice of
\epsilon_{3}
in Sect. 6.3 imply that for any
u\in\widehat{{\mathcal{D}}}^{(m,t)}_{j}
we have

\displaystyle|\Omega^{(m)}_{j}(u)|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle|\lambda^{(m)}_{2,j}-\lambda^{(m)}_{1,j}+{\tilde{\lambda}^{(m)}}_{1,j}|+|b|\,|\psi_{m}(u)-\psi_{m}(u^{(m)}_{i,j})|+|\theta^{(m)}_{2,j}(u)-\theta^{(m)}_{1,j}(u)|

(7.19)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\pi+\frac{\epsilon_{3}}{16}+\frac{\epsilon_{3}}{4}<\frac{3\pi}{2}.

We will show now that either for
\widehat{{\mathcal{D}}}^{(m,1)}_{j}
or for
\widehat{{\mathcal{D}}}^{(m,2)}_{j}
we can bound

|\Omega^{(m)}_{j}(u)|
from below by a positive constant.

It follows from the properties of the cylinders
\widehat{{\mathcal{D}}}^{(m,1)}_{j}
and
\widehat{{\mathcal{D}}}^{(m,2)}_{j}
in (
6.42
) that for

u\in\widehat{{\mathcal{D}}}^{(m,1)}_{j}
and
u^{\prime}\in\widehat{{\mathcal{D}}}^{(m,2)}_{j}
we have

|\psi_{m}(u)-\psi_{m}(u^{\prime})|\geq\delta_{1}e^{-s_{m}\hat{\epsilon}_{13}}\,\mbox{\rm diam}(\widetilde{{\mathcal{C}}}_{m}).

This, (
6.8
) and
\hat{\epsilon}_{15}\geq\hat{\epsilon}_{13}
imply

|b||\psi_{m}(u)-\psi_{m}(u^{\prime})|\geq|b|\delta_{1}e^{-s_{m}\hat{\epsilon}_{13}}\,\frac{\epsilon_{2}e^{s_{m}\hat{\epsilon}_{15}}}{|b|}\geq\delta_{1}\,\epsilon_{2}

for all
u\in\widehat{{\mathcal{D}}}^{(m,1)}_{j}
and
u^{\prime}\in\widehat{{\mathcal{D}}}^{(m,2)}_{j}
.
Then for such
u
and
u^{\prime}
we have

\displaystyle|\Omega^{(m)}_{j}(u)-\Omega^{(m)}_{j}(u^{\prime})|

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle|b|\,|\psi_{m}(u)-\psi_{m}(u^{\prime})|-|\theta^{(m)}_{1,j}(u)-\theta^{(m)}_{1,j}(u^{\prime})|-|\theta^{(m)}_{2,j}(u)-\theta^{(m)}_{2,j}(u^{\prime})|

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\delta_{1}\,\epsilon_{2}-\epsilon_{3}>2\epsilon_{3},

by the choice of
\epsilon_{3}
.

Thus,
|\Omega^{(m)}_{j}(u)-\Omega^{(m)}_{j}(u^{\prime})|\geq 2\epsilon_{3}
for all
u\in\widehat{{\mathcal{D}}}^{(m,1)}_{j}
and all
u^{\prime}\in\widehat{{\mathcal{D}}}^{(m,2)}_{j}
. Hence either

|\Omega^{(m)}_{j}(u)|\geq\epsilon_{3}
for all
u\in\widehat{{\mathcal{D}}}^{(m,1)}_{j}
or
|\Omega^{(m)}_{j}(u^{\prime})|\geq\epsilon_{3}
for all
u^{\prime}\in\widehat{{\mathcal{D}}}^{(m,2)}_{j}
. Indeed, if

|\Omega^{(m)}_{j}(u^{\prime})|<\epsilon_{3}
for some
u^{\prime}\in\widehat{{\mathcal{D}}}^{(m,1)}_{j}
, then for every
u\in\widehat{{\mathcal{D}}}^{(m,2)}_{j}
we get

|\Omega^{(m)}_{j}(u)|=|(\Omega^{(m)}_{j}(u)-\Omega^{(m)}_{j}(u^{\prime}))+\Omega^{(m)}_{j}(u^{\prime})|\geq|\Omega^{(m)}_{j}(u)-\Omega^{(m)}_{j}(u^{\prime})|-|\Omega^{(m)}_{j}(u^{\prime})|>\epsilon_{3}.

Similarly, if
|\Omega^{(m)}_{j}(u)|<\epsilon_{3}
for some
u\in\widehat{{\mathcal{D}}}^{(m,1)}_{j}
, then
|\Omega^{(m)}_{j}(u^{\prime})|\geq\epsilon_{3}
for every
u^{\prime}\in\widehat{{\mathcal{D}}}^{(m,2)}_{j}
.

Hence we either have

A:

|\Omega^{(m)}_{j}(u)|\geq\epsilon_{3}
for all
u\in\widehat{{\mathcal{D}}}^{(m,1)}_{j}
,

or

B:

|\Omega^{(m)}_{j}(u)|\geq\epsilon_{3}
for all
u\in\widehat{{\mathcal{D}}}^{(m,2)}_{j}
.

Assume for example that we have
A:

|\Omega^{(m)}_{j}(u)|\geq\epsilon_{3}
for all
u\in\widehat{{\mathcal{D}}}^{(m,1)}_{j}
.
It follows from this and (7.19) that
\epsilon_{3}\leq|\Omega^{(m)}_{j}(u)|<\frac{3\pi}{2}
for all
u\in\widehat{{\mathcal{D}}}^{(m,1)}_{j}
.
Hence, we see that for
u\in\widehat{{\mathcal{D}}}^{(m,1)}_{j}
the difference
\Omega^{(m)}_{j}(u)
between the arguments of the complex numbers

e^{{\bf i}\,b\,\tau_{N}(v^{(m)}_{1,j}(u))}h(v^{(m)}_{1,j}(u))
and
e^{{\bf i}\,b\,\tau_{N}(v^{(m)}_{2,j}(u))}h(v^{(m)}_{2,j}(u))
,
defined as a number in the interval
[0,2\pi)
, satisfies

\epsilon_{3}\leq\Omega^{(m)}_{j}(u)<3\pi/2
for all
u\in\widehat{{\mathcal{D}}}^{(m,1)}_{j}
.

It follows from Lemma 7.2(b) that either
H(v^{(m)}_{1,j}(u))\geq H(v^{(m)}_{2,j}(u))/4
for all

u\in\widehat{{\mathcal{D}}}^{(m,1)}_{j}
or
H(v^{(m)}_{2,j}(u))\geq H(v^{(m)}_{1,j}(u))/4
for all
u\in\widehat{{\mathcal{D}}}^{(m,1)}_{j}
.
Assume e.g. that

H(v^{(m)}_{1,j}(u))/4\leq H(v^{(m)}_{2,j}(u))\hskip 10.00002pt,\hskip 10.00002ptu\in\widehat{{\mathcal{D}}}^{(m,1)}_{j}.

(7.20)

As in
[
D1
]
(see also
[
St5
]
) we will show that

|\tilde{\psi}_{m}(u)|\leq\gamma^{(1)}_{m}(u)
for all
u\in\widehat{{\mathcal{D}}}^{(m,1)}_{j}
.
Given such
u
, consider the points

z_{1}=e^{(f^{(a)}_{N}+{\bf i}b\tau_{N})(v^{(m)}_{1,j}(u))}h(v^{(m)}_{1,j}(u))\>\>\>,\>\>\>z_{2}=e^{(f^{(a)}_{N}+{\bf i}b\tau_{N})(v^{(m)}_{2,j}(u))}h(v^{(m)}_{2,j}(u))

in the complex plane
{\mathbb{C}}
, and let
\varphi
be the smaller angle between the arguments of
z_{1}
and
z_{2}
.
It then follows from the above estimate for
\Omega^{(m)}_{j}(u)
that
\epsilon_{3}\leq\varphi\leq 3\pi/2
.
Moreover, (7.3),
|h|\leq H
, (7.15) and our assumption (7.20) imply

\displaystyle\frac{|z_{1}|}{|z_{2}|}=e^{f^{(a)}_{N}(v^{(m)}_{1,j}(u))-f^{(a)}_{N}(v^{(m)}_{2,j}(u))}\frac{|h(v^{(m)}_{1,j}(u))|}{|h(v^{(m)}_{2,j}(u))|}\leq e^{\epsilon_{3}}\,\frac{H(v^{(m)}_{1,j}(u))}{H(v^{(m)}_{2,j}(u))/4}\leq 16e^{\epsilon_{3}}<19,

by the choice of
\epsilon_{3}
. This yields

|z_{1}+z_{2}|\leq(1-t)|z_{1}|+|z_{2}|,

(7.21)

where we can take e.g.

\displaystyle t=\frac{1-\cos(\epsilon_{3})}{20}.

Indeed, we have

|z_{1}+z_{2}|^{2}=|z_{1}|^{2}+|z_{2}|^{2}+2\langle z_{1},z_{2}\rangle\leq|z_{1}|^{2}+|z_{2}|^{2}+2|z_{1}|\,|z_{2}|(1-s),

where
s=1-\cos\epsilon_{3}
. Thus, (7.21) will hold if

|z_{1}|^{2}+|z_{2}|^{2}+2|z_{1}|\,|z_{2}|(1-s)\leq(1-t)^{2}|z_{1}|^{2}+|z_{2}|^{2}+2(1-t)|z_{1}|\,|z_{2}|,

that is if

(1-(1-t)^{2})|z_{1}|+2|z_{2}|(1-s)\leq 2(1-t)|z_{2}|,

which equivalent to

\displaystyle|z_{1}|\leq 2\frac{s-t}{t(2-t)}\,|z_{2}|.

Since
t=s/20
, it follows that

\frac{|z_{1}|}{|z_{2}|}<19<2\frac{s-t}{t(2-t)}=\frac{38}{2-s/20},

so the above inequality holds.
This proves (7.21) with the given choice of
t
.

Since
\mu_{0}\leq t
by (
6.31
), it now follows from (7.21) that
|z_{1}+z_{2}|\leq(1-\mu_{0})|z_{1}|+|z_{2}|
.
Therefore
|\tilde{\psi}_{m}(u)|\leq\gamma^{(1)}_{m}(u)
for all
u\in\widehat{{\mathcal{D}}}^{(m,1)}_{j}
.
Now the argument from Case 1 proves that (
7.4
) holds for all
u\in\widehat{{\mathcal{D}}}^{(m,1)}_{j}
.

Thus, in the case
A
, (
7.4
) holds for all
u\in\widehat{{\mathcal{D}}}^{(m,1)}_{j}
.
In a similar way we prove that in the case
B
, (
7.4
) holds for all
u\in\widehat{{\mathcal{D}}}^{(m,2)}_{j}
.
This proves the Claim.

We will now define the set
{\mathcal{F}}_{m}
in the Sub-case 2.2.3. Consider the set
\widetilde{J}_{m}
of all
j\in J^{\prime\prime\prime\prime}_{m}
so that
(
7.4
) holds for all
u\in\widehat{{\mathcal{D}}}^{(m,2)}_{j}
(i.e. case
B
). If

\sum_{j\in\widetilde{J}_{m}}\nu(\widehat{{\mathcal{D}}}^{(m,2)}_{j})\geq\frac{d_{4}}{4}\nu(\widetilde{{\mathcal{C}}}^{\prime}_{m}),

then we just set
{\mathcal{F}}^{(2)}_{m}=\{(2,m,j):j\in\widetilde{J}_{m}\}
, and then (7.6) will be satisfied. We then set
J={\mathcal{F}}^{(2)}_{m}
.

Assume that

\sum_{j\in\widetilde{J}_{m}}\nu(\widehat{{\mathcal{D}}}^{(m,2)}_{j})<\frac{d_{4}}{4}\nu(\widetilde{{\mathcal{C}}}^{\prime}_{m}).

This and (7.14) imply

\sum_{j\in J^{\prime\prime\prime}_{m}\setminus\widetilde{J}_{m}}\nu(\widehat{{\mathcal{D}}}^{(m,2)}_{j})\geq\frac{d_{4}}{4}\nu(\widetilde{{\mathcal{C}}}^{\prime}_{m}).

For
j\in J^{\prime\prime\prime}_{m}\setminus\widetilde{J}_{m}
we have the case
A
, that is (
7.4
) holds for all
u\in\widehat{{\mathcal{D}}}^{(m,1)}_{j}
.
The above and (
6.37
) yield

\sum_{j\in J^{\prime\prime\prime}_{m}\setminus\widetilde{J}_{m}}\nu(\widehat{{\mathcal{D}}}^{(m,1)}_{j})\geq\frac{d_{4}}{4d_{3}}\nu(\widetilde{{\mathcal{C}}}^{\prime}_{m}).

Now we set
{\mathcal{F}}^{(1)}_{m}=\{(1,m,j):j\in J^{\prime\prime\prime}_{m}\setminus\widetilde{J}_{m}\}
and then (7.5) will be satisfied.
So, we just define
J={\mathcal{F}}^{(1)}_{m}
in this case.

This completes the construction of the set
J
in all possible cases.
Clearly
J\in{\mathcal{J}}(b)
and
(
7.4
) holds for all
u\in V_{b}
. As we mentioned in the beginning of the proof, (
7.4
)
always holds for
u\in\widehat{U}\setminus V_{b}
.

8
L^{1}
contraction estimates

Here we obtain
L^{1}
-contraction estimates for large powers of the contraction operators
{\mathcal{N}}_{J}
.
We continue to use the notation from Sections 5 and 6. Here

we assume that
f\in{\mathcal{F}}_{\theta_{1}}(\widehat{U})
and
N\geq N_{0}
is a fixed integer
as in (
6.33
).
Recall also the constants
\tilde{b}_{0}\geq b_{0}
,
D_{3}>1
from (
6.27
), the constant
c=|q_{0}|\epsilon_{0}

that appears in (
6.29
) in Sect. 6.2 and
C_{10}>0
,
a_{0}=a_{0}(N)>0
,
\rho_{3}=\rho_{3}(N)\in(0,1)
and

\rho_{4}=\rho_{4}(N)\in(0,1)
from Sect. 6.3.

Given
b
with
|b|\geq\tilde{b}_{0}
and a representative set
J\in{\mathcal{J}}(b)
set

W_{J}=\cup_{(t,m,i,j)\in J}\widehat{{\mathcal{D}}}^{(m,t)}_{j}\subset V_{b}.

Recall from (
6.6
) that
V_{b}
is a union of cylinders
{\mathcal{C}}^{\prime}_{m}={\mathcal{C}}^{\prime}_{m}(b)
and
for each
m=1,\ldots,m_{0}=m_{0}(b)
,
\widehat{{\mathcal{D}}}^{(m,t)}_{j}=\widehat{{\mathcal{D}}}^{(m,t)}_{j}(b)
are sub-cylinders of

{\mathcal{C}}^{\prime}_{m}
for
t=1,2
,
j=1,\ldots,j_{m}
for some
j_{m}=j_{m}(b)
.

The following lemma is the analogue of Lemma 12 in
[
D1
]
. It is similar to Lemma 7.1 in
[
St5
]
.

Lemma 8.1.

(a)
For any
H\in{\mathcal{K}}_{E|b|}
, any
J\in{\mathcal{J}}(b)
and any integer
r
with
0\leq r\leq N-1
we have

\int_{\cup_{r=0}^{N-1}\sigma^{-r}(V_{b})}H^{2}\,d\nu\leq C_{10}\,\int_{\cup_{r=0}^{N-1}\sigma^{-r}(W_{J})}H^{2}\,d\nu.

(8.1)

(b)
For any
H\in{\mathcal{K}}_{E|b|}
, any
a\in{\mathbb{R}}
with
|a|\leq a_{0}
and any
J\in{\mathcal{J}}(b)
we have

\int_{\cup_{r=0}^{N-1}\sigma^{-r}(V_{b})}({\mathcal{N}}_{J}H)^{2}\,d\nu\leq\rho_{3}\,\int_{\cup_{r=0}^{N-1}\sigma^{-r}(V_{b})}L^{N}_{f^{(0)}}(H^{2})\,d\nu.

(8.2)

Proofs.

(a)
Let
H\in{\mathcal{K}}_{E|b|}
. Then
0<H\leq 1
. Consider an arbitrary
J\in\Pi(b)
.
Then either all elements of
J
have the form
(1,m,i,j)
or all of them
have the form
(2,m,i,j)
. Assume e.g. that we have the first case, and that (
6.43
) holds.

Notice that the set
V=\cup_{r=0}^{N-1}\sigma^{-r}(V_{b})
is a finite union of cylinders in
\widehat{U}
.
Any two cylinders in the latter are either disjoint or one of them contains the other.
Since
V_{b}
is a disjoint union of cylinders
{\mathcal{C}}^{\prime}_{m}
(
1\leq m\leq m_{0}
),
V
is a union of cylinders in

\sigma^{-r}({\mathcal{C}}^{\prime}_{m})
,
0\leq r\leq N-1
,
m=1,\ldots,m_{0}
. Taking maximal cylinders of this kind, we get a
disjoint
family

\{Y_{p}\,:\,1\leq p\leq k\}

of cylinders in
V_{b}
so that for every
p=1,\ldots,k
we have
\sigma^{r_{p}}(Y_{p})={\mathcal{C}}^{\prime}_{m_{p}}

for some
r_{p}=0,1,\ldots,N-1
and some
m_{p}=1,\ldots,m_{0}
. Naturally,
r_{p}
may take the same
value
r
for many different
p
, and similarly
m_{p}
might take the same value
m
for various different
p
.
Then we have

V=\cup_{p=1}^{k}Y_{p}\hskip 10.00002pt,\hskip 10.00002ptV_{b}=\cup_{p=1}^{k}\sigma^{r_{p}}(Y_{p}).

For every
p=1,\ldots,k
and every
(1,m_{p},i,j)\in J

let
Z_{(1,m_{p},j)}
be the cylinder in
Y_{p}
such that
\sigma^{r_{p}}(Z_{(1,m_{p},j)})=\widehat{{\mathcal{D}}}^{(m_{p},1)}_{j}
.
Then

W_{J}=\cup_{p=1}^{k}\cup_{(1,m_{p},i,j)\in J}\sigma^{r_{p}}(Z_{(1,m_{p},j)}).

Consider now a fixed
p
and corresponding sets
Y_{p}
and
Z_{(1,m_{p},j)}\subset Y_{p}
.
By (
6.43
),

\displaystyle\sum_{(1,m_{p},i,j)\in J}\nu(\widehat{{\mathcal{D}}}^{(m,1)}_{j})\geq\frac{d_{4}}{8d_{3}}\nu({\mathcal{C}}^{\prime}_{m_{p}}).

(8.3)

Given
u,u^{\prime}\in{\mathcal{C}}^{\prime}_{m_{p}}
, it follows from
H\in{\mathcal{K}}_{E|b|}
and (
6.7
) that

\displaystyle\frac{|H(u)-H(u^{\prime})|}{H(u^{\prime})}\leq E\,|b|\,D_{\theta_{1}}(u,u^{\prime})\leq EC_{7},

therefore

H(u)/H(u^{\prime})\leq 1+EC_{7}\leq 2EC_{7}.

Thus, for

L_{1}=\max_{{\mathcal{C}}^{\prime}_{m_{p}}}H
and
L_{2}=\min_{{\mathcal{C}}^{\prime}_{m_{p}}}H
, we have
1\leq L_{1}/L_{2}\leq 2EC_{7}
.
Using (8.3) we derive

\int_{{\mathcal{C}}^{\prime}_{m_{p}}}H^{2}\,d\nu\leq L_{1}^{2}\nu({\mathcal{C}}^{\prime}_{m_{p}})\leq\frac{8d_{3}L_{1}^{2}}{d_{4}}\,\sum_{(1,m_{p},i,j)\in J}\nu(\widehat{{\mathcal{D}}}^{(m_{p},1)}_{j})\leq\frac{8d_{3}L_{1}^{2}}{d_{4}L_{2}^{2}}\,\sum_{(1,m_{p},i,j)\in J}\int_{\widehat{{\mathcal{D}}}^{(m_{p},1)}_{j}}H^{2}\,d\nu.

On the other hand, using
\sigma^{r_{p}}(Y_{p})={\mathcal{C}}^{\prime}_{m_{p}}
and the
\sigma
-invariance of
\nu
, we get

\displaystyle\int_{Y_{p}}H^{2}\,d\nu

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{U}H^{2}(y)\,\chi_{Y_{p}}(y)\,d\nu(y)=\int_{U}H^{2}(\sigma^{r_{p}}y)\,\chi_{Y_{p}}(\sigma^{r_{p}}y)\,d\nu(y)

(8.4)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{U}H^{2}(x)\,\chi_{{\mathcal{C}}^{\prime}_{m_{p}}}(x)\,d\nu(x)=\int_{{\mathcal{C}}^{\prime}_{m_{p}}}H^{2}\,d\nu.

Similarly,

\int_{Z_{(1,m_{p},j)}}H^{2}\,d\nu=\int_{\widehat{{\mathcal{D}}}^{(m_{p},1)}_{j}}H^{2}\,d\nu.

It now follows from (8.4) and
C_{10}\geq\frac{32d_{3}E^{2}C_{7}^{2}}{d_{4}}\geq\frac{8d_{3}L_{1}^{2}}{d_{4}L_{2}^{2}}
(see Sect. 6.3) that

\int_{Y_{p}}H^{2}\,d\nu\leq\frac{8d_{3}L_{1}^{2}}{d_{4}L_{2}^{2}}\,\sum_{(1,m_{p},i,j)\in J}\int_{Z_{(1,m_{p},j)}}H^{2}\,d\nu\leq C_{10}\,\sum_{(1,m_{p},i,j)\in J}\int_{Z_{(1,m_{p},j)}}H^{2}\,d\nu.

(8.5)

This,
\cup_{p=1}^{k}Y_{p}=V=\cup_{r=0}^{N-1}\sigma^{-r}(V_{b})
and

\cup_{p=1}^{k}\cup_{(1,m_{p},i,j)\in J}Z_{(1,m_{p},j)}=\cup_{r=0}^{N-1}\sigma^{-r}(W_{J})
imply

\int_{\cup_{r=0}^{N-1}\sigma^{-r}(V_{b})}H^{2}\,d\nu=\sum_{p=1}^{k}\int_{Y_{p}}H^{2}\,d\nu\leq C_{10}\,\sum_{p=1}^{k}\sum_{(1,m_{p},i,j)\in J}\int_{Z_{(1,m_{p},j)}}H^{2}\,d\nu=C_{10}\,\int_{\cup_{r=0}^{N-1}\sigma^{-r}(W_{J})}H^{2}\,d\nu,

which proves (8.1).

(b) The proof of this part is very similar to the proof of Lemma 7.1(b) in
[
St5
]
.
We provide some details since they will be used later.

Let again
H\in{\mathcal{K}}_{E|b|}
and
J\in{\mathcal{J}}(b)
, and let
0\leq r\leq N-1
be an integer. We continues to use the notation from
the proof of part (a) and the assumption about
J
.

We will use the representations

\cup_{p=1}^{k}Y_{p}=V=\cup_{r=0}^{N-1}\sigma^{-r}(V_{b})\hskip 10.00002pt,\hskip 10.00002ptW_{J}=\cup_{p=1}^{k}\cup_{(1,m_{p},i,j)\in J}\sigma^{r_{p}}(Z_{(1,m_{p},j)})

from the proof of part (a). Recall that
\sigma^{r_{p}}(Y_{p})={\mathcal{C}}^{\prime}_{m_{p}}
and

Z_{(1,m_{p},j)}
is the cylinder in
Y_{p}
with
\sigma^{r_{p}}(Z_{(1,m_{p},j)})=\widehat{{\mathcal{D}}}^{(m_{p},1)}_{j}

for every
(1,m_{p},i,j)\in J
.

By Lemma 6.11,
{\mathcal{N}}_{J}H\in{\mathcal{K}}_{E|b|}
, while
the Cauchy-Schwartz inequality implies

({\mathcal{N}}_{J}H)^{2}=({\mathcal{M}}_{a}^{N}\omega_{J}H)^{2}\leq({\mathcal{M}}_{a}^{N}\omega_{J}^{2})\,({\mathcal{M}}_{a}^{N}H^{2})\leq({\mathcal{M}}_{a}^{N}\omega_{J})\,({\mathcal{M}}_{a}^{N}H^{2})\leq{\mathcal{M}}_{a}^{N}H^{2}.

(8.6)

If
u\notin W_{J}
, then
\omega_{J}(u)=1
. Let
u\in W_{J}
; then
u\in\widehat{{\mathcal{D}}}^{(m_{p},1)}_{j}

for some (unique)
(1,m,i,j)\in J
.
Assuming e.g.
i=1
so that
\sigma^{N}(v)=u
for some
v=v^{(m)}_{1,j}(u)
with
\omega_{J}(v)=1-\mu_{0}
, we have

\displaystyle({\mathcal{M}}_{a}^{N}\omega_{J})(u)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{\sigma^{N}v=u,\;v\neq v^{(m)}_{1,j}(u)}e^{f^{(a)}_{N}(v)}+e^{f^{(a)}_{N}(v^{(m)}_{1,j}(u))}\omega_{J}(v^{(m)}_{1,j}(u))

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{\sigma^{N}v=u,\;v\neq v^{(m)}_{1,j}(u)}e^{f^{(a)}_{N}(v)}+(1-\mu_{0})e^{f^{(a)}_{N}(v^{(m)}_{1,j}(u))}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{\sigma^{N}v=u}e^{f^{(a)}_{N}(v)}-\mu_{0}\,e^{f^{(a)}_{N}(v^{(m)}_{1,j}(u))}\leq({\mathcal{M}}_{a}^{N}\;1)(u)-\mu_{0}\,e^{-NT_{0}}=1-\mu_{0}\,e^{-NT_{0}}.

This holds for all
u\in W_{J}
, so by (8.6),

\displaystyle({\mathcal{N}}_{J}H)^{2}\circ\sigma^{r_{p}}\leq(1-\mu_{0}e^{-NT_{0}})\,({\mathcal{M}}_{a}^{N}H^{2})\circ\sigma^{r_{p}}

on
Z_{(1,m_{p},j)}
. Therefore

\displaystyle\int_{Z(1,m_{p},j)}({\mathcal{N}}_{J}H)^{2}\,d\nu

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{U}({\mathcal{N}}_{J}H)^{2}(y)\chi_{Z(1,m_{p},j)}(y)\,d\nu=\int_{U}({\mathcal{N}}_{J}H)^{2}(\sigma^{r_{p}}y)\chi_{Z(1,m_{p},j)}(\sigma^{r_{p}}y)\,d\nu

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle(1-\mu_{0}e^{-NT_{0}})\int_{U}({\mathcal{M}}_{a}^{N}H)^{2}(\sigma^{r_{p}}y)\chi_{Z(1,m_{p},j)}(\sigma^{r_{p}}y)\,d\nu(y)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(1-\mu_{0}e^{-NT_{0}})\int_{U}({\mathcal{M}}_{a}^{N}H)^{2}(x)\chi_{Z(1,m_{p},j)}(x)\,d\nu(x)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(1-\mu_{0}e^{-NT_{0}})\int_{Z(1,m_{p},j)}({\mathcal{M}}_{a}^{N}H)^{2}\,d\nu.

Setting

Z_{p}=\cup_{(1,m_{p},j)\in J}Z_{(1,m_{p},j)}\subset Y_{p}

for every
p=1,\ldots,k
, the above implies

\int_{Z_{p}}({\mathcal{N}}_{J}H)^{2}\,d\nu\leq(1-\mu_{0}e^{-NT_{0}})\,\int_{Z_{p}}({\mathcal{M}}_{a}^{N}H)^{2}\,d\nu,

while (8.5) with
H
replaced by
{\mathcal{M}}_{a}^{N}H
yields

\int_{Y_{p}}({\mathcal{M}}_{a}^{N}H)^{2}\,d\nu\leq C_{10}\,\int_{Z_{p}}({\mathcal{M}}_{a}^{N}H)^{2}\,d\nu.

As in (8.4) we have
\displaystyle\int_{Y_{p}}({\mathcal{N}}_{J}H)^{2}\,d\nu=\int_{{\mathcal{C}}^{\prime}_{m_{p}}}({\mathcal{N}}_{J}H)^{2}\,d\nu
.
This, (8.6) and the above yield

\displaystyle\int_{Y_{p}}({\mathcal{N}}_{J}H)^{2}\,d\nu

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{Y_{p}\setminus Z_{p}}({\mathcal{N}}_{J}H)^{2}\,d\nu+\int_{Z_{p}}({\mathcal{N}}_{J}H)^{2}\,d\nu

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\int_{Y_{p}\setminus Z_{p}}({\mathcal{M}}_{a}^{N}H^{2})\,d\nu+(1-\mu_{0}e^{-NT_{0}})\,\int_{Z_{p}}({\mathcal{M}}_{a}^{N}H)^{2}\,d\nu

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{Y_{p}}({\mathcal{M}}_{a}^{N}H)^{2}\,d\nu-\mu_{0}e^{-NT_{0}}\,\int_{Z_{p}}({\mathcal{M}}_{a}^{N}H)^{2}\,d\nu

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\int_{Y_{p}}({\mathcal{M}}_{a}^{N}H)^{2}\,d\nu-\frac{\mu_{0}e^{-NT_{0}}}{C_{10}}\,\int_{Y_{p}}({\mathcal{N}}_{J}H)^{2}\,d\nu.

From the latter and

({\mathcal{M}}_{a}^{N}H)^{2}\leq({\mathcal{M}}_{a}^{N}1)^{2}({\mathcal{M}}_{a}^{N}H^{2})\leq{\mathcal{M}}_{a}^{N}H^{2}=L^{N}_{f^{(0)}}(e^{f^{(a)}_{N}-f^{(0)}_{N}}H^{2})\leq e^{a_{0}NT_{0}}(L^{N}_{f^{(0)}}H^{2}),

(8.7)

we get

\displaystyle(1+\mu_{0}\,e^{-NT_{0}}/C_{10})\,\int_{Y_{p}}({\mathcal{N}}_{J}H)^{2}\,d\nu\leq\int_{Y_{p}}({\mathcal{M}}_{a}^{N}H)^{2}\,d\nu\leq e^{a_{0}NT_{0}}\,\int_{Y_{p}}L^{N}_{f^{(0)}}H^{2}\,d\nu.

Thus,

\displaystyle\int_{Y_{p}}({\mathcal{N}}_{J}H)^{2}\,d\nu\leq\rho_{3}\,\int_{Y_{p}}L^{N}_{f^{(0)}}H^{2}\,d\nu

for every
p=1,\ldots,k
. This yields

\displaystyle\int_{\cup_{r=0}^{N-1}\sigma^{-r}(V_{b})}({\mathcal{N}}_{J}H)^{2}\,d\nu=\sum_{p=1}^{k}\int_{Y_{p}}({\mathcal{N}}_{J}H)^{2}\,d\nu\leq\rho_{3}\,\sum_{p=1}^{k}\int_{Y_{p}}L^{N}_{f^{(0)}}H^{2}\,d\nu=\rho_{3}\,\int_{\cup_{r=0}^{N-1}\sigma^{-r}(V_{b})}L^{N}_{f^{(0)}}(H^{2})\,d\nu,

which proves (8.2).

We can now prove that iterating sufficiently many contraction operators provides an
L^{1}
-contraction on
U
.

Let again
|b|\geq\tilde{b}_{0}
and let
n_{0}(b)\geq 1
be as in (
6.27
).
Define the function
\hat{h}:\widehat{U}\longrightarrow[0,\infty)
by

\hat{h}=\rho_{3}\,\chi_{\cup_{i=0}^{N-1}\sigma^{-i}(V_{b})}+S_{0}\,\chi_{\widehat{U}\setminus\cup_{i=0}^{N-1}\sigma^{-i}(V_{b})}.

Then
\hat{h}(x)=\rho_{3}
whenever
\sigma^{i}(x)\in V_{b}
for some
i=0,1,\ldots,N-1
and
\hat{h}(x)=S_{0}
otherwise.
Since
V_{b}
and
\widehat{U}\setminus V_{b}
are unions of finitely many cylinders, we have
\hat{h}\in{\mathcal{F}}_{\theta}(\widehat{U})
.

At some stage later on we will need the
Perron-Ruelle-Frobenius Theorem
(see e.g.
[
PP
]
):
there exist global constants
C_{11}\geq 1
and
\rho_{0}\in(0,1)
such that

\|L_{f^{(0)}}^{n}h-h_{0}\,\int_{U}h\,d\nu\|\leq C_{11}\,\rho_{0}^{n}\,\|h\|_{\theta}

(8.8)

for all
h\in{\mathcal{F}}_{\theta}(\widehat{U})
and all integers
n\geq 0
, where
h_{0}>0
is the normalised eigenfunction of

L_{f-P_{f}\tau}
in
{\mathcal{F}}_{\theta}(\widehat{U})
(see Sect. 4.2).
Fix such constants
C_{11}>0
and
\rho_{0}\in(0,1)
.

Here
\|h\|_{\theta}=\|h\|_{0}+|h|_{\theta}
as defined in Sect. 2.

The following is the main result in this section. It is similar to Lemma 7.3 in
[
St5
]
and its proof has
some similarity with that in
[
St5
]
.

Theorem 8.2.

Let
|b|\geq\tilde{b}_{0}
.

(a)
For any sequence
J_{1},J_{2},\ldots,J_{r}\ldots
of elements of
{\mathcal{J}}(b)
, setting

H^{(0)}=1
and

H^{(r+1)}={\mathcal{N}}_{J_{r}}(H^{(r)})
(
r\geq 0
) we have

\displaystyle\int_{U}(H^{(M)})^{2}\,d\nu\leq 2\,\rho_{4}^{M}

(8.9)

for all
M\geq n_{0}(b)
.

(b)
Let
k\geq D_{3}
be a constant.
For all
|a|\leq a_{0}
and
|b|\geq\tilde{b}_{0}
, all
h\in{\mathcal{F}}_{\theta}(U)
and all

m\geq(k/\beta_{4})\,\log|b|
we have

\int_{\widehat{U}}|L_{ab}^{mN}h|\,d\nu\leq\frac{2}{|b|^{2k}}\,\|h\|_{\theta,b}.

(8.10)

(c)
Let
s\geq D_{3}
be a constant. There exists a global constant
C_{12}>0
such that for any integer
k\geq 2s

and all
|a|\leq a_{0}
,
|b|\geq\tilde{b}_{0}
,
h\in{\mathcal{F}}_{\theta}(U)
and all integers
m\geq(k/\beta_{4})\,\log|b|
we have

\|L_{ab}^{2mN}h\|_{0}\leq\frac{C_{12}}{|b|^{k/2}}\,\|h\|_{\theta,b}\leq\frac{C_{12}}{|b|^{s}}\,\|h\|_{\theta,b}.

(8.11)

Proof of Theorem
8.2. (a) Set
\omega_{r}=\omega_{J_{r}}
and
{\mathcal{N}}_{r}={\mathcal{N}}_{J_{r}}
.
Since
H^{(0)}=1\in{\mathcal{K}}_{E|b|}
, it follows from Lemma 6.11 that
H^{(r)}\in{\mathcal{K}}_{E|b|}
for all
r\geq 1
.

Let
M\geq 1
be an arbitrary integer.
Using

L^{N}_{f^{(0)}}((\hat{h}\circ\sigma^{N})\,H)=\hat{h}\,(L_{f^{(0)}}^{N}H)
,
Lemma 8.1(b), (8.2) and (8.7), we get

\displaystyle\int_{U}(H^{(M)})^{2}\,d\nu

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{\cup_{r=0}^{N-1}\sigma^{-r}(V_{b})}(H^{(M)})^{2}\,d\nu+\int_{U\setminus\cup_{r=0}^{N-1}\sigma^{-r}(V_{b})}(H^{(M)})^{2}\,d\nu

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\rho_{3}\,\int_{\cup_{r=0}^{N-1}\sigma^{-r}(V_{b})}L^{N}_{f^{(0)}}(H^{(M-1)})^{2}\,d\nu+e^{a_{0}NT}\,\int_{U\setminus\cup_{r=0}^{N-1}\sigma^{-r}(V_{b})}L^{N}_{f^{(0)}}(H^{(M-1)})^{2}\,d\nu

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{U}\hat{h}\,(L_{f^{(0)}}^{N}(H^{(M-1)})^{2})\,d\nu=\int_{U}L_{f^{(0)}}^{N}((\hat{h}\circ\sigma^{N})\,(H^{(M-1)})^{2})\,d\nu

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{U}(\hat{h}\circ\sigma^{N})\,(H^{(M-1)})^{2}\,d\nu,

using here also one of the main properties of
L_{f^{(0)}}
, namely

\displaystyle\int_{U}L_{f^{(0)}}F\,d\nu=\int_{U}F\,d\nu
for all
F\in C(U)
.
Continuing by induction and using
H^{(0)}=1
, we get

\int_{U}(H^{(M)})^{2}\,d\nu\leq\int_{U}(\hat{h}\circ\sigma^{MN})\,(\hat{h}\circ\sigma^{(M-1)N})\ldots(\hat{h}\circ\sigma^{2N})\,(\hat{h}\circ\sigma^{N})\,d\nu.

(8.12)

By Corollary 6.6, the set
\widetilde{U}_{b}=\widetilde{U}_{b}(M,N)
defined in Sect. 6.2 satisfies (
6.29
) for any integers
M\geq n_{0}(b)
and
N\geq 1
.
Using the rough estimate
\hat{h}\leq S_{0}
on
\widetilde{U}_{b}
, it follows from (
8.12
), (
6.29
)
and (
6.40
) that for
M\geq n_{0}(b)
we have

\int_{\widetilde{U}_{b}}(H^{(M)})^{2}\,d\nu\leq S_{0}^{M}\,\nu(\widetilde{U}_{b})<S_{0}^{M}e^{-cMN}\leq\rho_{4}^{M}.

(8.13)

On the other hand when
x\in U\setminus\widetilde{U}_{b}
, the definition of
\widetilde{U}_{b}
implies

\sigma^{jN}(x)\in\cup_{r=0}^{N-1}\sigma^{-r}(V_{b})
for at least
M\epsilon_{0}
values of
j=0,1,2,\ldots,M-1
,
i.e.
\widetilde{S}^{(b)}_{M,N}(x)\geq M\epsilon_{0}
.
For such
j
the definition of
\hat{h}
gives
\hat{h}(\sigma^{jN}(x))=\rho_{3}
. For all other
j
we can
still use
\hat{h}(\sigma^{jN}(x))\leq S_{0}
. Thus, from (
8.12
) (or rather the analogous estimate we get for the integral over

U\setminus\widetilde{U}_{b}
), using (
6.40
), we derive

\displaystyle\int_{U\setminus\widetilde{U}_{b}}(H^{(M)})^{2}\,d\nu

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\int_{U\setminus\widetilde{U}_{b}}\rho_{3}^{\widetilde{S}^{(b)}_{M,N}(x)}\,S_{0}^{M-\widetilde{S}^{(b)}_{M,N}(x)}\,d\nu(x)\leq\rho_{3}^{M\epsilon_{0}}\,S_{0}^{M-M\epsilon_{0}}\nu(U\setminus\widetilde{U}_{b})

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle(\rho_{3}^{\epsilon_{0}}S_{0}^{1-\epsilon_{0}})^{M}\leq\rho_{4}^{M}.

This, (
8.12
) and (8.13) yield

\int_{U}(H^{(M)})^{2}\,d\nu\leq\rho_{4}^{M}+\rho_{4}^{M}=2\,\rho_{4}^{M}.

(8.14)

The latter holds for all integers
M\geq n_{0}(b)
.
This proves part (a).

(b) Let
h\in{\mathcal{F}}_{\theta}(\widehat{U})
be such that

\|h\|_{\theta,b}\leq 1
. Then
|h(u)|\leq 1
for all
u\in\widehat{U}
and
|h|_{\theta}\leq|b|
.

Assume that the points
u,u^{\prime}
, the cylinder
\widehat{{\mathcal{D}}}^{(m,t)}_{j}
in
U
, the integer
p\geq 0
and the points
v,v^{\prime}\in U

satisfy (
6.45
) for some
t=1,2
,
j=1,\ldots,j_{m}
. This implies

\displaystyle|h(v)-h(v^{\prime})|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle|h|_{\theta}\,D_{\theta}(v,v^{\prime})\leq|b|\,D_{\theta}(v,v^{\prime})=\theta^{p+N}\,|b|\,D_{\theta}(u,u^{\prime})\leq\theta^{p+N}\,|b|\,\mbox{\rm diam}_{\theta}(\widetilde{{\mathcal{D}}}^{(m,t)}_{j})

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle E\,|b|\,\theta^{p+N}\,\mbox{\rm diam}_{\theta}(\widetilde{{\mathcal{D}}}^{(m,t)}_{j}).

Thus,
(h,1)\in{\mathcal{K}}_{b}
(see Sect. 7).

Set
(h^{(0)},H^{(0)})=(h,1)\in{\mathcal{K}}_{b}
and
h^{(m)}=L_{ab}^{mN}{h}
for
m\geq 0
.
Define the sequence of functions
\{H^{(m)}\}
recursively by

H^{(0)}=1
and
H^{(m+1)}={\mathcal{N}}_{J_{m}}H^{(m)}
, where
J_{m}\in{\mathcal{J}}(b)
is chosen by induction as follows.
Since
(h^{(0)},H^{(0)})\in{\mathcal{K}}_{b}
, using Lemma 7.1 we find
J_{0}\in{\mathcal{J}}(b)
such that
for
h^{(1)}=L_{ab}^{N}h^{(0)}
and
H^{(1)}={\mathcal{N}}_{J_{0}}H^{(0)}
we have

(h^{(1)},H^{(1)})\in{\mathcal{K}}_{b}
. Continuing in this way we construct by induction
an infinite sequence of functions
\{H^{(m)}\}
with

H^{(0)}=1
,
H^{(m+1)}={\mathcal{N}}_{J_{m}}H^{(m)}
for all
m\geq 0
, such that
(h^{(m)},H^{(m)})\in{\mathcal{K}}_{b}
.
The latter implies
|h^{(m)}|\leq H^{(m)}
for all
m\geq 0
.

It follows from part (a) that

\displaystyle\int_{U}(H^{(m)})^{2}d\nu\leq 2\rho_{4}^{m}
for all
m\geq n_{0}(b)=D_{3}\,\log|b|
.
Hence

\int_{U}|L_{ab}^{mN}h|^{2}\;d\nu=\int_{U}|h^{(m)}|^{2}\;d\nu\leq\int_{U}(H^{(m)})^{2}\;d\nu\leq 2\,\rho_{4}^{m}

for all
m\geq n_{0}(b)
.
The latter applies to any
h\in{\mathcal{F}}_{\theta}(\widehat{U})
such that
\|h\|_{\theta,b}\leq 1
.
From this it follows that for any
h\in{\mathcal{F}}_{\theta}(\widehat{U})
and any
m\geq n_{0}(b)
we have

\displaystyle\int_{U}|L_{ab}^{mN}h|^{2}\;d\nu\leq 2\,\|h\|^{2}_{\theta,b}\,\rho_{4}^{m}
. Now Hölder’s inequality yields

\int_{U}|L_{ab}^{mN}h|\;d\nu\leq 2\,\rho_{4}^{m/2}\,\|h\|_{\theta,b}

(8.15)

for any
h\in{\mathcal{F}}_{\theta}(\widehat{U})
and any
m\geq n_{0}(b)
.

Let
k\geq D_{3}
be a fixed integer. Consider now integers
m
such that

m\geq\frac{k}{\beta_{4}}\,\log|b|.

(8.16)

Recall from (
6.41
) that
\rho_{4}\leq e^{-4\beta_{4}}
. Let
h\in{\mathcal{F}}_{\theta}(\widehat{U})
. Then (8.15) implies that
for any
m
with (8.16) we have

\int_{U}|L_{ab}^{mN}h|\;d\nu\leq 2\,\rho_{4}^{m/2}\,\|h\|_{\theta,b}\leq 2e^{-4\beta_{4}\frac{k}{2\beta_{4}}\,\log|b|}\,\|h\|_{\theta,b}=2\frac{1}{|b|^{2k}}\,\|h\|_{\theta,b}.

This proves (8.10).

(c)
We will now use a
standard procedure
(see
[
D1
]
) to derive an estimate of the form (8.11) from (8.10).
This partially repeats Sect. 7.4 in
[
St5
]
.

The main tool to use here is the Perron-Ruelle-Frobenius Theorem – see (8.8).

Fix an arbitrary constant
s\geq D_{3}
. Recall from Sect. 6.3 that

a_{0}NT_{0}\leq\beta_{4}/4
.
Let
m
and
k
satisfy (8.16), however this time we assume
k\geq 2s
.

Let
h\in{\mathcal{F}}_{\theta}(\widehat{U})
be such that
\|h\|_{\theta,b}\leq 1
. Then
\|h\|_{0}\leq 1
and

|h|_{\theta}\leq|b|
, so using Lemma 4.2 with
H=1
yields

|L_{ab}^{r}h|_{\theta}\leq A_{0}[|b|\theta^{r}+|b|]\leq 2A_{0}|b|

(8.17)

for any integer
r\geq 1
. It follows from Hölder’s inequality that

\displaystyle|L_{ab}^{2mN}h|

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle|L_{ab}^{mN}\left(|L_{ab}^{mN}h|\right)|\leq{\mathcal{M}}_{a}^{mN}|L_{ab}^{mN}h|=L_{f^{(0)}}^{mN}\left(e^{f^{(a)}_{mN}-f^{(0)}_{mN}}\,|L_{ab}^{mN}h|\right)

(8.18)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\left(L_{f^{(0)}}^{mN}\left(e^{f^{(a)}_{mN}-f^{(0)}_{mN}}\right)^{2}\right)^{1/2}\,\left(L_{f^{(0)}}^{mN}|L_{ab}^{mN}h|^{2}\right)^{1/2}.

For the first term in this product (
4.4
) and
a_{0}NT_{0}\leq\beta_{4}/4
imply

\displaystyle\left(L_{f^{(0)}}^{mN}\left(e^{f^{(a)}_{mN}-f^{(0)}_{mN}})\right)^{2}\right)^{1/2}\leq e^{a_{0}NT_{0}m}\leq e^{(k/4)\log|b|}=|b|^{k/4}.

For the second term in (8.18), using (
8.8
) replacing
h
by
|L_{ab}^{mN}h|
and
n
by
mN
, we get

L_{f^{(0)}}^{mN}|L_{ab}^{mN}h|^{2}\leq L_{f^{(0)}}^{mN}|L_{ab}^{mN}h|\leq\|h_{0}\|_{0}\,\int_{U}|L_{ab}^{mN}h|\,d\nu+C_{11}\,\rho_{0}^{mN}\,\|L_{ab}^{mN}h\|_{\theta}.

By Lemma 4.2,
\|L_{ab}^{mN}h\|_{\theta}\leq A_{0}[|b|\theta^{mN}+|b|]\leq 2A_{0}|b|
, so the above and (8.10) imply

L_{f^{(0)}}^{mN}|L_{ab}^{mN}h|^{2}\leq\frac{2}{|b|^{2k}}\|h_{0}\|_{0}+2A_{0}C_{11}|b|\,\rho_{0}^{mN}.

Assuming e.g.
\rho_{0}\leq\rho_{4}=e^{-4\beta_{4}}

we get

|b|\,\rho_{0}^{mN}\leq|b|\,e^{-4mN\beta_{4}}=|b|\,\frac{1}{|b|^{4Nk}}<\frac{1}{|b|^{2k}}.

Thus,

L_{f^{(0)}}^{mN}|L_{ab}^{mN}h|^{2}\leq\|h_{0}\|_{0}\,\frac{2}{|b|^{2k}}+2A_{0}C_{11}|b|\,\rho_{4}^{mN}\leq\frac{C^{\prime}_{12}}{|b|^{2k}}

for some constant
C^{\prime}_{12}>0
.
Therefeore

\left(L_{f^{(0)}}^{mN}|L_{ab}^{mN}h|^{2}\right)^{1/2}\leq\left(\frac{C^{\prime}_{12}}{|b|^{2k}}\right)^{1/2}\leq\frac{C_{12}}{|b|^{k}}

for some constant
C_{12}>0
.
Combining the estimates of the two terms in (8.18), it follows that

|L_{ab}^{2mN}h|\leq|b|^{k/4}\,\frac{C_{12}}{|b|^{k}}\leq\frac{C_{12}}{|b|^{k/2}}\leq\frac{C_{12}}{|b|^{s}},

using
k\geq 2s
. This was proved assuming
\|h\|_{\theta,b}\leq 1
. For general

h\in{\mathcal{F}}_{\theta}(\widehat{U})
we now get (8.11).

Proof of Theorem
1.2. This is derived from Theorem 8.2(c) by a standard argument as in
[
D1
]
.
We sketch the argument here for completeness. It is very similar to the one in Sect. 7 in
[
St5
]
.

Recall that
\theta=e^{-\beta_{0}}
for some
\beta_{0}>0
.

Let again
s\geq D_{3}
and let
C_{12}
be as in Lemma 8.2(c).
We now assume

m=\frac{k}{\beta_{4}\beta_{0}}\,\log|b|

for some
k\geq 2s
.
In particular we have
m\geq\frac{k}{\beta_{4}}\,\log|b|
and therefore (8.11) holds for all
h\in{\mathcal{F}}_{\theta}(\widehat{U})
.

Consider an arbitrary
h\in{\mathcal{F}}_{\theta}(\widehat{U})
with

\|h\|_{\theta,b}\leq 1
. Then
\|h\|_{0}\leq 1
and
|h|_{\theta}\leq|b|
.

Since
|h|_{\theta}\leq|b|
, as before, using Lemma 4.2 with
H=1
yields (8.17)
for any integer
r\geq 0
and any
h\in{\mathcal{F}}_{\theta}(\widehat{U})
.
Using again Lemma 4.2 and (8.11), we obtain

\displaystyle|L_{ab}^{4mN}h|_{\theta}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle|L_{ab}^{2mN}(L_{ab}^{2mN}h)|_{\theta}\leq A_{0}\left[2A_{0}|b|\,\theta^{2mN}+|b|\,\|L_{ab}^{2mN}h\|_{0}\right]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle A_{0}\left[2A_{0}|b|\;e^{-2mN\beta_{0}}+|b|\;\frac{C_{12}}{|b|^{k/2}}\right]\leq A_{0}\,\left[2A_{0}|b|\;e^{-2\frac{kN}{\beta_{4}}\log|b|}+|b|\;\frac{C_{12}}{|b|^{k/2}}\right]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle A_{0}\left[2A_{0}|b|\;\frac{1}{|b|^{2kN/\beta_{4}}}+|b|\;\frac{C_{12}}{|b|^{k/2}}\right]\leq A_{0}\left[2A_{0}|b|\;\frac{1}{|b|^{2k}}+|b|\;\frac{C_{12}}{|b|^{k/2}}\right]\leq\frac{C^{\prime}_{13}}{|b|^{k/2-1}},

for some constant
C^{\prime}_{13}>0
. This was done under the assumption
\|h\|_{\theta,b}\leq 1
.
Therefore

|L_{ab}^{4mN}h|_{\theta}\leq\frac{C^{\prime}_{13}}{|b|^{k/2-1}}\,\|h\|_{\theta,b}

for all
h\in{\mathcal{F}}_{\theta}(\widehat{U})
.
Using (8.11) again, we get

\displaystyle\|L_{ab}^{4mN}h\|_{0}\leq\frac{C_{11}}{|b|^{k/2}}\,\|h\|_{\theta,b},

therefore there exists a global constant
C_{13}>0
such that

\displaystyle\|L_{ab}^{4mN}h\|_{\theta,b}\leq\frac{C_{13}}{|b|^{k/2}}\,\|h\|_{\theta,b}

for all
h\in{\mathcal{F}}_{\theta}(\widehat{U})
.
Assuming
|b|\geq\tilde{b}_{0}
and
\tilde{b}_{0}>0
is sufficiently large, we get

\displaystyle\|L_{ab}^{4mN}h\|_{\theta,b}\leq\frac{C_{13}}{|b|^{k/2}}\,\|h\|_{\theta,b}\leq\frac{1}{|b|^{k/4}}\,\|h\|_{\theta,b}

for all
h\in{\mathcal{F}}_{\theta}(\widehat{U})
.

Let
n\geq 4mN
be an arbitrary integer. Writing
n=r\,(4mN)+\ell
for some

\ell=0,1,\ldots,4mN-1
, and using the above
r
times we get

\|L_{ab}^{r4mN}h\|_{\theta,b}\leq\frac{1}{|b|^{rk/4}}\,\|h\|_{\theta,b}.

As before, using Lemma 4.2 with
H=1
and
B=|L_{ab}^{r4mN}h|_{\theta}
, implies

\displaystyle|L_{ab}^{n}h|_{\theta}=|L_{ab}^{\ell}(L_{ab}^{r4mN}h)|_{\theta}\leq A_{0}\left[|L_{ab}^{r4mN}h|_{\theta}\,\theta^{\ell}+|b|\,\|L_{ab}^{r4mN}h\|_{0}\right],

so
\displaystyle\frac{1}{|b|}|L_{ab}^{n}h|_{\theta}\leq\frac{2A_{0}}{|b|^{rk/4}}\,\|h\|_{\theta,b}
.
This and
\displaystyle\|L_{ab}^{n}h\|_{0}\leq\|L_{ab}^{r4mN}h\|_{0}\leq\frac{1}{|b|^{rk/4}}\,\|h\|_{\theta,b}
imply

\displaystyle\|L_{ab}^{n}h\|_{\theta,b}\leq\frac{3A_{0}}{|b|^{rk/4}}\,\|h\|_{\theta,b}=3A_{0}\,e^{-(rk/4)\log|b|}\|h\|_{\theta,b}.

We have
r\geq(r+1)/2
for all
r\geq 1
, so the above and

(r+1)k\log|b|=\beta_{4}\beta_{0}\,(r+1)\frac{k}{\beta_{4}\beta_{0}}\,\log|b|=\frac{\beta_{4}\beta_{0}}{4N}\,(r+1)4mN>\frac{\beta_{4}\beta_{0}}{4N}\,n

yield

\displaystyle\|L_{ab}^{n}h\|_{\theta,b}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 3A_{0}\,e^{-(rk/4)\log|b|}\|h\|_{\theta,b}\leq 3A_{0}e^{-\frac{(r+1)k\log|b|}{8}}\|h\|_{\theta,b}

(8.19)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 3A_{0}e^{-\frac{\beta_{4}\beta_{0}4(r+1)mN}{32N}}\|h\|_{\theta,b}\leq 3A_{0}e^{-\frac{\beta_{4}\beta_{0}}{32N}\,n}\|h\|_{\theta,b}\leq 3A_{0}\,\rho_{6}^{n}\,\|h\|_{\theta,b},

where
\rho_{6}=\rho_{6}(N)=e^{-\frac{\beta_{4}\beta_{0}}{32N}}\in(0,1)
.

Thus, (8.19) holds for all
h\in{\mathcal{F}}_{\theta}(\widehat{U})
and all integers
n\geq 4mN=4(k/\beta_{4}\beta_{0})\,N\log|b|
.
Finally, recall the eigenfunction
h_{a}\in{\mathcal{F}}_{\theta}(\widehat{U})
for the operator
L_{f-(P_{f}+a)\tau}

from Sect. 4.2. It is known that
\|h_{a}\|_{\theta}\leq\mbox{\rm Const}\;
for bounded
a
, e.g. for
|a|\leq a_{0}
.
For
|a|\leq a_{0}
and
a_{0}>0
sufficiently small we have
\lambda_{a}\rho_{6}\leq\rho
for some
global constant
\rho\in(0,1)
. Now

\displaystyle L_{ab}^{n}(h/h_{a})=\frac{1}{\lambda_{a}^{n}h_{a}}\,L^{n}_{f-(P+a+{\bf i}b)\tau}h

and the above estimate show that there exist constants
0<\rho<1
,
a_{0}>0
,
and
C>0
such that if
a,b\in{\mathbb{R}}
satisfy
|a|\leq a_{0}
and
|b|\geq\tilde{b}_{0}
, then

\displaystyle\|L_{f-(P_{f}+a+{\bf i}b)\tau}^{n}h\|_{\theta,b}\leq C\;\rho^{n}\;\|h\|_{\theta,b}

for any integer
n\geq\frac{4kN}{\beta_{4}\beta_{0}}\,\log|b|
and any
h\in{\mathcal{F}}_{\theta}(\widehat{U})
. So, we can just set

T=T(k,N)=\frac{4kN}{\beta_{4}\beta_{0}}
, where we can take e.g.
k\geq 4D_{3}
.

This completes the proof of Theorem 1.2.

Proof of Theorem
1.1. This follows from the procedure described in
[
D1
]
(see Sect. 4 and Appendix 1 there).

Proof of Corollary 1.3.
This is essentially the same as the proof of Corollary 1.4 in Sect. 8 in
[
St5
]
.

9
Appendix I

Proof of Lemma
6.7. We will use Lemma 6.6 and a standard argument.

Let
h\in{\mathcal{K}}_{0}
and let
|s|\leq M_{1}
. We will prove that
L^{N}_{f-s\tau}h\in{\mathcal{K}}_{0}
.

Assume that
u,u^{\prime}\in\widetilde{{\mathcal{D}}}^{(m,t)}_{j}
for some
t=1,2
, some
m=1,\ldots,m_{0}
and some
j=1,\ldots,j_{m}
, and the integer
p\geq 0

and the points
v,v^{\prime}\in\widehat{U}
satisfy (
6.45
) for some
i=1,2
, and

w,w^{\prime}\in\widehat{U}
are such that
\sigma^{N}w=v
,
\sigma^{N}w^{\prime}=v^{\prime}
and
\ell(w,w^{\prime})\geq N
; then
w^{\prime}=w^{\prime}(w)
is
uniquely determined by
w
.

We have

D_{\theta}(\sigma^{j}(w),\sigma^{j}(w^{\prime}))=\theta^{N-j}\,D_{\theta}(v,v^{\prime})

for all
j=0,1,\ldots,N-1
, and

D_{\theta}(v,v^{\prime})=\theta^{N+p}\,D_{\theta}(u,u^{\prime})
. Assuming
|f|_{\theta}\leq T_{0}
, we get

\displaystyle|f_{N}(w)-f_{N}(w^{\prime})|\leq\sum_{j=0}^{N-1}|f(\sigma^{j}(w))-f(\sigma^{j}(w^{\prime}))|\leq\sum_{j=0}^{N-1}|f|_{\theta}\,\theta^{N-j}\,D_{\theta}(v,v^{\prime})\leq\frac{T_{0}D_{\theta}(v,v^{\prime})}{1-\theta}.

It follows from this and (
6.45
) that

\displaystyle|f_{N}(w)-f_{N}(w^{\prime})|\leq\frac{T_{0}}{1-\theta}\theta^{p+N}\,D_{\theta}(u,u^{\prime})\leq\frac{T_{0}}{1-\theta}\theta^{p+N}\,\mbox{\rm diam}_{\theta}(\widetilde{{\mathcal{D}}}^{(m,t)}_{j}).

This and Lemma 6.6 imply

|(f-s\tau)_{N}(w)-(f-s\tau)_{N}(w^{\prime})|\leq\left(\frac{T_{0}C_{7}C_{4}}{1-\theta}+\frac{M_{1}C_{4}}{d_{0}^{2}}\right)\,\theta^{p+N}\,\mbox{\rm diam}_{\theta}(\widetilde{{\mathcal{D}}}^{(m,t)}_{j})=C_{8}\,\theta^{p+N}\,\mbox{\rm diam}_{\theta}(\widetilde{{\mathcal{D}}}^{(m,t)}_{j})

for all
s\in{\mathbb{R}}
with
|s|\leq M_{1}
, where
C_{8}>0
is as in (6.30).

Since
h\in{\mathcal{K}}_{0}
, using the assumptions about
w,w^{\prime}
we get

|h(w)-h(w^{\prime})|\leq E_{1}\,\theta^{p+2N}\,h(w^{\prime})\,\mbox{\rm diam}_{\theta}(\widetilde{{\mathcal{D}}}^{(m,t)}_{j}).

Thus, given
s
with
|s|\leq M_{1}
we have:

\displaystyle|(L^{N}_{f-s\tau}h)(v)-(L^{N}_{f-s\tau}h)(v^{\prime})|=\left|\sum_{\sigma^{N}w=v}e^{(f-s\tau)_{N}(w)}\,h(w)-\sum_{\sigma^{N}w=v}e^{(f-s\tau)_{N}(w^{\prime}(w))}\,h(w^{\prime}(w))\right|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\left|\sum_{\sigma^{N}w=v}e^{(f-s\tau)_{N}(w)}\,[h(w)-h(w^{\prime})]\right|+\sum_{\sigma^{N}w=v}\left|e^{(f-s\tau)_{N}(w)}-e^{(f-s\tau)_{N}(w^{\prime})}\right|\,h(w^{\prime})=(I)+(II).

Estimating separately each of the sums
(I)
and
(II)
, we get

\displaystyle(I)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sum_{\sigma^{N}w=v}e^{(f-s\tau)_{N}(w)-(f-s\tau)_{N}(w^{\prime})}e^{(f-s\tau)_{N}(w^{\prime})}\,E_{1}\theta^{p+2N}\,h(w^{\prime})\,\mbox{\rm diam}_{\theta}(\widetilde{{\mathcal{D}}}^{(m,t)}_{j})

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle E_{1}\,e^{C_{8}}\,\theta^{p+2N}\mbox{\rm diam}_{\theta}(\widetilde{{\mathcal{D}}}^{(m,t)}_{j})\,(L^{N}_{f-s\tau}h)(v^{\prime}),

and

\displaystyle(II)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sum_{\sigma^{N}w=v}\left|e^{(f-s\tau)_{N}(w)-(f-s\tau)_{N}(w^{\prime})}-1\right|\,e^{(f-s\tau)_{N}(w^{\prime})}h(w^{\prime})

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle e^{C_{8}}\,C_{8}\,\theta^{p+N}\,\mbox{\rm diam}_{\theta}(\widetilde{{\mathcal{D}}}^{(m,t)}_{j})\,(L^{N}_{f-s\tau}h)(v^{\prime}).

Therefore

\displaystyle(I)+(II)\leq E_{1}\,\theta^{p+N}\,\mbox{\rm diam}_{\theta}(\widetilde{{\mathcal{D}}}^{(m,t)}_{j})\,(L^{N}_{f-s\tau}h)(v^{\prime}),

using
e^{C_{8}}\,C_{8}\leq E_{1}/2
and
\theta^{N}\,e^{C_{8}}<1/2
by
N\geq N_{0}
and the choices of
N_{0}
and
E
in Sect. 6.3.

Hence
L^{N}_{f-s\tau}h\in{\mathcal{K}}_{0}
.

A simple induction implies now that
L^{mN}_{f-s\tau}h\in{\mathcal{K}}_{0}
for all
h\in{\mathcal{K}}_{0}
and all integers
m\geq 1
.

Proof of Lemma
6.11.
Let
J\in{\mathcal{J}}(b)
and let
H\in{\mathcal{K}}_{E|b|}
. Set
{\mathcal{N}}={\mathcal{N}}_{J}
. We will show that
{\mathcal{N}}H\in{\mathcal{K}}_{E|b|}
.
Since
0<H\leq 1
, it follows from the definition of
{\mathcal{N}}
that
0<{\mathcal{N}}H\leq 1
.

Let
u,u^{\prime}\in\widehat{U}
.
Given
v\in\widehat{U}
with
\sigma^{N}(v)=u
, let
C[{\imath}]=C[i_{0},\ldots,i_{N}]

be the cylinder of length
N
containing
v
.
Set
\widehat{C}[{\imath}]=C[{\imath}]\cap\widehat{U}
. Then
\sigma^{N}(\widehat{C}[{\imath}])=\widehat{U}_{i_{0}}
. Moreover,

\sigma^{N}:\widehat{C}[{\imath}]\longrightarrow\widehat{U}_{i_{0}}
is a homeomorphism, so
there exists a unique
v^{\prime}=v^{\prime}(v)\in\widehat{C}[{\imath}]
such that
\sigma^{N}(v^{\prime})=u^{\prime}
. Then

D_{\theta_{1}}(\sigma^{j}(v),\sigma^{j}(v^{\prime}(v)))=\theta^{N-j}\,D_{\theta_{1}}(u,u^{\prime})

for all
j=0,1,\ldots,N-1
.
Also
D_{\theta_{1}}(v,v^{\prime}(v))=\theta_{1}^{N}D_{\theta_{1}}(u,u^{\prime})
. Hence

\displaystyle|f^{(a)}_{N}(v)-f^{(a)}_{N}(v^{\prime})|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sum_{j=0}^{N-1}|f^{(a)}(\sigma^{j}(v))-f^{(a)}(\sigma^{j}(v^{\prime}))|\leq\sum_{j=0}^{N-1}|f^{(a)}|_{\theta_{1}}\,\theta_{1}^{N-j}\,D_{\theta_{1}}(u,u^{\prime})

(9.1)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sum_{j=0}^{N-1}T_{0}\,\theta_{1}^{N-j}\,D_{\theta_{1}}(u,u^{\prime})\leq\frac{T_{0}}{1-\theta_{1}}\,D_{\theta_{1}}(u,u^{\prime}).

The definition of
\omega
implies that either
\omega(v)=\omega(v^{\prime})
or at least one of these numbers is
<1
.
Using Lemma 6.9 we get

|\omega(v)-\omega(v^{\prime})|\leq\frac{\theta_{1}^{-\ell_{0}}}{\epsilon_{4}}\,|b|\,D_{\theta_{1}}(u,u^{\prime}).

Since
\sigma^{N}(v)=u
,
\sigma^{N}(v^{\prime})=u^{\prime}
and
\ell(v,v^{\prime})\geq N
, the assumption
H\in{\mathcal{K}}_{E|b|}
implies

|H(v)-H(v^{\prime})|\leq E\,|b|\,H(v^{\prime})\,D_{\theta_{1}}(v,v^{\prime})\leq E\,|b|\,H(v^{\prime})\theta_{1}^{N}D_{\theta_{1}}(u,u^{\prime}).

It follows from (9.1) that

\left|e^{f^{(a)}_{N}(v)-f^{(a)}_{N}(v^{\prime})}-1\right|\leq e^{T_{0}/(1-\theta_{1})}\frac{T_{0}}{1-\theta_{1}}\,D_{\theta_{1}}(u,u^{\prime}).

Also,
2\omega_{J}(v^{\prime})\geq 1\geq\omega_{J}(v)
.

Using the above and the definition of
{\mathcal{N}}={\mathcal{N}}_{J}
, setting
v^{\prime}=v^{\prime}(v)
and
\omega=\omega_{J}
for brevity, we get

\displaystyle\frac{|({\mathcal{N}}H)(u)-({\mathcal{N}}H)(u^{\prime})|}{{\mathcal{N}}H(u^{\prime})}=\frac{\displaystyle\left|\sum_{\sigma^{N}v=u}e^{f^{(a)}_{N}(v)}\,\omega(v)H(v)-\sum_{\sigma^{N}v=u}e^{f^{(a)}_{N}(v^{\prime}(v))}\,\omega(v^{\prime}(v))H(v^{\prime}(v))\right|}{{\mathcal{N}}H(u^{\prime})}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{\displaystyle\sum_{\sigma^{N}v=u}e^{f^{(a)}_{N}(v)-f^{(a)}_{N}(v^{\prime})}e^{f^{(a)}_{N}(v^{\prime})}\,|\omega(v)-\omega(v^{\prime})|H(v^{\prime})}{{\mathcal{N}}H(u^{\prime})}+\frac{\displaystyle\sum_{\sigma^{N}v=u}e^{f^{(a)}_{N}(v)}\,\omega(v)|H(v)-H(v^{\prime})|}{{\mathcal{N}}H(u^{\prime})}

\displaystyle+\frac{\displaystyle\sum_{\sigma^{N}v=u}\left|e^{f^{(a)}_{N}(v)-f^{(a)}_{N}(v^{\prime})}-1\right|\,e^{f^{(a)}_{N}(v^{\prime})}\omega(v^{\prime})H(v^{\prime})}{{\mathcal{N}}H(u^{\prime})}.

Estimating separately each of the three sums above, we get

\displaystyle\frac{|({\mathcal{N}}H)(u)-({\mathcal{N}}H)(u^{\prime})|}{{\mathcal{N}}H(u^{\prime})}\leq e^{T_{0}/(1-\theta_{1})}\frac{\theta_{1}^{-\ell_{0}}}{\epsilon_{4}}\,|b|\,\frac{\displaystyle\sum_{\sigma^{N}v=u}e^{f^{(a)}_{N}(v^{\prime})}\,H(v^{\prime})\,D_{\theta_{1}}(u,u^{\prime})}{{\mathcal{N}}H(u^{\prime})}

\displaystyle+\frac{\displaystyle\sum_{\sigma^{N}v=u}e^{f^{(a)}_{N}(v)-f^{(a)}_{N}(v^{\prime})}\,e^{f^{(a)}_{N}(v^{\prime})}\,2\omega(v^{\prime})\,E\,|b|H(v^{\prime})\theta_{1}^{N}D_{\theta_{1}}(u,u^{\prime})}{{\mathcal{N}}H(u^{\prime})}+e^{T_{0}/(1-\theta_{1})}\frac{T_{0}}{1-\theta_{1}}\,D_{\theta_{1}}(u,u^{\prime})

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\left(e^{T_{0}/(1-\theta_{1})}\frac{\theta_{1}^{-\ell_{0}}}{\epsilon_{4}}\,|b|+2e^{T_{0}/(1-\theta_{1})}E|b|\,\theta_{1}^{N}+e^{T_{0}/(1-\theta_{1})}\frac{T_{0}}{(1-\theta_{1})}\,\right)\,D_{\theta_{1}}(u,u^{\prime})\leq E\,|b|\,D_{\theta_{1}}(u,u^{\prime}),

since by (
6.32
), the choice of
N_{0}
in (
6.33
) and
N\geq N_{0}
, we have

e^{T_{0}/(1-\theta_{1})}\theta_{1}^{-\ell_{0}}/\epsilon_{4}\leq\frac{E}{3}\hskip 10.00002pt,\hskip 10.00002pt2e^{T_{0}/(1-\theta_{1})}\,\theta_{1}^{N}\leq\frac{1}{3}\hskip 10.00002pt,\hskip 10.00002pte^{T_{0}/(1-\theta_{1})}\frac{T_{0}}{(1-\theta_{1})}\leq\frac{E}{3}.

Hence
{\mathcal{N}}H\in{\mathcal{K}}_{E|b|}
.

Proof of Lemma
7.2.
(a) Let
u,u^{\prime}\in\widehat{{\mathcal{D}}}^{(m,t)}_{j}
for some
m\leq m_{0}
,
t=1,2
and
j=1,\ldots,j_{m}
. For any
i=1,2

for
v=v^{(m)}_{i,j}(u)\in X^{(m,t)}_{i,j}
and
v^{\prime}=v^{(m)}_{i,j}(u^{\prime})\in X^{(m,t)}_{i,j}
,
we have
\ell(v,v^{\prime})\geq N
and
u=\sigma^{N}(v),u^{\prime}=\sigma^{N}(v^{\prime})\in\widehat{{\mathcal{D}}}^{(m,t)}_{j}
.
This,
H\in{\mathcal{K}}_{E|b|}
,
u,u^{\prime}\in{\mathcal{C}}^{\prime}_{m}
and (
6.7
) imply

|\ln H(v)-\ln H(v^{\prime})|\leq\frac{|H(v)-H(v^{\prime})|}{\min\{H(v),H(v^{\prime})\}}\leq E\,|b|\,D_{\theta_{1}}(v,v^{\prime})=E\,|b|\,\theta_{1}^{N}D_{\theta_{1}}(u,u^{\prime})\leq E\,C_{7}\,\theta_{1}^{N}<\ln 2,

using (
6.33
) as well.
Hence
|\ln H(v)-\ln H(v^{\prime})|\leq\ln 2
, so
\frac{1}{2}\leq\frac{H(v)}{H(v^{\prime})}\leq 2
.

(b) Consider again
\widehat{{\mathcal{D}}}^{(m,t)}_{j}
for some
m\leq m_{0}
,
t=1,2
and let
j=1,\ldots,j_{m}
.
Assume e.g.
H(v^{(m)}_{1,j}(u^{\prime}))\geq H(v^{(m)}_{2,j}(u^{\prime}))
for some
u^{\prime}\in\widehat{{\mathcal{D}}}^{(m,t)}_{j}
. Then,
for every
u\in\widehat{{\mathcal{D}}}^{(m,t)}_{j}
, using part (a) twice, we get

H(v^{(m)}_{1,j}(u))\geq H(v^{(m)}_{1,j}(u^{\prime}))/2\geq H(v^{(m)}_{2,j}(u^{\prime}))/2\geq H(v^{(m)}_{2,j}(u))/4.

Similarly, if
H(v^{(m)}_{2,j}(u^{\prime}))\geq H(v^{(m)}_{1,j}(u^{\prime}))
for some
u^{\prime}\in\widehat{{\mathcal{D}}}^{(m,t)}_{j}
, then
H(v^{(m)}_{2,j}(u))\geq H(v^{(m)}_{1,j}(u))/4

for all
u\in\widehat{{\mathcal{D}}}^{(m,t)}_{j}
.

(c) Assume that
u,u^{\prime}\in\widehat{{\mathcal{D}}}^{(m,t)}_{j}
for some
m\leq m_{0}
, some
t=1,2
, and
j=1,\ldots,j_{m}
.
Let
i\in\{1,2\}
.
Consider the case when for some
v\in X^{(m,t)}_{i,j}
we have
|h(v)|\geq\frac{3}{4}H(v)
.
Fix
v
with this property and consider an arbitrary
v^{\prime}\in X^{(m,t)}_{i,j}
. It follows from (7.1) and (7.2) that

|h(v^{\prime})-h(v)|\leq E|b|\,\theta^{N}H(v)\,\mbox{\rm diam}_{\theta}(\widetilde{{\mathcal{D}}}^{(m,t)}_{j})\leq H(v)\,\frac{\epsilon_{3}}{32}.

Using
2H(v)\geq H(v^{\prime})
which follows from (a), one obtains

\displaystyle|h(v^{\prime})|

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle|h(v)|-(\epsilon_{3}/32)H(v)\geq(3/4-\epsilon_{3}/32)H(v)\geq\frac{1}{4}\,H(v^{\prime}).

Thus, in this case the second alternative in (c) holds for all
v^{\prime}\in X^{(m,t)}_{i,j}
.

In the same way one shows that if
|h(v)|\leq\frac{1}{4}H(v)
for some

v\in X^{(m,t)}_{i,j}
, then the first alternative in (c) holds.
